Every user deactivation, store suspension, and product takedown or rejection that was in force just before the restore is in force again **before the application serves**, not repeated by an admin afterwards while the application is already open. How is for T-071, with these conditions. When the database that is being replaced can still be read (a rollback), the ids under each restriction are listed from it before it is overwritten; the list holds ids and statuses only, is reachable by the people of SR-06 item 3 only, and is deleted when the restore is finished. When it cannot be read (a loss), the admin-action lines of the server log are the only source (SR-04), and they may not reach back far enough, because logs have no minimum retention (SR-06 item 2); the record of the restore then says which period could not be reconstructed, and the shop admin is told before the application serves. Re-applying a restriction is written to the audit log like the admin action it repeats (TM-25).

Both hold for every database the application serves from, staging and a rehearsal with real data included. What a restore loses and cannot give back stays as DEPLOYMENT.md lists it (orders, and the audit log entries written since the backup).

### Scheduled work: security minimums (2026-10-11)

Review of `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` "Scheduled work" and its open questions DEP-Q5, DEP-Q8, and DEP-Q10. Nothing here is built; these are the least any answer must keep. Where a number is the owner's, it is said so.

| Point | Security minimum | Whose number |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Expired-session purge | Runs with no visitor present, once when the application starts, and at most 12 hours apart (SR-07 item 5). A missed or failed run is visible in the log. The hourly plan is approved | The one-day bound is the owner's (D-026, OQ-49); the 12 hours are a technical margin |
| Payment timeout sweep (DEP-Q8) | Security sets no number for the delay on top of the 15 minutes. What it requires: the delay is a stated, fixed bound; the check query's threshold is the timeout plus that bound; the sweep runs with no visitor present and a missed run is visible, because a sweep that has stopped keeps stock away from every other customer without any error (TM-10); it fails an order only through the guarded payment-failure transaction, so an order paid in the meantime is never failed (TM-12, TM-13); and its run lines hold counts, not order contents (SR-04). Every minute, with a check at 20 minutes, is approved | The delay is the owner's (DEP-Q8) |
| Investigation hold review (DEP-Q5) | A dated review of every open hold, added to the record, at intervals of 90 days at most (SR-06 item 8) | The interval is the owner's, within that ceiling |
| What runs the jobs (DEP-Q10) | Never a `GET`, and never an endpoint reachable from the internet unless it has its own secret, the checks of SR-05 item 5, a rate limit, and a security review first. The security agent agrees with devops that candidate C is not to be chosen for the MVP | T-015, auth with devops |

One thing the delay does not settle: an unpaid order holds its stock for the whole timeout, and a customer may check out 10 times a minute, so an account can keep stock away from others for as long as it keeps ordering. With the mock provider an order is meant to be paid or failed in the same request, so the hold should be short; that is a statement of the design, not of anything built. With a real provider it is not, and a bound on the unpaid orders one account may hold at a time is to be decided with that integration (residual risks).

### Position on the blind resend of a refused checkout (`docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` item 8.2-1; 2026-10-11)

**A position, not a ruling.** D-031 point 6 leaves the ruling to backend-api and security together, before T-032 starts. Nothing here is in effect.

- **Keeping the rule in the client only is not acceptable as the final state.** The rule is "the customer sees the lowered quantity and the new total before an order is placed for them" (D-026, OQ-30; BR-22). Today the only thing that keeps it is the page's script. A second tab with an older page, a retry made by a library, a service worker, or an intermediary that resends a request whose answer was lost each place the order unseen, and the server cannot tell that from the customer's confirmation. CLAUDE.md rule 2 makes the same point about hidden buttons: what only the page enforces is not a control. The harm is bounded, and that is why this is a position on a design and not a registered finding: the caller must hold the customer's own session, no other person gains anything, the quantity can only be lower than the one the customer chose, and the charge is computed by the server.
- **Preferred guard: a revision of the cart.** The cart carries a counter that goes up with every stored change of its lines, the lowering done by a refused checkout included. `GET /cart` and every cart and checkout answer return it; `POST /checkout` must send the value the customer was shown; inside the checkout transaction, under the cart lock that D-023 already takes, a value that is not the current one refuses the checkout and changes nothing. A blind resend carries the old value and is refused, because the refusal itself raised the counter.
- **Why this one of the three.** A stored refusal answers a repeat of the same key with the same refusal, which stops the exact resend but not a client that sends a fresh key on each try, and it adds rows that an account can create ten times a minute and that then need their own clean-up. A confirmation value does what the revision does, but only on the path after a refusal, and needs either storage or a signing key. The revision is one integer on a row that is already locked, needs no clean-up and no secret, and also covers a case the other two do not: a checkout sent from one tab after the cart was changed in another.
- **What no guard can do.** A client that reads the new revision and resends without showing anything still places the order. The server can make sure the client has received the new state, not that a person looked at it.
- **Conditions if it is adopted.** The revision is not an amount and not a secret; it is compared for equality only and never used for anything else. The idempotency lookup comes first, so a repeat of a checkout that succeeded still returns its order whatever revision it carries (TM-11). The refusal for a stale revision says only that the cart changed and returns the cart as `GET /cart` would.
- **Not covered by any of the three, stated so that nobody assumes it:** a price that the seller changed between the page the customer saw and the checkout. BR-18 makes the new price the one checkout uses. Whether the customer must also confirm a changed total is a product question for the owner, not part of 8.2-1.

### CSRF helper (`assertSameOrigin`, `assertContentType`, `readJsonBody`): review and decisions (2026-10-11)

First security review of the helpers added in commit 28c92d1, against TM-16.

| Point | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| The `Origin` header is compared as an exact string with the origin of `APP_URL`; the header is never parsed | Accepted, and the right design: nothing an attacker writes is interpreted. Browsers always send the origin in one canonical form (lower-case scheme and host, punycode for an internationalised host, no default port, no path), which is the form `new URL(APP_URL).origin` produces. Tested: 26 near misses are refused on every mutating method, among them an explicit `:443`, upper case, a trailing dot, a trailing slash, user-info, a look-alike suffix or prefix host, the other scheme, another port, `null`, an empty value, and repeated or combined values |
| A client that is not a browser and writes the origin differently (`https://shop.example:443`, upper case) is refused | Accepted. The API has no non-browser clients; fail-closed |
| A host name with a trailing dot is a different origin and is refused | Accepted. The deployment serves one canonical host and redirects any other spelling to it |
| `OPTIONS` and unknown methods are checked like mutations, so a cross-origin preflight gets 403 | Accepted and intended. The API is same-origin only and sends no CORS headers; a same-origin page never sends a preflight. Allowing any other origin would be a new decision recorded here, with an explicit allow-list and never a wildcard with credentials |
| `GET` and `HEAD` are exempt | Accepted, on the standing condition of TM-16 that they never change state. A handler that changes state on `GET` is a finding |
| An `APP_URL` that is not an absolute http or https URL throws `TypeError` on every call, including `GET` | Accepted: a broken configuration never passes a request. `src/server/env.ts` must reject the same values at startup (devops) |
| The content type must be exactly `application/json` (any letter case, parameters allowed); a missing, combined, or look-alike value is 415, decided before the body is read (tested: `bodyUsed` stays false) | Accepted. This is what keeps a plain HTML form, which can only send three content types, from reaching a JSON endpoint even where an old browser omits `Origin` |
| A body that is not JSON is 400 with a fixed message, no cause, and no log line | Accepted. The parser's message quotes the body, so dropping it is correct (tested: nothing of the body reaches the response or the log) |
| `__proto__` and `constructor` keys in a body | No pollution: `JSON.parse` creates them as ordinary own properties (tested). Schemas must stay strict objects so that such keys are rejected rather than carried into a service (TM-04, TM-27) |
| `trustsIncomingRequestId(config)` | Accepted. It is true exactly when SR-03 says so and false for every invalid configuration (tested with 14), so a broken configuration cannot make client-chosen request ids trusted |
| No body size limit (state at the first review) | Not accepted: SR-05, SEC-019. Fixed in commit e2a4a7a and re-checked 2026-10-11; what the re-check found is SEC-020, fixed and re-checked the same day; what that re-check found is SEC-021 (Low), fixed and re-checked the same day with nothing further found |

What these helpers do not cover, by design: they are opt-in per handler, they do not apply to the auth library's own routes (T-015 must show the library's equivalent checks), and the upload endpoint needs the same origin check with `multipart/form-data` (upload task).

## Session and cookie settings

Written by the auth agent on 2026-10-13 (T-015) from the configuration as built: `src/server/auth/auth.ts` (the Better Auth 1.7.7 options) and `src/server/auth/config.ts` (the constants). Each value below is asserted by a test named in brackets; "integration" means `tests/integration/auth/`, run against the test database. The security agent has not reviewed this section yet, and the status columns of TM-02, TM-03, TM-04, TM-19, TM-26, and TM-34 above are left for that review.

**Security review, 2026-10-13.** This section was checked against the code of commits 4bd5d96 to 7f3a767 and found accurate, with the exceptions registered as SEC-032 to SEC-036 (the per-email limit locks accounts out; bidirectional controls in names; `__Secure-` instead of `__Host-`; timing; `mysql2` in the production tree). The use of `security()` for the purge line is confirmed. The TM statuses above are updated. Details, commands, and evidence: [reviews/T-015-T-016-security.md](reviews/T-015-T-016-security.md).

**Library and what of it is used.** Better Auth 1.7.7 (`better-auth/minimal` with `better-auth/adapters/prisma`, transactions on). It hashes passwords (its default, scrypt; the stored form is `<salt hex>:<key hex>`), generates session tokens (32 random characters from `[A-Za-z0-9]`), and signs and verifies the session cookie (HMAC-SHA-256 with `AUTH_SECRET`). **Its HTTP handler is not mounted**: there is no `src/app/api/auth/**` route, so none of its own endpoints (update-user, change-password, reset-password, list-sessions, revoke-sessions, delete-user, get-session) is reachable [integration: "no route of the library is reachable"]. The application's routes under `src/app/api/v1/auth/` call it through `auth.api.*` after the checks of SR-05 item 5. Its own rate limiter is off (`rateLimit.enabled: false`), its IP detection is off (`advanced.ipAddress.disableIpTracking: true`), telemetry is off, and its log lines reach the application logger at `error` only (its `info` line for a duplicate sign-up contains the email).

**Cookie.**

| Setting | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Name | `better-auth.session_token`; `__Host-better-auth.session_token` in production (SEC-034, changed 2026-10-13 from `__Secure-`: in better-auth 1.7.7 `advanced.useSecureCookies` is `false`, `advanced.cookiePrefix` is `__Host-better-auth`, and `advanced.defaultCookieAttributes` is `{ secure: true, path: "/" }`, so every cookie the library writes or expires carries the `__Host-` name, `Secure`, `Path=/`, and no `Domain`) |
| Value | session token, `.`, HMAC signature (a token without a valid signature is refused [integration: forged cookies]) |
| `HttpOnly` | always |
| `Secure` | when `NODE_ENV=production` (`advanced.defaultCookieAttributes.secure`, set explicitly from the validated `NODE_ENV`, not inferred by the library); not in development and tests, which run on `http://localhost` |
| `SameSite` | `Lax` |
| `Path` | `/` |
| `Domain` | not set (host-only cookie) |
| `Max-Age` | 604,800 seconds (7 days), the session lifetime |
| Other cookies | none: the cookie cache (`session_data`) is off and `rememberMe` is always true, so no `dont_remember` cookie |

Asserted in a production-mode response [integration: "cookie attributes in a production-mode response", which builds the auth instance with secure cookies and an `https` origin and signs in and out through the sign-in and sign-out handlers]. **Not run:** a real `next start` with `NODE_ENV=production`, because production mode refuses the local databases by design (CR-49); the derivation `secureCookies = (NODE_ENV === "production")` is in `getAuth()` and is read, not tested against a running server.

**Session lifetime and renewal.** 7 days from sign-in, fixed (`session.expiresIn` 604,800 s). Not renewed while used (`disableSessionRefresh: true`) and refresh deferred to `POST` (`deferSessionRefresh: true`, and the library's `get-session` endpoint is not mounted), so **reading a session writes nothing**: no expiry update and no deletion of an expired row on read. That settles D-031 point 6 (X-07) for this project: a `GET` never writes, the library included [integration: "refuses an expired session, writes nothing while reading it"]. The cookie cache is off: the user, role, active flag, store id, and store status are read from the database on every request (TM-26) [integration: suspension and reinstatement take effect on the next request with the same session].

**Rotation.** Every sign-in creates a new session with a new token. When the sign-in request carries a valid session cookie, that session is deleted, so a browser ends with exactly one fresh session [integration: "rotates on login"]. A token chosen by a client is never accepted (tokens are generated by the library and signed).

**Response floor (SEC-035).** A failed sign-in and every sign-up answer take at least 250 ms plus a random 0 to 25 ms, counted from the start of the password step (`AUTH_RESPONSE_FLOOR`, `src/server/auth/config.ts`) [integration: "response floor (SEC-035, TM-03)"].

**What the session record stores (SR-07).** `token`, `userId`, `expiresAt`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, and, set in the library's `session.create.before` hook: `ipAddress` = the SR-01 result of `getClientAddress` (an IPv4 address or IPv6 /64 network), `NULL` when that is `unknown`, never a header the client wrote [integration: with no trusted proxy and a client `X-Forwarded-For`, `NULL`; with one trusted hop, the address one place from the right]; `userAgent` = the `User-Agent` header cut to 512 characters, never splitting a surrogate pair, `NULL` when absent [integration: 600 characters stored as 512].

**Ending a session.**

| Event | What happens | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Sign-out (`POST /api/v1/auth/sign-out`) | The session row is deleted by the application (a failure is a 500, not a success: the library's own sign-out swallows a failed delete), then the cookie is cleared (`Max-Age=0`, same attributes) | integration: the old cookie is refused afterwards and the row count is 0 |
| Deactivation | `deleteSessionsOfUser(userId, tx)` deletes every session of the user, in the transaction that sets `isActive = false` (to be called by the admin service, T-050/T-051). As a second line, `getActor` refuses an inactive user even if a session row survived, and the session hook refuses to create a session for an inactive user | integration: "deactivation" cases; the hook case fails when its check is removed |
| Store suspension | Nothing (D-027): the session stays, `requireApprovedSeller` refuses with `403 SELLER_NOT_APPROVED` | integration: "a suspended seller keeps the login" |
| Expiry | An expired session is refused when presented and removed by the purge below | integration and unit |

**Removal of expired sessions (SR-07 item 5).** `purgeExpiredSessions()` deletes every `Session` row with `expiresAt` in the past and every expired `Verification` row. `startSessionPurgeSchedule()` runs it once when the server starts and then every hour (`SESSION_PURGE_INTERVAL_MS`; SR-07 requires at most 12 hours), one schedule per process (`globalThis`, `Symbol.for("marketplace.auth.sessionPurgeSchedule")`), timer unref'd, no overlapping runs. `src/instrumentation.ts` starts it after the environment is validated, and not when `NODE_ENV=test`. Every run writes one line: `security("info", "Expired sessions removed", { removedCount, removedVerifications })`, which `LOG_LEVEL` does not filter so that a missed run stays visible, or `error` "Expired session removal failed" with the error's class name and code only. The `security()` path is used here for a line SR-07 requires, not for an SR-04 event; the security agent should confirm that use. **Not run:** the schedule inside a real `next start` or `next dev` over an hour; the unit tests use fake timers and a stand-in purge, the integration test calls the purge directly.

**`Verification` table (SR-07 "Restore").** It is the library's table for one-time tokens (email verification, password reset). Nothing in this application writes to it today (no email delivery, so no verification or recovery flow exists); its expired rows are purged with the sessions. If a recovery flow is ever added, its rows are credentials with a lifetime and must be emptied at a restore together with the sessions.

**Passwords.** 8 to 128 characters at sign-up, checked by the request schema and again by the library (`minPasswordLength`, `maxPasswordLength`); sign-in accepts 1 to 128 so that its answer never depends on the policy. No composition rule and no breached-password check.

**Registration and enumeration (TM-03).** The library's sign-up never signs in (`autoSignIn: false`). The library then answers an email that is already registered with the same response as a new one, after hashing the submitted password so that the time is alike; nothing is created or changed for the existing account [integration]. Since CR-57 (D-036 item 3, 2026-10-13) the route signs a new user in through the library's sign-in with the password just chosen, and gives a duplicate email a decoy `Set-Cookie` line instead: same name and attributes, a value of the same form (32 random characters of `a-z A-Z 0-9`, a dot, 32 random bytes in base64, URL-encoded) that is signed by nothing and has no session row, so it reads as signed out (`src/server/auth/sign-up-cookie.ts`) [unit: the decoy line equals the line better-call's `serializeSignedCookie` writes for the same name and attributes, but for the value, in both cookie settings; integration, not yet run: a new and a duplicate sign-up answer with the same status, body, header names, and cookie line but for the value]. A duplicate is never signed in, even with the account's own password. The duplicate path checks the password once against a fixed hash so both paths do the same scrypt work (new: hash plus verify; duplicate: the library's hash plus that verify). The route answers `200 { "data": { "accepted": true } }` in both cases. A session the request came with is deleted in both cases (rotation). Sign-in answers an unknown email, a wrong password, and a deactivated account with the same `401 UNAUTHENTICATED` and message, and the library hashes the password for an unknown email too [integration: identical bodies]. **Residual:** the sign-up answer itself does not tell a new email from a registered one, but the next request does: with the cookie of a new account `GET /api/v1/auth/session` returns an actor, with the decoy it returns `null` (and before CR-57, a sign-in with the password just chosen showed the same). Without email verification this cannot be avoided while sign-up signs in; it costs one sign-up allowance (5 an hour per address) per probe, and it creates an account for every unregistered address probed. REQUIREMENTS REQ-C-01 ("creates a CUSTOMER and logs in"; "duplicate email gives a generic error") holds in this form: the generic error is shown by the client after the session check, without stating that the email exists.

**Secret.** `AUTH_SECRET`, required in every environment, at least 32 characters, and in production not containing `not_a_secret` (the marker of `.env.example` values). There is no default. Since CR-66 (commit a561503) the rule lives in `src/server/env.ts`: `parseEnv` checks it at start-up with the other variables (a refusal ends the process through the start-up hook), and `readAuthSecret()` (re-exported by `src/server/auth/config.ts`) applies the same rule when the auth instance is first created. A refusal names the variable and a rule, never the value [unit; re-checked 2026-10-13, [reviews/SEC-039-040-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-039-040-recheck.md)]. `.env.example` holds a placeholder that production refuses. In production a value with fewer than 8 distinct characters, a shorter unit repeated, or a published value (the library's default, the integration-test secret) is refused too (SEC-044, fixed by auth 2026-10-13, awaiting re-check). The library is given the secret as `secret` and as the only entry of `secrets`, so it never reads `BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS`; `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, `BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS`, `BETTER_AUTH_URL`, and `BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` are refused by name when set, at start-up and by `readAuthSecret` (SEC-045, fixed by auth 2026-10-13, awaiting re-check) [unit].

**Rate limits on the auth routes.** As the table of `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` section 6, through `src/server/rate-limit.ts` only: `sign-up` per client address and `sign-in-address` before the body is read, `sign-in-email` on the client address and the normalised email, then `sign-in-account` on the normalised email, after the body and before the password (SEC-032, 2026-10-13) [integration: the 6th attempt is 429 even with a body that is not JSON; the per-account limit holds across addresses, counts unknown emails alike, and its 429 has the per-address refusal's body and a `Retry-After` of at most 60 s; requests refused for origin or content type use no allowance]. No password-reset route exists, so none is limited.

**Security events.** `sign-in` (succeeded at `info`, failed at `warn`), `sign-up` (`info`), `origin-refused` and `rate-limited` (`warn`), all through `security()`, with `clientIp`, the user agent cut to 200 characters, the route, the outcome, and on success the user id; never the email, password, token, or body [integration: canaries]. Events that come before any allowance (`origin-refused`, `rate-limited`) are throttled to one line per kind and rule per minute, carrying the count suppressed since the last line [unit]; the others are bounded by the rate limit that counts them.

**Post-login redirect (TM-19).** `safeRedirectPath()` in `src/server/auth/redirect.ts` accepts only a path that starts with one `/`, has no backslash, blank, or control character, and resolves to the same origin; anything else becomes `/` [unit, 30 cases]. `src/proxy.ts` uses it for the `next` parameter. No sign-in page exists yet (frontend).

**Proxy (`src/proxy.ts`).** Redirects a request for `/seller`, `/admin`, `/account`, or anything below them that carries no session cookie to `/sign-in?next=...` (`SIGN_IN_PATH`, equal to the shell's `ROUTES.signIn`; D-036 item 4, a unit test fails if the two differ). It tests only the presence of a cookie with the session cookie's name; it is not the security boundary (TM-07) [unit: matcher cases and redirect].

**Admin bootstrap (T-016, REQ-A-11, BR-26).** `pnpm admin:bootstrap` (`scripts/bootstrap-admin.ts`, logic in `src/modules/auth/bootstrap-admin.ts`) is the only way an `ADMIN` comes to exist. Input from the environment only (`ADMIN_BOOTSTRAP_EMAIL`, `ADMIN_BOOTSTRAP_PASSWORD`, optional `ADMIN_BOOTSTRAP_NAME`), never from argv, so the password stays out of shell history and the process list; no default. The password policy is the sign-up schema's (8 to 128 characters) [unit, integration]. The password is hashed with the library's own `hashPassword` (the same scrypt the instance uses) and the user id has the library's format, so the admin signs in through `POST /api/v1/auth/sign-in` [integration]. It only inserts (one `User`, one credential `Account`, one transaction); an email already an admin's is "no change" (exit 0), a customer's or seller's is refused (exit 3) with nothing changed, compared without regard to letter case [integration: whole-database snapshot before and after]. The output names the account by user id; never the password or the email, and a failure by error class and Prisma code only [unit]. No audit row is written: DATA_MODEL section 4.1 lists the rows a run inserts, and `AuditLog.actorId` needs an acting user.

**Password recovery.** Not implemented, because no email delivery exists (DECISIONS.md, "Pending decisions": email provider). No reset route is mounted and the library's reset endpoints are unreachable.

## Rate-limited endpoints

See the table in [API_CONTRACTS.md](API_CONTRACTS.md). "Per IP" there means per client address as derived by SR-01 (IPv4 address or IPv6 /64, or the shared `unknown` subject). The limiter is in-process and fails closed at its key cap (SR-02); see residual risks.

## Upload policy

JPEG, PNG, WebP. Maximum 5 MB per file, 8 images per product. Content verified by magic bytes. Stored under generated keys. Product images are public by design; no private documents are uploaded in the MVP. The upload endpoint is the only one that accepts `multipart/form-data`, and it is covered by the same `Origin` check as every other mutation (TM-16).

## Privacy and data retention

- Personal data collected: email, name, phone, shipping addresses, order history, the client IP address and browser details (`User-Agent`) in security log lines (SR-04), and the IP address and browser details of the sign-in in the session record (SR-07). Nothing else.
- No payment card data is collected or stored. The mock provider takes no card input.
- What may and may not be written to logs is defined in SR-04.
- Thailand's PDPA applies to a real launch: a privacy notice, a lawful basis, data-subject request handling, and a retention schedule are required and are **not** part of the MVP. Recorded in KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md.
- Order and audit records are retained indefinitely in the MVP; deactivated accounts keep their order history. Neither holds an IP address or browser details (SR-06 item 5, SR-07 item 6).
- Server logs are kept at most 90 days and then deleted, in every copy including log backups, and only system administrators may read them: the named people who operate the servers, never the application's `ADMIN` role (owner decisions of 2026-10-11, D-025 OQ-21, D-026 OQ-47 and OQ-48; rule SR-06). 90 days is a maximum, not a promise that logs exist that long. Lines that are evidence in an open security investigation may be kept until it is closed, only the relevant lines and with a written record of what is kept and why (SR-06 item 8). Not implemented: it is a deployment task (T-071).
- The IP address and browser details of a sign-in may be stored with the session and are deleted when the session ends, and within one day of its expiry (owner decisions of 2026-10-11, D-025 OQ-22, D-026 OQ-49; rule SR-07). Not implemented: it is part of T-015, and the mechanism that removes expired sessions is an open point there.
- Database backups are kept 30 days (owner decision of 2026-10-11, D-026 OQ-50; rule SR-07). Not implemented: no backup exists.
- The rules together: after a session is gone, the address of its sign-in is still in the server log until that line is at most 90 days old, and in any database backup taken meanwhile until that backup is 30 days old.

## Findings register

Reproduction for every finding against `src/server`: `pnpm exec vitest run --project security` (files `tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts` and, for the SEC-019 re-check and SEC-020, `tests/security/sec019-body-limit-recheck.test.ts`, for the SEC-020 re-check and SEC-021, `tests/security/sec020-bounded-buffer-recheck.test.ts`, for SEC-022 to SEC-024, SEC-027, and SEC-028, `tests/security/sec022-024-json-body-recheck.test.ts`, and for SEC-009, SEC-025, and SEC-026, `tests/security/t011-env-health-recheck.test.ts`, and for the re-check of SEC-025 to SEC-028 and for SEC-029 to SEC-031, `tests/security/round7-recheck.test.ts`, and for the re-check of SEC-030 and SEC-031, `tests/security/sec030-031-recheck.test.ts`; the project is also part of `pnpm test`). Each open finding is an `it.fails` test whose body asserts the secure behaviour; it is reported as "expected fail" while the flaw exists and turns red when the code is fixed. The owner may then change `it.fails` to `it` without touching the body, and the security agent re-checks independently before the finding is marked fixed.

Format:


### SEC-001 Personal data is written to logs in clear text
Severity: Medium
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/logger.ts:50-76 (redaction lists), src/server/logger.ts:83-87 (`isSensitiveKey`); line numbers as of the first review
Description: The logger redacts by key name, and the lists cover secrets, `line1`, and postal code only. Email, phone, personal names, subdistrict, district, province, IP address, review comments, and tracking numbers pass through unchanged at any depth, and so does an email inside an error message. `errorResponse` logs the original error with its message and stack (src/server/http.ts:120-123), so a database error that quotes its arguments would put a customer's email or phone into the log without any developer choosing to log it. Failure scenario: a checkout handler logs `{ order }` or an unexpected error carries the address payload; the customer's name, phone, and delivery area are then readable by anyone with access to the host's log collector, outside the database's access control and outside any PDPA deletion. No log call site exists yet, which is why this is Medium and not higher.
Reproduction: `tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts`, block "SEC-001" (17 expected-fail cases, one per key plus free-text email).
Remediation: Implement the logger list in SR-04; keep an `Error`'s own `name`. Extend src/server/logger.test.ts accordingly.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 4407b71. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: the 17 original cases pass unchanged. 16 new cases written for the re-check pass: an email under harmless keys, in the message, the request id, logger bindings, a `Map` key, an object key, a `Set`, a symbol, a cause chain, an error's extra property, and a `URL` object; key spellings such as `E_Mail`, `X-Real-IP`, `postal code`; `Headers` and `URLSearchParams` redacted by name; `toJSON` not called; `AppError` keeps its name while a person's `name` is redacted. Code read: `SENSITIVE_KEY_PARTS` and `SENSITIVE_KEYS` in src/server/logger.ts match the SR-04 lists of the first review entry for entry. What the re-check found beyond the original scope is registered as SEC-014 and SEC-015.

### SEC-002 Secrets survive logger redaction
Severity: Medium
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/logger.ts:89-95 (`scrubString`), src/server/logger.ts:50-61 (`SENSITIVE_KEY_PARTS`); line numbers as of the first review
Description: (a) The URL-credential pattern stops at the first `@`, so for `postgresql://app:Pa@ssTAIL99@db:5432/shop` the log contains `ssTAIL99@db`; a database password containing an unencoded `@` is a common configuration and connection errors quote the URL. (b) `password=hunter2` inside an error message is logged as is; the project's own test fixture at src/server/http.test.ts:137 uses such a message, and the test checks only the response body. (c) A URL such as `/reset?token=...` logged under a harmless key keeps its token. (d) A raw `Cookie: name=value` line inside a string or array keeps the session cookie. (e) The keys `pwd`, `pass`, `passphrase`, `otp`, `pin`, `signature`, `jwt`, and `hash` are not treated as sensitive. An attacker with read access to logs (or a support engineer, or a third-party log service) obtains credentials or live session tokens.
Reproduction: same test file, block "SEC-002" (12 expected-fail cases).
Remediation: Implement the string-scrubbing and key rules in SR-04.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 4407b71. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: the 12 original cases pass unchanged, so items (a) to (e) are closed. 16 new cases pass: upper-case scheme, a password containing `/` and several `@`, an empty user name, a URL after a local-scheme URL and a blank, tabs around `=`, `COOKIE   :   a=...; b=...`, `set-cookie:sid=...`, JSON text with escaped quotes, a quoted value with a blank, `bEaReR`, `BEARER<tab>`, standalone `Basic`, `authorization: basic ...`, several secret query parameters, secrets under sensitive keys in nested arrays and objects, and a real file position kept readable. Each text was checked as a field, as an error message with its stack, and as the log message. The re-check also found four narrower bypasses that the original finding did not describe, two of them introduced by exemptions added in the fix; they are registered as SEC-012 to SEC-015 (all Low) instead of keeping this finding open, because every flaw it described is gone.

### SEC-003 A client chooses the request id written to logs and responses
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/request-id.ts:37-47 (`resolveRequestId`); line numbers as of the first review
Description: Any well-formed `X-Request-Id` from any client is adopted as the request id for the response header, the error envelope, and every log line. An attacker can replay an id copied from another request's error message, or send one id on thousands of requests, so that a later log search for that id returns the attacker's lines mixed with the real ones. Injection is not possible (the pattern check holds; verified).
Reproduction: same test file, block "SEC-003".
Remediation: SR-03. Default to generating the id; accept the header only when the caller passes an explicit "trusted proxy configured" flag.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 4407b71. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: the original case passes unchanged; 4 new cases pass: the id is ignored whether passed as a `Request`, a `Headers` object, or a raw value, with no options, `{}`, or `trustIncoming: false`; only the boolean `true` is trusted (`"true"`, `1`, `{}`, `[]` are not); two calls with the same client id give two different UUIDs; with trust on, a client-added second header line is not reused. Code read: `resolveRequestId` takes the decision as an argument defaulting to "do not trust" and the module does not read the environment, as SR-03 requires. Not verifiable yet: that handlers derive `trustIncoming` from the SR-01 configuration and log a client id only as `clientRequestId`, because no handler exists.

### SEC-004 One flood of new keys refuses every other rate-limited action
Severity: Medium
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/rate-limit.ts:116-121 (saturation branch), src/server/rate-limit.ts:119 (`resetAt` at saturation), src/server/rate-limit.ts:169 (single shared store); line numbers as of the first review
Description: All rules share one store of 100,000 windows. When it is full, every subject without a window is refused. Sign-up windows last one hour and seller-application windows a day, so 100,000 distinct sign-up keys (for example one per IPv6 /64 if addresses are keyed in full, or one per forged `X-Forwarded-For` value if that header were ever trusted) make checkout, uploads, reviews, and sign-in refuse every new subject for up to an hour, and the flood can be repeated. Checkout windows last one minute, so in practice every customer is refused. In addition the refusal reports the rule's whole window as `Retry-After` (24 hours for a seller application) although space may free within a second. Nothing calls the limiter yet, and SR-01 raises the cost of creating keys, hence Medium.
Reproduction: same test file, block "SEC-004" (2 expected-fail cases; the first fills the default store with 100,000 sign-up keys and shows a checkout key refused).
Remediation: SR-02 items 1, 3, and 4. Fail-closed itself stays.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 4407b71. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: the 2 original cases pass unchanged. New case: all three anonymous rules flooded past capacity on the default store (126,000 distinct subjects); the store holds exactly 120,000 windows; a new subject on each flooded rule is refused with `saturated: true` and a retry of at most 60 seconds; a subject that already had a window is still counted (no eviction, no fail-open); `checkout`, `uploads`, `seller-application`, and `review-create` each still accept a new subject; exactly 3 warn lines were written (one per rule) and none contains a subject; after 61 seconds the one-minute rule accepts new subjects again while `sign-up` still refuses. A regression guard checks that `sign-up` holds exactly 40,000 windows. SR-02 items 1, 3, and 4 are met. The capacity split is accepted in SR-02; the related trap found in the re-check is SEC-018.

### SEC-005 Two rules that share a key share one counter and one window
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/rate-limit.ts:147-164 (`consume` takes a bare key), src/server/rate-limit.ts:110-114 (existing window reused whatever the rule); line numbers as of the first review
Description: The stored key is exactly what the caller passes, and the window length is fixed by whichever rule touched the key first. If two handlers both pass `user:<id>`, ten checkouts in a minute consume the hourly review allowance, and a review made first would give checkout a one-hour window. The result is either a limit that is far too loose or a user locked out of an unrelated action. It is a trap in the interface rather than a present bug, because no caller exists.
Reproduction: same test file, block "SEC-005".
Remediation: SR-02 item 2: the limiter takes the rule name and prefixes the key itself.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 4407b71. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: the original case passes unchanged; 2 new cases pass. A subject cannot be shaped to reach another rule's counter (`x` under `sign-in-address` against `x`, `sign-in-address:x`, and `sign-in-email:x` under the other rule: all separate; rule names cannot contain a colon). A 500-character subject is stored as a digest, and a neighbouring subject, the digest itself, and `sha256:<digest>` each get their own window. The window length follows the rule (checkout resets after a minute, review does not, for the same subject). Reusing a rule name with a different limit throws `TypeError`.

### SEC-006 API responses carry no cache directive
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/http.ts:58-62 (`buildHeaders`); line numbers as of the first review
Description: `ok`, `paginated`, and `errorResponse` set no `Cache-Control`. These helpers will carry profiles, addresses, and orders, and DEPLOYMENT.md expects a reverse proxy in front. A proxy or CDN configured to cache by URL could store one customer's `/api/v1/me/addresses` response and serve it to another. Whether the framework adds its own directive to route-handler responses was not verified, because no route handler exists.
Reproduction: same test file, block "SEC-006".
Remediation: Set `Cache-Control: no-store` in `buildHeaders` unless the caller passes a different value on purpose (public catalog responses may opt in to caching).
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 4407b71. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: the original case passes unchanged. New case: `ok`, `created`, `paginated`, and `errorResponse` (404, 429, 400, 500) all answer `Cache-Control: no-store`, including when the caller passes `Cache-Control: public, max-age=600` in `headers`, and `errorResponse` ignores a forced `cacheControl` property; only the explicit `cacheControl` option of a success helper changes the value. Observation, no change required: `cacheControl: ""` produces an empty header, which is the caller's explicit choice. Still not verified: what the framework adds or changes on a real route-handler response, and that only endpoints documented as public use the option; both need a route handler.

### SEC-007 Unicode line separators and bidirectional controls reach the log unescaped
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/logger.ts:184 (`JSON.stringify` of the line); line number as of the first review
Description: CR, LF, and C0 control characters are escaped (verified), so a classic forged-line attack fails. `JSON.stringify` leaves U+2028, U+2029, U+0085, and bidirectional overrides (U+202E and similar) as raw characters. Log viewers and terminals that treat the first three as line breaks will show attacker text (a product title, a `User-Agent`) as a separate, forged-looking entry, and a bidirectional override can visually reorder a line for a human reader. Machine parsing is unaffected.
Reproduction: `node -e 'console.log(JSON.stringify({m:"a\u2028b\u202Ec"}))'` prints the characters raw (run 2026-10-11).
Remediation: After `JSON.stringify`, replace those code points with their `\uXXXX` escapes.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 4407b71. This finding had no test in `tests/security`; 12 were added, one per code point of the original list (U+0085, U+2028, U+2029, U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069). Each puts the character into the message, a field key, a nested value, a binding key and value, an error message, and the request id, and checks that the output line does not contain the raw character, does contain its escape, and parses back to the original message. All 12 pass (`pnpm exec vitest run --project security`). The characters this finding did not list (C1 controls, direction marks, zero-width and other format characters) are still written raw; that is SEC-016.

### SEC-008 Local database image is referenced by a mutable tag
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: devops
Location: docker-compose.yml:18, docker-compose.yml:49 as of the first review; since commit af7acf8 the image is named once, at docker-compose.yml:21 (`x-postgres-image`), and used at lines 26 and 57
Description: `postgres:18-trixie` is a moving tag. A later pull can silently bring a different image than the one exercised in T-012; if the tag were ever compromised upstream, every developer machine would run it with the development data mounted. Local scope only.
Reproduction: read the file; `docker compose config` shows `image: postgres:18-trixie` with no digest.
Remediation: Pin the digest (`postgres:18-trixie@sha256:...`) and let the dependency bot update it, or record the accepted risk here.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit af7acf8 (`git show --stat af7acf8`: `docker-compose.yml` and `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`). File read: one YAML anchor, `x-postgres-image`, holds `postgres:18-trixie@sha256:74935e72241653ca55e0414067e6d8763aceb8a810eb51b452253ec3dcfc4336`, and both services take their image from it; no other `image:` line exists. `docker compose config --images` (Docker 29.8.2) prints that reference with the digest twice, once per service. `docker compose ps` shows `db-dev` and `db-test` running from the same reference, both healthy. `docker image inspect postgres:18-trixie --format "{{json .RepoDigests}}"` prints the same digest, so the pin is the image that is actually present locally. `git grep "postgres:"` finds no other place that starts a database image (the other matches are prose and test fixtures). Not verified: that the digest is the one the upstream registry publishes for that tag (nothing was pulled or queried for this re-check; it is the digest of the image exercised in T-012, which is what the finding asked for). Standing condition: no dependency bot updates the digest yet (T-070), so the image receives no security updates until someone follows "Updating the database image" in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`; listed under residual risks. Local scope only, as before.

### SEC-009 Nothing stops the local placeholder database credentials from being used outside local development
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-12), with the follow-up SEC-025
Owner: devops
Location: docker-compose.yml:29, docker-compose.yml:60, `.env.example` entries `DATABASE_URL` and `DATABASE_URL_TEST` (named rather than numbered because the file changes; re-checked 2026-10-28 against c711239: both still hold the local-only passwords); the control, as of commit b40abf0 (the function moved in commit b123ef9), is src/server/env.ts:61-65 (the two passwords), 207-267 (`containsPlaceholder`, `decodeEscapes`, `hasPlaceholderPassword`, and `checkDatabaseUrlForProduction`, which holds the refusal of `DATABASE_URL` and of `PGPASSWORD`), 296-301 (the call in `parseEnv` and the refusal of `DATABASE_URL_TEST`), 340-347 (`readDatabaseUrl`), and src/instrumentation.ts:35-55 (`register`, the start-up hook; lines 44-50 end the process)
Description: The compose file falls back to publicly known passwords when the variables are unset, and `.env.example` contains working connection strings. Both are labelled local-only and the ports are bound to 127.0.0.1 (verified), so there is no exposure today. The failure scenario is procedural: someone copies `.env.example` or the compose file to a staging host, and the database then runs with a password published in the repository. Comments do not prevent that; a startup check does.
Reproduction: `docker compose config` with no `.env` renders `POSTGRES_PASSWORD: local_dev_only_not_a_secret`.
Remediation: In `src/server/env.ts`, when `NODE_ENV=production` refuse to start if `DATABASE_URL` contains `local_dev_only_not_a_secret` or `local_test_only_not_a_secret`, or if `DATABASE_URL_TEST` is set. Keep the 127.0.0.1 bindings.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-12, commit ff4a567 (T-011), read and tested on commit d61099f. The remediation is implemented as written.
- Code read: `parseEnv` and `readDatabaseUrl` refuse a `DATABASE_URL` that contains either published password when `NODE_ENV=production`, and `parseEnv` refuses any non-empty `DATABASE_URL_TEST`. An error carries the variable name and one of the fixed rule texts of `ENV_RULES`, never a value. `register` in `src/instrumentation.ts` calls `loadEnvAtStartup`, sets exit code 1, and ends the process after the log line.
- Real start, production build: `node node_modules/next/dist/bin/next start -p 3197` with the local `.env` (a copy of `.env.example`) exited with code 1. The output has one error line with `"issues":[{"variable":"DATABASE_URL","rule":"must not contain a local placeholder password from .env.example when NODE_ENV=production"},{"variable":"DATABASE_URL_TEST","rule":"must not be set when NODE_ENV=production"}]` and no occurrence of either password or of `postgresql://`. The framework printed "Ready" before the hook ran, as devops reported; no request was sent in that window. No node process was left.
- Tests, `tests/security/t011-env-health-recheck.test.ts`, blocks "SEC-009 re-check" (7 cases, all pass): both passwords refused in nine positions of the URL each (the two lines of `.env.example` as copied, the authority, the `password` query parameter, as part of a longer password, as the user name), through `parseEnv` and `readDatabaseUrl`; `DATABASE_URL_TEST` refused whatever it holds and both faults reported together; six misspellings of `NODE_ENV` (`Production`, a blank in front, `staging`) stop the start with the `NODE_ENV` rule instead of switching the refusal off; the error, its stack, its issues, and the start-up log line contain no value; `register` with a placeholder in production sets exit code 1, requests `exit(1)` once, and rethrows. That the cases fail without the control was not shown by removing it, because `src/server/env.ts` is not the security agent's to edit, even for a moment; the evidence is the case in the same block that accepts the same URLs when `NODE_ENV` is not `production`, so the refusals come from the rule and from nothing else.
- Limits, each listed under residual risks: the rule applies only when `NODE_ENV=production`, so a deployed host that runs `next dev` is not refused; it runs when the application starts, not when the Prisma CLI, the seed, or a script reads `DATABASE_URL` (the database client still reads the variable itself until code-review finding CR-23); `docker-compose.yml` still falls back to the published passwords, by design, for local use; and the exit waits for one standard-output callback (code-review finding CR-44, see residual risks).
- Not closed by this at the time: the check read the raw text of the variable, so an encoded spelling of a placeholder, and a placeholder supplied through `PGPASSWORD`, were accepted. That was SEC-025, fixed in commit b123ef9 and re-checked on 2026-10-12 in the seventh round. It did not keep this finding open: the scenario of this finding is a file copied by mistake, which was refused, and writing `%5F` for an underscore is not a mistake.
- Found in the seventh round: the refusals run in the start-up hook only, and one variable in the server's environment keeps the framework from calling the hook (SEC-029, Low, open). The copied-file scenario of this finding is not affected.

### SEC-010 Development dependency with a published high-severity advisory
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN
Owner: orchestrator (package.json and lockfile)
Location: package.json:38 (`eslint-config-next` 16.4.0, which brings `@next/eslint-plugin-next` > `fast-glob` > `micromatch` > `braces` <= 3.0.3)
Description: `pnpm audit` reports GHSA-vfj7-8cjw-p6xm (stack exhaustion in `braces` on deeply nested patterns), rated high by the advisory, with no patched version listed. The package is reached only through the lint toolchain; it is not in the production dependency tree (`pnpm audit --prod`: no known vulnerabilities) and the patterns it expands come from the repository's own lint configuration, not from users. Project severity is therefore Low, but it will make a CI audit step fail as soon as one exists.
Reproduction: `pnpm audit` (exit 1, "1 vulnerabilities found, Severity: 1 high").
Remediation: Update when a fixed release exists, or add a documented override or audit exception limited to this advisory; make the CI gate `pnpm audit --prod` plus a reviewed exception list.
Re-check evidence: none yet. Still open on 2026-10-12, and wider: `pnpm audit` (pnpm 12.11.2) exits 1 with "4 vulnerabilities found, Severity: 1 moderate | 3 high". Beside the `braces` advisory above there are now three that arrived with the Prisma CLI in T-013, all on the path `@prisma/client > prisma`: GHSA-ggr8-5vv4-36mx (`deepmerge-ts` below 8.0.0, through `@prisma/config`; high), GHSA-3f6p-5ww8-9rcr (`mysql2` below 3.22.0; high), and GHSA-rgwj-5xj2-c3m3 (`mysql2` up to 3.23.0; moderate). `pnpm audit --prod` still exits 0 with "No known vulnerabilities found", so none is in the production tree, and the application connects to PostgreSQL only; whether the Prisma CLI ever loads `mysql2` for a PostgreSQL schema was not examined. The severity stays Low on that basis, and the remediation is the same: an exception list reviewed per advisory, and `pnpm audit --prod` as the gate.
Update 2026-10-26 (SEC-089 re-check, tree efbdb9f): `pnpm audit` (pnpm 12.11.2) exits 1 with 8 advisories (2 high, 4 moderate, 2 low). `braces` is no longer reported. Still present: GHSA-ggr8-5vv4-36mx (`deepmerge-ts` <8.0.0, Prisma CLI). New since the 2026-10-13 audit, versions not changed by any recent commit: `undici` >=8.0.0 <8.12.2 through `jsdom` (the Vitest DOM environment), GHSA-xjj6-f33q-929c (high), GHSA-4qv8-2p9j-2685 and GHSA-f72v-6w45-q472 (moderate); `fast-uri` <3.1.9 through `prisma > @prisma/dev > @prisma/streams-local > ajv`, GHSA-w74x-v6f9-j3xg and GHSA-wjr9-j795-jxcr (moderate), GHSA-xhq7-xmqj-769h and GHSA-333x-w4r6-6crp (low). `pnpm why <pkg> --prod` is empty for `deepmerge-ts`, `undici`, and `fast-uri`, and `pnpm audit --prod` exits 0, so none is in the production tree. Severity stays Low; the advisory texts were not read.

### SEC-011 .gitignore does not cover key material
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: orchestrator (root config)
Location: .gitignore:13-16
Description: `.env` files are ignored (verified with `git check-ignore`), but private keys and certificates (`*.pem`, `*.key`, `*.p12`, `*.pfx`, `id_rsa*`) are not, and no secret scanner runs on commits. A locally generated TLS key or a downloaded service-account file placed in the repository folder would be picked up by `git add .`.
Reproduction: `git check-ignore -v server.key` prints nothing.
Remediation: Add those patterns; add a secret scan to CI (planned in T-070).
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11. `git check-ignore -v server.key id_rsa cert.pem` prints `.gitignore:20:*.key server.key`, `.gitignore:23:id_rsa* id_rsa`, `.gitignore:19:*.pem cert.pem`, exit 0. The ignore rules are in place. The secret scan in CI is not part of this finding any more; it stays planned in T-070 and listed under residual risks.

### SEC-012 A URL password survives when a local-scheme URL starts the same run of text
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/logger.ts:224-248 (`maskUrlUserInfo`: the exemption at line 240 applies to the whole run, and line 245 continues the search after the run)
Description: The fix for SEC-002 exempts the schemes `file`, `node`, `webpack`, `webpack-internal`, and `turbopack` from user-info masking, so that stack frames stay readable. The exemption is applied to the whole unbroken run of non-blank characters that follows the scheme, and the scan then skips that run. A connection URL that follows a local-scheme URL without a blank, quote, or angle bracket in between is therefore never looked at: `at connect (file:///app/db.js:1:1):postgresql://app:PASSWORD@db:5432/shop`, `webpack-internal:///./src/db.ts?u=postgresql://app:PASSWORD@db:5432/shop`, and `[file:///a.js,redis://:PASSWORD@cache:6379]` are written with the password in clear text. When the host has no dot (`db`, `cache`, `localhost`, the usual container names) the email mask does not catch it either. An attacker with read access to logs obtains a database or cache password. It needs two URLs glued together in one error text, which is unusual, hence Low.
Reproduction: `tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts`, block "SEC-012" (3 expected-fail cases).
Remediation: SR-04 addition of 2026-10-11: the exemption covers only the local URL itself. Any further `://` inside the same run must still be masked, and the scan must stay linear (for example by remembering, while walking the run, the last `://` whose scheme is not local, instead of restarting the walk).
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 28c92d1. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: the 3 original cases pass with their bodies unchanged (`git show 28c92d1 -- tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts` changes only `it.fails` to `it`). 7 new cases pass: two local-scheme URLs in front of the connection URL; `turbopack://` with a `mongodb+srv://` URL in its query; `node://...;amqp://...`; an `@` inside the local path in front of the real URL; mixed-case schemes with a password that itself contains `://` and `@`; a connection URL followed by a local one; and a local stack frame with `@scope` in its path, which stays readable. Code read: `maskUrlUserInfo` walks each run once and masks from the first `://` whose scheme is not local to the last `@` of the run.

### SEC-013 A secret shaped like a file position is exempt from masking
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/logger.ts:262 (`FILE_NAME_PATTERN`), src/server/logger.ts:316 (`isFilePosition`)
Description: To keep `session.ts:12:5` readable, a `key: value` pair is left alone when the key ends in a dot and one to four letters or digits and the value starts with a digit. Nothing checks that the value is only a line number. `db.password.prod:9x7QzSecret`, `api.token.v1:12345abcdef`, and `auth.secret.key:0abc` are written unmasked. Dotted configuration keys followed by a value that starts with a digit are an ordinary shape for properties files and for values such as numeric tokens.
Reproduction: same test file, block "SEC-013" (3 expected-fail cases); the plain test "keeps a real file position readable" must keep passing after the fix.
Remediation: SR-04 addition of 2026-10-11: exempt only when the colon is followed by digits, optionally `:` and digits, and then the end of the text, a blank, a line end, or `)`.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 28c92d1. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: the 3 original cases pass with their bodies unchanged (`git show 28c92d1 -- tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts` changes only `it.fails` to `it`). 8 new cases pass: a value that starts like a position but is not one is masked (`:9x7...`, 11 digits, `:12:` followed by text, `:12:5` followed by letters, `:12:5:...`, `:12,...`, `:443/...`), while `session.ts:12:5)`, `token.ts:7` at the end of the text, and `cookie.tsx:120:33` in front of a line end stay readable. Accepted remainder, recorded in SR-04 and under residual risks: a value made only of digits after a dotted key that ends like a file extension (`user.pin:1234`) cannot be told from a file position and is not masked.

### SEC-014 Clipping before scrubbing leaves the start of a secret or an email at the cut
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/logger.ts:443-446 (`scrubString` clips, then scans), src/server/logger.ts:463-468 (`scrubWithinBudget`, the same at the entry-budget boundary)
Description: To bound the cost, a string is cut to 8,000 characters (or to what is left of the 64,000-character entry budget) before the masks run. The masks then see an incomplete shape and do not recognise it: a URL whose `@` lies beyond the cut is written as `postgresql://app:SECRET[Truncated]`, and an email whose domain is cut is written as `somchai@exam[Truncated]`. Observed on 2026-10-11 with both. The first part of a password, or a customer's whole local part, reaches the log. It needs a text longer than 8,000 characters with the sensitive part exactly at the boundary; long database error messages and stacks are the realistic source. `key=value`, `key: value`, and `Bearer` pairs are not affected (the part in front of the cut is still masked; verified).
Reproduction: same test file, block "SEC-014" (2 expected-fail cases).
Remediation: SR-04 addition of 2026-10-11. Keep the clip first, but treat the cut as hostile: after a clip, mask from the last `://` that has no blank after it to the end, and mask the local part in front of an `@` that lies within the last 256 characters and has no complete domain. Alternatively scan a bounded look-ahead (for example 512 characters past the limit) and clip afterwards.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 28c92d1. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: the 2 original cases pass with their bodies unchanged (`git show 28c92d1 -- tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts` changes only `it.fails` to `it`). 11 new cases pass. For five shapes (a connection URL, a connection URL behind a local-scheme URL, a `key=value` pair, a header line, a bearer token) the cut was moved through every position of the secret, once at the 8,000-character clip and once at the point where the 64,000-character entry budget runs out; no part of the marker was written at any position, and each loop also checks that the cut really passed through the secret. An email with an apostrophe and a tag loses its whole local part at every cut position from the `@` onwards. Accepted remainder, recorded in SR-04: a cut that falls inside the local part, in front of the `@`, leaves that fragment.

### SEC-015 Key and key=value matching misses shapes that SR-04 now lists
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api (logger); the rule itself was extended by the security owner on 2026-10-11
Location: src/server/logger.ts:291 and 322 (at most 4 blanks around a separator), src/server/logger.ts:307 (inline keys are matched against the "contains" list only), src/server/logger.ts:100-138 (`SENSITIVE_KEYS`), src/server/logger.ts:373-384 (`isEmailLocalChar`)
Description: Found while trying to break the SEC-001 and SEC-002 fixes. (a) More than four blanks between a key and its separator, or after `=`, defeat the mask: `DB_PASSWORD        = value` (an aligned configuration dump) and `password=      value` are written in clear text. (b) `'password' => 'value'`, the form Node prints for a `Map` entry, is not masked. (c) Inside strings only the "contains" list is used, so `?sid=...`, `?otp=...`, `pin=...`, and `pass=...` keep their values although the same names are redacted as object keys. (d) The keys `userName`, `customerName`, `contactName`, `tel`, and `mobile` are not on any list; they are the obvious names for a copy of `User.name` or a phone number. This part is a gap in the rule as first written, not a deviation by the implementer. (e) An email with an apostrophe in its local part, which `z.email()` accepts, is only partly masked: `somchai'o@example.com` is written as `somchai'[REDACTED]`. None of this is reachable today because nothing logs such data; it weakens the second line of defence.
Reproduction: same test file, block "SEC-015" (13 expected-fail cases).
Remediation: The SR-04 additions of 2026-10-11: accept up to 32 blanks on either side of the separator (still a bounded look-back), treat `=>` as a separator, match the exact inline keys `sid`, `pass`, `pin`, `otp`, add the five keys to the "equals" list, and accept `'` as a local-part character.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 28c92d1. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: the 13 original cases pass with their bodies unchanged (`git show 28c92d1 -- tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts` changes only `it.fails` to `it`). 12 new cases pass: 32 blanks on both sides of `=` and of `:`; tabs and blanks mixed; `=>` without blanks, quoted, and with a long gap; `PIN : ...`; `&otp=...`; `sid=...` inside a cookie line under a harmless name; a quoted `pass`; addresses with several apostrophes fully masked while an opening quote is left alone; the five keys in other spellings (`user_name`, `Customer-Name`, `CONTACTNAME`, `Tel`, `MOBILE`); and look-alike words (`passed=3`, `spin=2`, `compass:north`, `pinned: yes`) left readable.

### SEC-016 Invisible, format, and C1 control characters reach the log unescaped
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api (logger); the rule itself was extended by the security owner on 2026-10-11
Location: src/server/logger.ts:638-643 (`UNSAFE_OUTPUT_RANGES`)
Description: SEC-007 listed line separators and bidirectional embeddings, overrides, and isolates, and those are now escaped. The implementer correctly pointed out that the direction marks U+200E, U+200F, and U+061C were not on the list. The re-check found the same for DEL and the C1 controls U+0080 to U+009F other than U+0085 (U+009B is the 8-bit form of the escape-sequence introducer, which some terminals honour, so a logged `User-Agent` could move the cursor or recolour text when an operator tails the log), the soft hyphen, zero-width characters U+200B to U+200D, U+2060 to U+206F, the byte order mark, U+FFF9 to U+FFFB, U+180E, and the invisible tag characters U+E0001 and U+E0020 to U+E007F (which can hide text from a human reader while a tool still reads it). The effect is on people and tools reading the log, not on parsing: an entry can be made to look different from what it contains. Machine parsing is unaffected, hence Low.
Reproduction: same test file, block "SEC-016" (18 expected-fail cases, one representative per group).
Remediation: The SR-04 addition of 2026-10-11: escape `[\u007F-\u009F\u2028\u2029\p{Cf}]` (with the `u` flag) in the output line; write a character outside the Basic Multilingual Plane as its two surrogate escapes.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 28c92d1. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: the 18 original cases pass with their bodies unchanged (`git show 28c92d1 -- tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts` changes only `it.fails` to `it`). 2 new cases pass: 16 further code points (C1 controls U+0081, U+008F, U+0090; format characters U+0600, U+06DD, U+070F, U+200C, U+2061, U+2067, U+206A, U+FFFA, U+FFFB; and U+110BD, U+1D173, U+E0020, U+E007F outside the Basic Multilingual Plane) are escaped in the message, a key, and nested values, and the line parses back to the original text; Thai text, an emoji, and an accented letter are written as they are, and a lone surrogate does not break the line. Code read: the pattern has the `u` flag and is built from U+007F to U+009F, the two separators, and category Cf, as SR-04 states.

### SEC-017 Details passed to an INTERNAL_ERROR reach the client
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/errors.ts:88-100 (the constructor fixes the message of an `INTERNAL_ERROR` but keeps `options.details`), src/server/errors.ts:103-105 (`toPublic`)
Description: The fix made the public message of a 500 constant, whatever the caller passes. `details` is not treated the same way: `new AppError("INTERNAL_ERROR", { details: { sql: "...", target: "..." } })` sends those details to the client (observed 2026-10-11). docs/API_CONTRACTS.md says that an `INTERNAL_ERROR` response "carries nothing about the cause", so the code does not meet its own contract. It takes a developer attaching details to a 500 on purpose, and the helper `internalError(cause)` does not allow it, hence Low; but a 500 is exactly where a developer is tempted to attach diagnostic data, and the type system does not stop it.
Reproduction: same test file, block "SEC-017" (1 expected-fail case).
Remediation: Decision of the security owner: details of an `INTERNAL_ERROR` are forced to `{}` in the constructor, next to the fixed message (and the same for any future code whose status is 500 or above). Diagnostic data belongs in `cause`, which goes to the log only.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 28c92d1. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: the original case passes with its body unchanged (`git show 28c92d1 -- tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts` changes only `it.fails` to `it`). 1 new case passes: details are `{}` on the error object, in `JSON.stringify`, in `toPublic`, and in the response body for an `AppError` created with details and a cause, a plain `Error`, a thrown object, and a thrown string; a 409 keeps its details. Code read: src/server/errors.ts forces details to `{}` for every status of 500 or above.

### SEC-018 One extra rate-limit rule makes a built-in rule throw at its first use
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/rate-limit.ts:261-296 (`register` runs on first use of each rule), src/server/rate-limit.ts:284-288 (capacity check), src/server/rate-limit.ts:124-137 (built-in capacities add up to exactly `MAX_TOTAL_WINDOWS`)
Description: Rules are registered lazily, when `consume` first sees them, and the built-in rules already use the whole budget of 200,000 windows. If any other rule is ever passed to the process-wide limiter (the API invites it: `consume` accepts any rule object), it registers without complaint as long as it is seen early, and the capacity check then fails for whichever built-in rule happens to be used last. Observed on 2026-10-11: after one call with a new 10,000-key rule, `review-create` throws `TypeError` on every call. In production that would be a permanent 500 on one endpoint, decided by request order after each restart, and it would appear only after deployment. It fails closed, so it is an availability trap rather than a bypass, and it needs a developer mistake, hence Low.
Reproduction: same test file, block "SEC-018" (1 expected-fail case).
Remediation: Make the outcome independent of call order. For example register the built-in rules when the limiter is created, so that a rule that does not fit is the one refused, at its own first use and in every test run; or take the full rule set as an argument of `createRateLimiter` and validate it there. A rule added later must come with a new capacity split recorded in SR-02.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit 28c92d1. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: the original case passes with its body unchanged (`git show 28c92d1 -- tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts` changes only `it.fails` to `it`). 3 new cases pass: a rule set over the budget (the built-in rules plus a rule with one key), a set with a repeated name, and a set with an invalid name each throw `TypeError` in `createRateLimiter`; a rule outside the set, a built-in name with another limit, and a near-miss name are rejected on every call during three rounds mixed with all built-in rules, which keep working, and the rejected calls consume nothing; changing the caller's rule object after creation has no effect; an empty rule set rejects every call (fails closed).

### SEC-019 readJsonBody buffers a request body of any size
Severity: Medium
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/http.ts:221-229 (`readJsonBody` calls `request.json()` with no limit; the comment at line 219 says so); line numbers as of the second re-check
Description: `readJsonBody` is documented as the one way a handler reads a JSON body, and it reads whatever is sent. Observed on 2026-10-11: a 2 MiB document is accepted, with or without a `Content-Length` header, and nothing in the helper would stop 2 GiB. The body is held in memory as text and again as a parsed value, and the handler pattern in the same file reads it before `requireUser`, so the cost is available to an anonymous client on every mutation endpoint. The application is one process; a few concurrent oversized requests exhaust its memory and take the whole site down, and they can be repeated at will because the per-user rate limits apply only after the body is read. Not reachable today, because no handler exists, and a reverse proxy may add a limit of its own; but it becomes reachable with the first handler that follows the documented pattern, hence Medium and not Low. Whether the framework limits route-handler bodies by itself was not verified.
Reproduction: `tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts`, block "SEC-019" (2 expected-fail cases: 2 MiB with and without a declared length must be refused with 413).
Remediation: Rule SR-05. `readJsonBody(request, { maxBytes })` with a default of 102,400 bytes and a ceiling of 1,048,576; refuse on `Content-Length` before reading; count bytes while reading the stream and stop at the limit; answer `413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`; document the limit in `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md`. Must be fixed before the first route handler that reads a body is merged.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, commit e2a4a7a. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: 237 passed, 5 expected fail (SEC-020), exit 0, three runs in a row. The 2 original cases pass with their bodies unchanged (`git show e2a4a7a -- tests/security` changes one line, `it.fails` to `it`). Code read (`src/server/http.ts`, `contentLengthAllows`, `readBytesUpTo`, `readJsonBody`): the limit is validated first, then the content type, then `Content-Length`, and only then is a reader taken; the count is of `byteLength` and runs while reading; the text is decoded only after the whole body is known to fit. The implementer's own tests in `src/server/http.test.ts` were read; they are not relied on here. 14 new cases in `tests/security/sec019-body-limit-recheck.test.ts` pass:
- Before reading: with a declared length over the limit (one over the default, one over the ceiling, 2^31, 2^64, and one over a custom limit) nothing at all is pulled from an endless body and `bodyUsed` stays false. 19 further spellings that a lenient parser would read as a small number (`0x1`, `0b1`, `1_0`, `1e0`, `-0`, `+1`, `1,1`, a superscript digit, a no-break space, `1n`, and others) are refused with 413 with nothing pulled.
- Missing or untrue length: an endless body with no length, or declaring 0, 2, or exactly the limit, is refused in chunks of 1, 1,000, and 16,384 bytes after more than the limit and at most the limit plus one chunk; the source is cancelled and is not asked for anything afterwards. A first chunk of 8 MiB is refused after that one read. The boundary is exact for five chunk sizes: the limit passes, one more byte is 413. With no option the cut is at 102,400 bytes plus one chunk; a `maxBytes` above 1 MiB throws before anything is pulled.
- Bytes, not characters: 40,000 Thai characters (40,002 characters, 120,002 bytes) are refused although the character count is under the limit, also when the length is declared in characters, and a stream of them is cut off before its end; Thai text of exactly 102,400 bytes passes and comes back unchanged, and one more byte is 413; four-byte characters and a byte order mark are counted as their bytes.
- Envelope: both refusals (by declared length and while reading) give status 413, the body `{ error: { code: "PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE", message, details: {}, requestId } }`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, and the request id header; no log line is written and a marker placed in the body appears in neither the error object nor the response. Nesting 524,288 arrays deep at the ceiling parses, and 1 MiB of unclosed brackets is a 400, not a 500.
- Abort and failure: a source that throws from its cancellation still gets the 413. A body that breaks off mid-read is passed on as the original error (a 500 through `errorResponse`), nothing of the partial body reaches the response or the log, and nothing is read afterwards.
- Over a real connection: `readJsonBody` behind a plain `node:http` server on the loopback interface, sent an endless `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` body, answers 413 after at most the limit plus one network read (106,496 bytes observed in the probe) and takes no more although the sender continues.
What the re-check found beyond this finding is registered as SEC-020. Not verified, because no handler exists: that handlers read bodies only through this helper and in the order of SR-05 item 5, that an endpoint with a raised limit documents it, what the framework does with the connection after the 413 (in the loopback harness, cancelling the body closed the connection before a response could be written; that is the harness's adapter, not the framework's), and that a broken-off upload is not logged at error level once per request.

### SEC-020 The memory held while reading a body grows with the number of chunks, not with the limit
Severity: Medium
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/http.ts:250-275 as of commit e2a4a7a (`readBytesUpTo`: line 266 keeps every chunk object, line 260 counts `byteLength` without checking that the chunk is bytes, line 263 awaits the cancellation). After the fix the function is at src/server/http.ts:266-301: line 282 the bytes check, line 286 the skip of an empty chunk, lines 287-291 the count and the refusal, lines 292-296 the growth, lines 272-274 the cancellation that is not awaited
Description: The fix for SEC-019 bounds the number of bytes read, and that part holds. It keeps each chunk as a separate object until the body is complete, and the sender decides how many chunks there are. Over HTTP a body can be sent as chunks of one byte, and each arrives as its own object. Measured on 2026-10-11 (Node 24.20.0, forced collection, memory still reachable while the request is being read): a 102,400-byte body in one-byte chunks holds about 24 MB, and a 1,048,576-byte body, the largest an endpoint may allow, about 245 MB; that is roughly 240 bytes held per byte sent. The same numbers were observed with `readJsonBody` behind a plain `node:http` server on the loopback interface, where 614 KB on the wire produced 102,399 separate chunks and 24 MB held, and 6.3 MB on the wire held 245 MB. The sender can stop before the last byte and keep the memory held for as long as the server lets a request stall, and the handler pattern reads the body before the actor is known. So about a hundred slow anonymous connections of 614 KB each can hold more than 2 GB in the one process that serves the whole site, which is the outcome SR-05 exists to prevent, at a higher price than before the fix. Three weaker points in the same loop, none shown to be reachable from the network: (b) a chunk with no bytes never counts towards the limit and is kept, so 200,000 empty chunks hold 42 MB and an endless run of them is never refused; (c) a chunk that is not bytes makes the count not a number, so the limit never triggers, and 400 KiB of text chunks were read against a limit of 10,000 bytes before the call failed with a `RangeError`; (d) the 413 waits for the source to confirm the cancellation, so a source that never does keeps the request pending for ever. Not reachable today, because no handler exists; whether the framework hands small chunks to a route handler unchanged, and how long it lets a body stall, was not verified. It becomes reachable with the first handler that reads a body, hence Medium, as SEC-019 was.
Reproduction: `tests/security/sec019-body-limit-recheck.test.ts`, block "SEC-020" (5 expected-fail cases: memory held for a default-size and a ceiling-size body in one-byte chunks, empty chunks, text chunks, a cancellation that never settles). The thresholds were checked against a reference reader that copies into one buffer: it holds 0.2 MB, 1.1 MB, and 0.1 MB where the current code holds 24 MB, 245 MB, and 42 MB, and the tests ask for less than 4 MB, 8 MB, and 4 MB.
Remediation: SR-05 item 8. Copy each chunk into one buffer that grows up to the limit (or is allocated from a `Content-Length` that already passed the check) and drop the chunk; skip empty chunks; throw a `TypeError` for a chunk that is not a `Uint8Array`; start the cancellation without awaiting it.
