Must be fixed before the first route handler that reads a body is merged, and the upload reader must follow the same rule.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, uncommitted working tree on top of commit 8bbc997 (`git diff -- src/server/http.ts`: one function rewritten, 56 changed lines; the fix has no commit id yet, and the re-check must be repeated if the committed code differs from what was read). Node 24.20.0, Vitest 5.0.3.
- The lent test was not weakened. `git diff -- tests/security` is 100 lines because Prettier re-wrapped four of the five cases. Checked mechanically: `git show HEAD:tests/security/sec019-body-limit-recheck.test.ts`, with `it.fails(` replaced by `it(` and put through `pnpm exec prettier --stdin-filepath`, differs from the working file only in the one header comment line that the replacement itself changed. The committed file has 6 occurrences of `it.fails(` (5 tests and that comment), the working file had 1 (the comment). Bodies, thresholds (4 MB, 8 MB, 4 MB, 11 pulls, 1,000 ms), and timeouts (60,000 ms twice) are identical.
- The five reproductions pass as plain tests: `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, 277 passed, 2 expected fail (SEC-021), exit 0, on four consecutive runs. Measured with the same method as before the fix (forced collection while the body stalls before its last byte): a default-size body in one-byte chunks holds 0.21 MB (before: 24 MB), a ceiling-size body 1.16 MB (before: 245 MB), 200,000 empty chunks 0.10 MB (before: 42 MB). Text chunks: refused at the first one, 1 pull (before: 400). A cancellation that never settles: the 413 arrives at once.
- Code read (src/server/http.ts:266-301). The buffer starts at min(limit, 4,096) bytes; when a chunk does not fit it becomes min(limit, max(twice its size, the size needed)), so it never exceeds the limit and never is smaller than what is needed, because the refusal for `needed > maxBytes` comes first. `needed == maxBytes` is accepted and one more byte is refused, as before. All sizes are at most 1,048,576 (`readJsonBody` validates `maxBytes` before the reader is taken), so there is no overflow in the arithmetic. The buffer is a local variable: after a 413, a server fault, or a broken stream nothing refers to it, and neither the error nor the response nor the log holds any of it. `Content-Length` is still judged before a reader is taken and is not used to size the buffer, so an untrue length cannot make the reader allocate. The result is a view of exactly the bytes read. The implementer's 12 cases in `src/server/http.test.ts` were read; they are not relied on here.
- 35 new cases in `tests/security/sec020-bounded-buffer-recheck.test.ts` pass. The copy: documents of 11 sizes on both sides of every growth point, each cut 8 ways, come back unchanged; so do irregular chunks of 0 to 6,000 bytes with empty ones between, a source that reuses and overwrites one chunk object for every read, a chunk that is a view into a larger buffer, and three bodies read at once. The boundary: for 14 limits from 1 byte to 102,400 (among them 4,095, 4,096, 4,097, 5,000, 8,191 to 8,193, 65,537) and up to 6 chunk sizes each, exactly the limit passes without a cancellation and one more byte is 413 with a cancellation, whether that byte is a chunk of its own or the end of the last chunk; the same at the 1 MiB ceiling in chunks of 3, 4,096, 65,536, 1,000,000, and the whole body; empty chunks move the boundary in neither direction; a true, an understated, and an overstated `Content-Length` behave as before. A body refused after part of it was copied leaves no marker in the error, the response, or the log. Bytes only: 18 kinds of chunk that are not this realm's `Uint8Array` (text, `ArrayBuffer`, `SharedArrayBuffer`, `DataView`, five other typed arrays, an array, a look-alike object, number, bigint, boolean, null, undefined, function, `Blob`, and a `Uint8Array` of another realm) arriving after valid bytes are each a `TypeError` that is not an `AppError`, with no cause, 2 pulls in all, the source cancelled, nothing read afterwards, a 500 response, and exactly one log line, none of which contains the marker; `Buffer` is accepted; the fault does not wait for a cancellation that hangs, survives one that throws, and a cancellation that rejects later causes no unhandled rejection. Memory: 20 stalled default-size bodies in one-byte chunks hold 2.3 MB together (bound 8 MB; about 480 MB before the fix by the earlier measurement); a stalled ceiling-size body holds 1.1 to 1.6 MB for chunks of 1,000, 4,096, 65,536, and 524,287 bytes (bound 3 MB); 50 stalled bodies of 2,000 bytes under the 1 MiB limit hold 0.5 MB, so the limit is not allocated up front. Over a real connection: `readJsonBody` behind a plain `node:http` server on the loopback interface, sent 102,399 HTTP chunks of one byte and then stalled, saw more than 50,000 separate chunks and held less than 4 MB (24 MB before the fix), then returned the value when the last byte came.
- The implementer's own three concerns, judged. (1) The bytes check is bound to one realm: confirmed, a `Uint8Array` made in another realm is refused as a server fault. It fails closed and is accepted; if a framework ever hands over such chunks the symptom is a 500 on every request with a body, visible at the first test. (2) An endless run of empty chunks is never refused: confirmed, and worse than stated when the source answers without yielding, see SEC-021. (3) A transient peak above the limit while the buffer grows: confirmed by reading; the old and the new buffer exist together during one copy, so the peak is below twice the limit plus the chunk in hand (1.5 times the limit when the last doubling is capped), for the length of one copy. Accepted: it is in proportion to the limit, which is what SR-05 item 8 requires.
What the re-check found beyond this finding is registered as SEC-021 (Low). Not verified: the same points as for SEC-019, namely anything through the framework, because no handler exists; and the five reproductions were not re-run against the old code in this re-check (they were recorded as failing at commit 154f764 with bodies that are shown above to be unchanged).

### SEC-021 A body can be read without end when its chunks add nothing to the byte count
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-11)
Owner: backend-api
Location: src/server/http.ts:286 (`if (chunk.byteLength === 0) continue;` has no bound), src/server/http.ts:287-298 (the count trusts `chunk.byteLength`, which a subclass can override, while line 297 copies the real bytes); line numbers as of the SEC-020 fix (commit 9c9158b). After the fix: src/server/http.ts:252 (`MAX_EMPTY_BODY_CHUNKS`), lines 260-267 (`realByteLength`), and in `readBytesUpTo` (lines 297-341) line 318 the real length, lines 319-326 the count of empty chunks and the refusal, line 337 the copy
Description: Two ways are left in which `readBytesUpTo` takes chunks from a source without ever reaching the limit. Memory stays bounded in both, which is why SEC-020 is closed; what is not bounded is the reading itself. (a) A chunk with no bytes is skipped and the loop asks for the next one, with no count of how many were skipped. A source that produces only empty chunks is read for ever and the request never gets an answer. When such a source answers each read without yielding to the event loop, the loop does not yield either: measured on 2026-10-11, 500,000 empty chunks were read in 181 ms and 2,000,000 in 561 ms, and in both runs a 1 ms interval timer did not fire once and a 0 ms timer fired only after the read had ended. With a run that does not end, no timer in the process fires again, a request timeout included, and the one process that serves the whole site stops answering. (b) The count uses the `byteLength` the chunk reports. A subclass of `Uint8Array` passes the bytes check and can report anything: with `NaN` every comparison is false and the limit never triggers, and with an understated length the count runs behind the bytes taken. Measured: 400 chunks of 1,000 bytes, 400,000 bytes in all, were read against a limit of 10,000 bytes in both cases (401 pulls); the call then failed as a 400. Neither was shown to be reachable from the network, hence Low: an HTTP chunk of length zero ends the body, Node's own streams do not emit empty chunks, a body delivered by network events yields between chunks, and no network body arrives as a subclass with its own `byteLength`. Both need a source inside the server process (a framework layer, a proxy adapter, a test double, a future caller that passes a stream it built). Whether the framework can hand an empty chunk to a route handler was not verified. Point (a) was known as part of SEC-020 (b), whose remediation asked only for the chunks not to be kept; the starvation is new evidence from this re-check.
Reproduction: `tests/security/sec020-bounded-buffer-recheck.test.ts`, block "SEC-021" (2 expected-fail cases: a run of 500,000 empty chunks must be refused after at most 204,800 pulls; 400 chunks that report `NaN` as their length must be stopped after at most 11 pulls against a limit of 10,000 bytes).
Remediation: SR-05 item 9. Count the chunks that add no bytes and refuse the body once the count passes a fixed allowance (for example the byte limit; a 413 or a server fault are both acceptable, and the cancellation is started without awaiting it). Refuse a chunk whose `byteLength` is not a non-negative integer the same way as a chunk that is not bytes, or take the length through the built-in getter (`Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(Object.getPrototypeOf(Uint8Array.prototype), "byteLength")`) so that the count and the copy agree. Optional and not required by the tests: yield to the event loop every few thousand chunks, so that even a permitted run cannot hold it. The upload reader must follow the same rule when it is written.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-11, uncommitted working tree on top of commit 3f4e508 (`git diff -- src/server/http.ts`: 48 changed lines, one constant, one helper, and the loop of `readBytesUpTo`; the fix has no commit id yet, and the re-check must be repeated if the committed code differs from what was read). Node 24.20.0, Vitest 5.0.3.
- The lent test was not weakened. `git show HEAD:tests/security/sec020-bounded-buffer-recheck.test.ts`, with `it.fails(` replaced by `it(` and put through `pnpm exec prettier --stdin-filepath`, differs from the working file only in the header comment line that the replacement itself changed (3 occurrences of `it.fails(` at HEAD, 1 in the working file, the comment). Bodies, bounds (204,800 pulls, 11 pulls), and the 60,000 ms timeout are identical; the only other change is two trailing commas that the formatter removed with the re-wrapping.
- The two reproductions pass as plain tests: `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, 3 files, 308 passed, 0 expected fail, exit 0, on four consecutive runs. The run of 500,000 empty chunks is refused at pull 200,001 (before: read to the end, 500,001 pulls); 400 chunks that report `NaN` are stopped with a 413 at pull 11 (before: 401 pulls).
- Code read. (a) `emptyChunks` is one counter per call, incremented for every chunk whose real length is 0 and never reset; the 200,001st starts the cancellation without awaiting it and throws a `TypeError` whose message is a fixed text. (b) `realByteLength` captures the `byteLength` getter of `%TypedArray%.prototype` once, when the module is loaded, and applies it to the chunk; the result is the engine's own length (0 for a detached or out-of-bounds view), always a non-negative integer, so `NaN` cannot enter the arithmetic. `buffer.set(chunk, total)` is called on the reader's own plain buffer and copies by the same internal length, so the count and the copy agree. Between reading the length and the copy there is no `await`, so nothing can resize or detach the chunk in between. The implementer's new block in `src/server/http.test.ts` (12 test definitions, two of them tables) were read; they are not relied on here.
- 29 new cases in `tests/security/sec020-bounded-buffer-recheck.test.ts` pass. The allowance: the constant is 200,000; for byte limits of 1, 1,000, 102,400, and 1,048,576 a body with exactly 200,000 empty chunks and one byte is read, and an endless run is refused with exactly 200,001 pulls, the source cancelled, and nothing pulled afterwards; the refusal is a `TypeError` that is not an `AppError`, has no cause, becomes a 500 with one log line, carries no marker from bytes read before it, and does not wait for a cancellation that never settles. Not reset: an endless alternation of an empty chunk and one byte under the 1 MiB limit is refused at pull 400,001, under a limit of 1,000 bytes it is a 413 at pull 2,002, and 199,999 + 1 + 1 empty chunks separated by bytes are refused at the last one. The bound on reads: for limits of 1, 5,000, and 102,400 the worst source (every permitted empty chunk, then one byte per chunk) is stopped after exactly the limit plus 200,001 pulls. The real length: subclasses of `Uint8Array` whose `byteLength` and `length` report `NaN`, 0, 1, -1, -0.5, minus infinity, infinity, 2^40, a text, and `undefined` are each stopped by their real bytes at pull 11 against a limit of 10,000, and a 9,000-byte document cut into such chunks comes back unchanged at a limit of exactly 9,000 and is a 413 with one byte more. A subclass that overrides `byteLength`, `length`, `byteOffset`, the iterator, `subarray`, `set`, and `slice` so that each throws is read correctly and none of the seven is called. A chunk with no bytes that reports 1,000 is an empty chunk and counts against the allowance. A detached chunk and a view left out of bounds by a shrunk resizable buffer count as empty; a length-tracking view counts as what it holds.
- Points the implementer asked to have looked at. (1) Alternating empty and one-byte chunks: the count is not reset, shown above. (2) Redirecting the getter: own properties and prototype overrides of the chunk are not consulted (shown above). Replacing the global `Reflect.apply` or the getter on `%TypedArray%.prototype` after the module loaded would need code that already runs inside the server process with full control; the getter is captured at load and is immune, `Reflect.apply` is looked up at call time. Accepted: no boundary is crossed. (3) Errors other than the reader's own `TypeError`: two exist. An object that passes `instanceof Uint8Array` without being a typed array (a `Proxy` of one, or an object created with `Uint8Array.prototype`) makes the built-in getter throw the engine's `TypeError` ("called on incompatible receiver"); it is a 500 and nothing is read after it (2 pulls), but **the source is not cancelled** on that path, unlike every other refusal, and the message is the engine's, not the reader's. And a `Proxy` whose `getPrototypeOf` trap throws makes the `instanceof` check itself throw whatever the trap throws (a `RangeError` in the probe); that predates this fix, and it is the one place where code of a chunk does run. Both fail closed, neither is reachable from the network, and neither bypasses a limit, so they are recorded as an observation and not as a finding; a `try` around the two checks that cancels and throws the reader's own `TypeError` would make every refusal uniform (request to backend-api, optional). (4) `buffer.set` and a subclass that overrides `length`: it copies the real bytes, shown above. (5) The event loop during a permitted run: measured with a source that answers each read without yielding, 200,000 empty chunks take 73 to 87 ms and no timer fires in that time. Accepted, see SR-05 item 9: a body of one-byte chunks at the ceiling holds the loop four times as long (328 to 349 ms) and cannot be refused, so the allowance is not the worst case; listed under residual risks.
- The allowance of 200,000 against the first wording of SR-05 item 9 ("up to the byte limit"): the wording was wrong, not the code; item 9 now states the number and the reason.
Nothing further was found. Not verified: anything through the framework, because no handler exists, in particular whether the framework can hand a route handler an empty chunk at all; and the two reproductions were not re-run against the old code in this re-check (they were recorded as expected failures at commit 9c9158b with bodies shown above to be unchanged).

### SEC-022 A JSON body that is not valid UTF-8 is repaired and accepted
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-12)
Owner: backend-api
Fixed by: the backend-api follow-up in `src/server` of 2026-10-12 (code-review finding CR-03; uncommitted on commit d61099f when it was re-checked)
Location: when found, src/server/http.ts:389 (`new TextDecoder().decode(bytes)`, no `fatal` option; the comment on line 388 said it decodes "like `request.json()` does"). After the fix: src/server/http.ts:496-503
Description: `readJsonBody` decodes the bytes of a body leniently. Each byte sequence that is not valid UTF-8 becomes the replacement character U+FFFD and the result is parsed as if the client had sent it. `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` section 1.1 now says such a body is `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` with `invalid_json` and "is never repaired or read with replacement characters", and marks that as planned; the code does the opposite. What follows from the repair: the schema validates, and a service would store, a text the client did not send; any two invalid sequences become the same character, so two different bodies become one value; and anything in front of the application that reads the raw bytes (a proxy rule, a filter) sees a different request from the one the application acts on. No way to gain anything from it was found: an unknown key stays unknown after the repair, and the decoder does not turn an overlong encoding into the character it imitates. Nothing reads a body yet, hence Low.
Reproduction: by reading, 2026-10-11; not run. Line 389 constructs the decoder with no options, and a decoder constructed that way replaces invalid input instead of throwing. Steps for the re-check: call `readJsonBody` with content type `application/json` and the nine bytes `7B 22 61 22 3A 22 FF 22 7D` (the text `{"a":"?"}` with the single byte FF in place of the question mark). Today it is expected to return an object whose `a` is U+FFFD; the secure behaviour is `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` with `invalid_json`. No test in `tests/security` covers invalid UTF-8.
Remediation: SR-05 item 11. Decode with `fatal: true` and map the decoder's failure to `invalidJsonBody()`, with no cause kept and nothing logged; keep removing a leading byte order mark.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-12, working tree on commit d61099f (`git diff -- src/server/http.ts`). Code read: the bytes are decoded once, as a whole, after the read has ended, with `new TextDecoder("utf-8", { fatal: true, ignoreBOM: false })`; the `catch` has no binding and throws `invalidJsonBody()`, so the decoder's error is neither kept nor passed on. Because the whole buffer is decoded in one call, how the body was split into chunks cannot matter, and a character cut off at the end is an error, not a pending state. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, block "SEC-022 re-check" of `tests/security/sec022-024-json-body-recheck.test.ts` (9 cases, all pass):
- 25 invalid sequences: bytes that never occur (FE, FF), lone and doubled continuation bytes, leads followed by ASCII, overlong forms of NUL, the slash, the quote, the backslash, the opening bracket and brace (two, three, and four bytes long), surrogates written as UTF-8 singly and as a pair, a code point above U+10FFFF, lead bytes above F4, and five- and six-byte forms. Each is refused inside a string value, inside a key, between tokens, at the start, at the end, and as the whole body. For the value case the test first shows that a lenient decoder would have produced a document that parses.
- 5 characters cut off at the end of the body, in three positions each.
- Every one of those 30 again with the body split into chunks of 1, 2, and 3 bytes, with the invalid bytes in a chunk of their own, and with one byte per chunk; a valid Thai body split inside its characters at six chunk sizes still comes back unchanged.
- The answer in every case: `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` with exactly the field error of a body that is not JSON (`path` `""`, `code` `invalid_json`, the fixed message), no `cause`, no U+FFFD and nothing of the body in the response, and no log line at any level.
- Byte order mark: removed once at the start, also when it arrives split over three chunks; kept as a character inside a string; refused when it is the whole body, when it is doubled, at the end, between tokens, or cut short. UTF-16 and UTF-32 bodies, with and without their marks, are refused. A `charset` parameter in the content type (UTF-16, ISO 8859-1, windows-874, UTF-7) selects no other decoder.
- Still accepted and unchanged: U+007F, U+0080, U+07FF, U+0800, U+D7FF, U+E000, U+FFFD sent as its own three bytes, U+FFFF, U+10000, U+10FFFF.
- Order: a body that is too large and not UTF-8 is `413`; one that is too deep and not UTF-8 is the one `400` above.
- Without the control: with `fatal: true` changed to `fatal: false` in `src/server/http.ts`, 7 of the 9 cases failed (347 passed, 7 failed); the file was restored and its SHA-256 compared with the one taken at the start.
Found beside it and registered separately: text that no UTF-8 body can carry still arrives through a `\u` escape (SEC-028). Not verified: anything through the framework, because no handler reads a body.

### SEC-023 The byte limit does not bound how deeply a JSON body nests
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-12)
Owner: backend-api
Fixed by: the same backend-api follow-up of 2026-10-12 (code-review finding CR-04), before any route handler reads a JSON body
Location: when found, src/server/http.ts:368-396 (`readJsonBody` returned whatever `JSON.parse` produced; the only limits were in bytes). After the fix: src/server/http.ts:398 (`MAX_JSON_NESTING_DEPTH`), 417-435 (`nestsDeeperThan`), 504-506 (the call, before `JSON.parse`); src/server/errors.ts:238-253 (`JSON_TOO_DEEP_FIELD_CODE`, `jsonBodyTooDeep`)
Description: A body of 102,400 bytes can nest about 51,200 arrays inside one another, and one at the 1 MiB ceiling about 524,288. The parser accepts both: the SEC-019 re-check recorded that "nesting 524,288 arrays deep at the ceiling parses", and at that time this document counted it as good behaviour, because the alternative it looked at was a stack overflow in the parser. The value is then handed to the caller as it is. Anything that walks it by recursion overflows the stack: a schema that refers to itself, a schema that lets an arbitrary value through followed by a copy or by serialising it for a JSON column. The overflow is a `RangeError`, so a 500, with an error-level line and a stack, for every such request and from any caller allowed to send a body to that endpoint. It is a trap in what the helper hands out, not a present fault: no schema and no handler exists, and a strict schema of fixed depth rejects a deep value at its first unexpected level without descending. Hence Low, as SEC-005 and SEC-018 were.
Reproduction: by reading, and from evidence already in this register (SEC-019, "Envelope" paragraph of the re-check); not run on 2026-10-11. Steps for the re-check: `readJsonBody` with a body of 33 opening and 33 closing square brackets. Today it is expected to return the nested value; the secure behaviour is `400 VALIDATION_ERROR`. Two existing security tests pin today's behaviour and are changed by the security agent together with the fix: `tests/security/sec019-body-limit-recheck.test.ts`, the case at line 339 ("Deep nesting at the ceiling"), and `tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts`, the case at line 1429 (20,000 nested arrays).
Remediation: SR-05 item 12. Refuse a body that nests arrays and objects more than 32 levels deep, in `readJsonBody`, with a check that does not recurse and is linear in the length of the body; `400 VALIDATION_ERROR`, nothing echoed, nothing logged; the field code is written into `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` section 1.1.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-12, working tree on commit d61099f. Code read: `nestsDeeperThan` is one loop over the UTF-16 units of the decoded text with a depth counter and a flag for "inside a string"; it does not recurse and allocates nothing, and it returns at the first bracket that exceeds the limit. Inside a string a backslash skips the next unit, so an escaped quote does not end the string and `[` is five ordinary characters. It runs after the size limit and the decoding and before `JSON.parse`. Why it cannot be passed with a valid document: in valid JSON a quote outside a string always opens one and inside a string is always escaped, so the flag is exact, and the counter is then the true depth; a text for which the flag would be wrong is not valid JSON and is refused by the parser with the same status. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, block "SEC-023 re-check" of `tests/security/sec022-024-json-body-recheck.test.ts` (10 cases, all pass):
- The limit: `MAX_JSON_NESTING_DEPTH` is 32. Depth 32 is returned and depth 33, 34, and 1,000 are refused, for arrays, arrays around a value, objects, and both alternating. The deepest point counts wherever it stands: 200 siblings of depth 32 are accepted, one deeper sibling at the start, the middle, or the end is refused, and so is a deep value under the last of 500 keys.
- The refusal: `400 VALIDATION_ERROR`, exactly one field error with `path` `""`, the fixed message naming 32, and the `code` of the constant `JSON_TOO_DEEP_FIELD_CODE`; no `cause`; nothing of the body in the response; no log line. The value of that constant is provisional (contract item 8.2-12): the tests pin the constant and that it is one fixed lower-case word, not the word.
- Strings: 5,000 brackets or braces in a value or a key, 500 `[` and `{` escapes, escaped quotes between brackets, 999 escaped backslashes before the closing quote, `"` followed by a bracket, and brackets in strings at depth 32 are all accepted and equal what `JSON.parse` gives. A deep value after a string that ends in one or two escaped backslashes, after a string holding an escaped quote, or under a key made of closing brackets is refused: a string neither hides a bracket nor lowers the count. Blank space and line ends between the brackets change nothing, with 50,000 blanks on either side as well.
- 1,500 documents built at random with a known depth of 28 to 36 from the characters the scanner has to tell apart (brackets, braces, quotes, backslashes, `u005b`, Thai), compact and indented: every one of depth 32 or less comes back equal to what was sent, every deeper one is `400`; more than 400 on each side.
- Order: a document that is too deep and also not JSON (33 unclosed brackets, garbage inside, wrong closers, 500 closers first) gets the nesting refusal, so the parser never sees it; a text that is not too deep and not JSON gets the ordinary `invalid_json`. A body over the size limit is `413` whatever its depth, by the declared length and by the running count; at exactly the limit it is read and refused for its depth.
- Size and cost at the 1 MiB ceiling: 524,288 nested arrays, 1,048,576 opening brackets, and 200,000 nested objects are refused; a flat array of 524,287 numbers, 16,000 arrays of depth 31 inside one array, and a string of 1,048,574 brackets are accepted; four megabyte-long strings that never end or are all escapes are `invalid_json`. Six megabyte-long shapes aimed at the scanner each take under 1.5 s for the whole read, scan, and parse (the bound of the test; the times were not recorded one by one, and the whole file of 37 cases ran in about one second), and 20 megabyte-long documents whose 33rd bracket is at the start are refused in under 5 s together.
- Without the control: with the call replaced by a condition that is never true, 10 cases failed, among them the changed cases of the two older files; with the comparison off by one, 8 failed; with the backslash no longer skipped, 2 failed; with strings no longer tracked, 4 failed. The file was restored each time.
The two security tests that pinned the earlier behaviour were changed by the security agent: `tests/security/sec019-body-limit-recheck.test.ts`, "the largest accepted documents do not turn into a server error", which required 524,288 nested arrays to parse and now requires the exact refusal for 524,288, 51,200, and 33 levels, depth 32 accepted at the default limit and at the ceiling, and the largest accepted flat and comb-shaped documents at the ceiling answered without a server error; and `tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts`, the case "a hostile but valid document does not pollute prototypes or crash the parser", which accepted `200` or `400` for 20,000 nested arrays and now requires `400` for 20,000 and for 33, `200` for 32, no cause, and no log line. Neither is weaker than before: each still requires that no such document is a server error, and now fixes which answer it gets. Not verified: anything through the framework.

### SEC-024 A request body that breaks off is logged at error level with a stack
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-12)
Owner: backend-api
Fixed by: the same backend-api follow-up of 2026-10-12 (code-review finding CR-05)
Location: when found, src/server/http.ts:365-366 (the comment of `readJsonBody`: "Any other failure (the body was already read, the connection broke) is passed on unchanged and becomes a 500"), src/server/http.ts:456-460 (`errorResponse` logged every status of 500 or above at error level with the original error). After the fix: src/server/http.ts:355-361 (the read of the stream), 577-583 (the warn line); src/server/errors.ts:263-322 (`ABORTED_BODY_ERROR_CODE`, `readLabel`, `RequestBodyAbortedError`, `requestBodyAborted`)
Description: When the source of a body fails in the middle of a read, `readJsonBody` passes the error on, `errorResponse` turns it into a 500, and, when the handler gave it a logger, writes one error-level line with the stack. Dropping a connection half-way through a body costs a client nothing and can be repeated. On an endpoint with no rate limit, which is most of them, every signed-in account can therefore write error-level lines as fast as it can open connections; on the rate-limited endpoints the limit of step 5 bounds it once the order of SR-05 item 5 is implemented, and under the handler pattern as it is written today (body before identity) an anonymous caller can do it too. Error-level lines are what an operator alerts on, so the effect is alerts that mean nothing and real faults that are harder to see (TM-32), plus log volume. Nothing of the body is written (verified in the SEC-019 re-check), and no request is served wrongly, hence Low. This finding corrects that re-check, which recorded the 500 as correct and left the log line as a residual risk. Whether the framework reports a dropped connection to a handler in this way was not verified, because no handler exists.
Reproduction: by reading, and from evidence already in this register (SEC-019, "Abort and failure": "a body that breaks off mid-read is passed on as the original error (a 500 through `errorResponse`)"); not run on 2026-10-11. Steps for the re-check: a request whose body stream delivers some bytes and then errors, passed through `readJsonBody` and `errorResponse` with a recording logger. Today one `error` call with the original error is expected; the secure behaviour is a status below 500 and at most one `warn` call with no stack. One existing security test pins today's behaviour and is changed by the security agent together with the fix: `tests/security/sec019-body-limit-recheck.test.ts`, the case at line 357 ("a body that breaks off is a server-side error that carries nothing of the body"); the half of it that checks that nothing of the body is kept stays.
Remediation: SR-05 item 10. Catch a failure of the source's read in `readJsonBody` and map it to a client error that `errorResponse` logs at `warn` at most, without a stack and without `clientIp`; leave the reader's own refusals (a chunk that is not bytes, the empty-chunk allowance) as server faults. The upload reader follows the same rule when it is written.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-12, working tree on commit d61099f. Code read: only `reader.read()` is inside the `try`; what it throws is handed to `requestBodyAborted`, which reads two properties of it, `name` and `code`, inside a `try` of its own, keeps each only when it is a non-empty string (cut to 64 characters) or a finite number, and builds a new `RequestBodyAbortedError` with no `cause`. The source's error is referenced by nothing afterwards. `errorResponse` recognises the class by `instanceof` and writes one `warn` call with the code, the status, and the two labels. The reader's own two refusals and the "already read" check are outside that `try` and are still plain `TypeError`s. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, block "SEC-024 re-check" of `tests/security/sec022-024-json-body-recheck.test.ts` (9 plain cases, all pass):
- A source error that carries a marker in its message, its stack, its cause, and six further properties (an address, a body, headers): the error thrown is a `RequestBodyAbortedError`, not the source; its `cause` is undefined; no own property of it, enumerable or not, is the source or its cause; and nothing reachable from it, its string form, or its JSON form contains the marker, the body, or the source's message. Nothing is read after the failure.
- The response: the status of `ABORTED_BODY_ERROR_CODE`, at least 400 and below 500, never `INTERNAL_ERROR`; the error object has the keys `code`, `message`, `details`, and `requestId` only; neither label and nothing of the body or of the source is in it.
- The log: exactly one line, level `warn`, the fixed message "Request body was not received completely", the keys `code`, `status`, `sourceError`, `sourceCode` and no other beside the time, level, message, and request id; no stack, no `clientIp`, no marker.
- Twelve kinds of failure (an `Error` with properties, a `TypeError` with a cause as the HTTP client library throws it, an `AbortError`, a `RangeError`, a string, a number, `null`, `undefined`, a plain object, an object without a prototype, a symbol, a function), each after 0, 1, and 5 chunks: always that class, always below 500, always one `warn` line, never the marker.
- A source error that fights back (getters for `name` and `code` that throw, a `Proxy` whose every read throws, labels that are objects, empty strings, `NaN`, infinity): the same answer, and the `name` getter ran once.
- Labels of 5,000 characters are written as at most 64, and the whole line stays under 600 characters; a `password=` pair, a URL with credentials, a `Bearer` token, and an email inside a label are masked by the logger; a label with a line end and U+2028 does not start a second line.
- The reader's own refusals stay server faults: a chunk that is not bytes, the 200,001st empty chunk, and a body that was already read each give a `TypeError`, a 500 `INTERNAL_ERROR`, and one `error` line. A stream that only closes early is `invalid_json` with no line; a failure after the size limit was passed is never reached (`413`, no line); an error that merely carries the name `RequestBodyAbortedError` is a 500.
- Without the control: with `throw requestBodyAborted(error)` changed to `throw error`, 7 cases failed, among them the changed case of the older file; with the `warn` branch of `errorResponse` disabled, 5 failed. The file was restored each time.
The security test that pinned the earlier behaviour was changed by the security agent: `tests/security/sec019-body-limit-recheck.test.ts`, formerly "a body that breaks off is a server-side error that carries nothing of the body", now "a body that breaks off is a client error below 500 that carries nothing of the body". The half that checks that nothing of the body is kept is unchanged and now also covers the source's message and cause; the other half required a non-`AppError` and a 500 and now requires a `RequestBodyAbortedError`, the status of the constant, at least 400 and below 500, and exactly one `warn` line with no stack.
Answers to what the re-check was asked. The source error is fully dropped. The two labels are not restricted to a known list, and anything that is a string in those two properties is written when it passes the logger's masks: that was SEC-027, Low, fixed in commit b40abf0 and re-checked on 2026-10-12 in the seventh round; until then it was acceptable under SR-04 only because the runtime, not the caller, sets both properties. The status and the code are provisional (contract item 8.2-13); the tests pin the status class and the constant. Remaining and accepted (SR-05 item 10): one `warn` line per broken-off body, so the volume is bounded only where a rate limit is; residual risks. Not verified: whether, and with which error, the framework reports a dropped connection to a route handler; no handler reads a body.

### SEC-025 The placeholder check reads the raw text of DATABASE_URL, not the password the driver will use
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-12)
Fixed by: devops, commit b123ef9
Owner: devops
Found by: code-review finding CR-46, confirmed in the security re-check of SEC-009 on 2026-10-12
Location: when found, src/server/env.ts:202-208 (`databaseUrl.includes(placeholder)`), used at 239 and 284. After the fix (commit b40abf0): src/server/env.ts:207-267 (`containsPlaceholder`, `decodeEscapes`, `hasPlaceholderPassword`, `checkDatabaseUrlForProduction`), called at 298 and 344
Description: The refusal of SEC-009 looks for the two published passwords as substrings of the variable as written. The database driver does not use the variable as written. `pg-connection-string` 2.14.1 percent-decodes the password, prefers a `password` query parameter to the one in front of the host (`index.js` line 45), and `pg` 8.23.1 falls back to the variable `PGPASSWORD` when the URL has no password (`lib/connection-parameters.js`, `val`). So `postgresql://marketplace:local%5Fdev_only_not_a_secret@...`, the same value in `?password=`, and a URL without a password beside `PGPASSWORD=local_dev_only_not_a_secret` all pass the check, and the application then runs in production mode on a password that is published in the repository. What an attacker gains is what SEC-009 describes, a database whose password is public, but only where an operator wrote the password in a form the check does not see. That takes intent or a tool that encodes the URL for them (the underscore needs no encoding, so no tool does it by itself; an encoder that escapes every character would). The mistake SEC-009 is about, a copied `.env.example`, is refused. Hence Low, as code review rated it.
Reproduction: (1) `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, the three `it.fails` cases "SEC-025 ..." of `tests/security/t011-env-health-recheck.test.ts`: six encoded spellings of each password in the authority and in the `password` query parameter, and `PGPASSWORD`, through `parseEnv` and `readDatabaseUrl`. The case in front of them shows that each spelling decodes to the placeholder and does not contain it as written. (2) Against the installed driver's parser, by hand: `parse()` of `pg-connection-string` returns `local_dev_only_not_a_secret` as the password for the encoded authority form, for the encoded query form, and for a query parameter that overrides another password. (3) End to end, 2026-10-12: the production build started with `DATABASE_URL_TEST` empty and `DATABASE_URL='postgresql://marketplace:local%5Fdev_only_not_a_secret@127.0.0.1:5432/marketplace_dev?schema=public'` (`node node_modules/next/dist/bin/next start -p 3197` from Git Bash) reported "Ready", wrote no validation error, and answered `GET /api/health` with 200 `{"status":"ok","database":"up"}`: it was connected to the local development database with the published password, in production mode. The process was stopped afterwards; no node process and no listener on the port remained.
Remediation: judge the password the driver will use, not the text. In `checkDatabaseUrlForProduction`: parse the value with `new URL`; take the password in front of the host and the `password` query parameter, each percent-decoded (`URL.searchParams` decodes the second; a malformed escape in the first is a refusal under the existing rule `databaseUrl`); refuse when either contains a placeholder. Keep the present substring test on the raw text as well, since it also catches the placeholder in places the parser does not name. In `parseEnv` and `readDatabaseUrl`, when `NODE_ENV=production`, also refuse a `PGPASSWORD` that contains a placeholder, reported against the variable `PGPASSWORD` with a fixed rule text and no value. Not asked for: reading a password file (`PGPASSFILE`); the driver itself calls that path deprecated. The fix is complete when the three `it.fails` cases turn red and are changed to `it`, and the case "a real password is not refused for looking like the placeholder in part" still passes.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-12, seventh round, commit b40abf0 (the fix is in b123ef9). The lent test was not weakened: the committed file before the fix with only `it.fails(` replaced by `it(`, put through Prettier, differs from the file of b123ef9 in one header comment line that the replacement itself touched.
- Code read, with the driver beside it (`pg-connection-string` 2.14.1 `index.js`, `pg` 8.23.1 `lib/connection-parameters.js` and `lib/client.js`, `@prisma/adapter-pg` 7.10.0, which hands the string to `pg.Pool` unchanged). The check looks at the whole text, the user name, the password in front of the host, and every `password` query parameter, the last three as the platform's URL parser gives them, each as written and with every well-formed escape decoded by a decoder that cannot throw. `PGPASSWORD` is judged as written, which is how the driver takes it. Every refusal is a variable name and a constant rule text.
- Against the driver itself: a scratch test that loaded the installed `ConnectionParameters` class by path generated 400,000 connection strings from the two placeholders (escapes in upper, lower, and mixed case; escapes encoded twice; tabs, line feeds, and carriage returns before, after, and inside an escape; `+`; `\u005f` and `%u005F`; a blank or a broken escape elsewhere in the string; thirteen positions, among them the query parameter under an encoded or tab-split name, two `password` parameters, an empty password beside `PGPASSWORD`, an empty host, and surrounding blanks). The driver read 38,448 of them as exactly a placeholder; `parseEnv` and `readDatabaseUrl` refused every one. The file imported the driver, which lint forbids outside `src/server/db`, and was deleted; what stays is a model of the driver's reading in `tests/security/round7-recheck.test.ts`, which agreed with the driver on all 400,000.
- `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, block "SEC-025 re-check" of `tests/security/round7-recheck.test.ts` (5 plain cases, all pass): 52 written forms that the model reads as a placeholder are refused through both functions, by name and rule, with no value, host, or piece of the URL in the message, the stack, or the issues; 30,000 generated strings, of which more than 2,000 reach the driver as a placeholder, none accepted; 18 passwords that only resemble a placeholder (encoded twice in front of the host, `+`, `\u005f`, `%u005F`, another letter case, one character short, a part, a full-width underscore, and others) are accepted, as are `PGPASSWORD` values that are not a placeholder; outside production nothing is refused.
- Production build, `node node_modules/next/dist/bin/next start -p 3197` from Git Bash: exit code 1, one error line naming the variable and the rule, and no value in the output, for the local `.env` as it is, for `local%5Fdev_only_not_a_secret`, for `lo%63al_dev_only_not_a_secret` beside a broken escape `%zz` in the query, for a placeholder split by a tab, and for a URL without a password beside `PGPASSWORD` (there the issue names `PGPASSWORD`). No node process and no listener was left.
- Without the control (each change made to `src/server/env.ts`, the block run, the file restored; hashes of every file under `src/server` compared before and after): escapes not decoded, 3 cases fail; the URL's components not looked at, 3; `PGPASSWORD` not looked at, 2.
- **Deviation accepted (security owner, 2026-10-12): a broken percent escape is not a refusal; every well-formed escape is decoded instead.** Devops' reason was that the driver still decodes the well-formed escapes around a broken one. Confirmed against the driver, with a correction of extent: when a string holds a blank or a broken escape, the driver re-encodes it whole (`encodeURI`) and then restores only escapes of two decimal digits (`%63`), so `lo%63al...` beside `%zz` is the placeholder for the driver, while `%5F` beside `%zz` stays the text `%5F`. The check decodes both, which refuses a little more than the driver would read as a placeholder, never less. A decoder that gives up on a broken escape would not have been unsafe either (that change is not noticed by the tests, because the password is also judged as a component by itself, and a component that the driver reads as exactly a placeholder holds no broken escape); the decoder that cannot throw is accepted because it does not depend on that argument.
- Over-refusal, accepted as the safe direction: the placeholder anywhere in the text; `%5F` forms beside a broken escape; a placeholder encoded twice in the `password` query parameter (the URL parser decodes the parameter once, the check once more).
- Not covered, listed under residual risks: a password file (`PGPASSFILE`, `~/.pgpass`; not asked for); the Prisma CLI, the seed, and scripts, which read `DATABASE_URL` without this check; `NEXT_PHASE` (SEC-029). Not run: `PGPASSWORD` against a real server (the refusal happens before any connection).

### SEC-026 Every request to the public health endpoint writes an error-level line while the database is down
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-12)
Fixed by: devops, commit b123ef9
Owner: devops
Found by: code-review finding CR-48, confirmed by a test on 2026-10-12
Location: when found, src/server/health.ts:125 and 133 (one `requestLog.error` per call of `checkHealth`), src/app/api/health/route.ts (public, no rate limit). After the fix (commit b40abf0): src/server/health.ts:126-189 (the log state, `noteFailure`, `noteAnswer`, `noteOutcome`) and 234-238 (a caller that timed out)
Description: Callers that arrive together share one database probe, which bounds the queries. The log line is not shared: each caller gets its own, at error level, with the error's class, code, and up to 300 characters of its message. A probe that fails at once, as a refused connection does, is also started again for the next request. So for as long as the database is down or slow, anyone who can reach `/api/health` writes error-level lines at the rate of their requests, from an endpoint that by design has no session and no rate limit. That is against the rule of SR-05 item 10, "nothing a client can cause at will is logged at `error` level": the client cannot cause the outage, but during one it decides how many lines there are. Effect: log volume and a flood of identical alerts at the moment an operator most needs the log (TM-32). Nothing of a request is written, and the fixed response body says only up or down, hence Low. Seen beside it and accepted: the line carries the first 300 characters of the driver's message, which for a connection failure names the host, the port, or the database user; the logger masks credentials in it (tested), the statement is a constant `SELECT 1`, and those names are the operator's own.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, the `it.fails` case "SEC-026 an outage is logged once, not once for every caller of the public endpoint" of `tests/security/t011-env-health-recheck.test.ts`: 50 calls together and 200 one after another, against a probe that is refused at once and a clock that does not move, write 250 error lines; the case requires at most 2.
Remediation: log the outage, not the request. Write the error line where the probe settles, once per probe, and not more than once in a fixed interval for the same kind of failure (for example 30 seconds, by the injectable clock), with a count of the checks that failed since the last line; write one `info` line when the database answers again. Each caller still gets its 503 and its request id header. The fix is complete when the `it.fails` case turns red and is changed to `it`.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-12, seventh round, commit b40abf0 (the fix is in b123ef9). The lent `it.fails` case was changed to `it` and to nothing else (checked by reformatting, as for SEC-025).
- Code read: a failed probe is noted once where it settles, for all callers still waiting; a caller that timed out is noted by itself; every note adds to a count, and a failure line is written only when none was written in the last 30 seconds of a monotonic clock, with the count and the request id of the first check it reports. A recovery line (`info`) is written only after a failure line. The request id is generated by the route handler; nothing of a request reaches a line. The two response bodies are frozen constants and do not depend on the log state; a failure of the logger is caught on every path.
- `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, block "SEC-026 re-check" of `tests/security/round7-recheck.test.ts` (5 plain cases, all pass): 5,000 checks over eleven minutes of a database that fails and answers at random give at most 25 failure lines, never two within 30 seconds, never a recovery line without a failure line before it, every failed check in exactly one count, and no field outside a fixed list; 300 callers of one hanging probe give one probe and one line, and the other 299 are in the next line's count; a logger whose every method throws changes no answer; 1,000 checks against a database that answers write nothing.
- Without the control (changes to `src/server/health.ts`, restored each time): no interval limit, 3 cases fail; a recovery line without a failure line, 2; timed-out callers not counted, 1.
- What a caller can do: nothing found. A caller cannot fail a probe, so it cannot use up the one line of an interval before a real outage; it cannot add a line (at most one `error` and one `info` line per 30 seconds); the answer and its timing do not depend on what was logged. The first failure after a quiet interval is always written, so a real outage is announced by its first failed check.
- **Deviation accepted (security owner, 2026-10-12): one limit for all reasons, not one per kind of failure.** The remediation said "for the same kind of failure". With one limit the bound is simpler and holds whatever the database does: a database that alternates between timing out and refusing cannot double the lines. What an operator loses: when the reason changes inside an interval (a timeout, then a refused connection), the second reason and its error are not written until the interval ends; the checks are counted, and the next line carries the reason that holds then (tested). And of the request ids of the 503 answers, only the first of each line's count is in the log; the others identify nothing in it. Both are acceptable for an endpoint that has no state per request: the id on a 503 of the health endpoint leads to the outage line, and the line says how many checks failed.
- Observed, not security-relevant, for devops: failed checks that fall inside an interval and are followed by a recovery are reported only by the next failure line, however much later, together with the request id of the first of them; and `describeError` throws for an error whose `message` is not a string, which is caught and leaves that failure uncounted and unwritten (by reading).
- Not run by the security agent: the endpoint against a stopped database (devops' run: 42 requests, one line); the 30 seconds on a real clock.

### SEC-027 The labels logged for a body that broke off are any text the source's error carries
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-12)
Fixed by: backend-api, commit b40abf0
Owner: backend-api
Found by: the security re-check of the SEC-024 fix on 2026-10-12
Location: when found, src/server/errors.ts:266-281 (`readLabel`: any non-empty string, cut to 64 characters), src/server/http.ts:577-583 (written as `sourceError` and `sourceCode`). After the fix (commit b40abf0): src/server/errors.ts:306-309 (the two patterns) and 317-332 (`readLabel`), src/server/http.ts:632-639
Description: SR-05 item 10 allows "the error's class name and code" in the one `warn` line for a body that stops arriving. The code copies whatever strings the properties `name` and `code` of the thrown value hold; nothing checks that they have the form of a class name or of an error code. 64 characters of free text are enough for a phone number, a street address, or a piece of a body, and the logger masks only the shapes it knows (SR-04). No way to choose those two properties from the network was found: with the present runtime they are set by the platform's stream and HTTP code (`TypeError`, `AbortError`, `ECONNRESET`, and the like), and the tests show that a message, a stack, a cause, and every other property are dropped. The flaw is that the helper relies on that instead of enforcing it, and that `requestBodyAborted` is exported for the multipart reader of T-041, where the thrown value will come from a form parser this project has not chosen yet. Hence Low: a rule of SR-04 that holds by circumstance, not by the code.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, the `it.fails` case "SEC-027 a label that is not a class name or an error code is not written" of `tests/security/sec022-024-json-body-recheck.test.ts`: a body stream that fails with an error whose `name` and `code` are a sentence containing a marker, a phone number, and a street name; the `warn` line contains all three.
Remediation: in `readLabel`, keep a string only when it has the form of what it claims to be, and drop it otherwise (the field is then absent, as for an error without a code). For `name`: `^[A-Za-z_$][A-Za-z0-9_$]{0,63}$`. For `code`: `^[A-Za-z0-9_.-]{1,64}$`; a finite number stays written as its digits. No list of known names is needed; the form is enough to keep prose, addresses, and payload out. The fix is complete when the `it.fails` case turns red and is changed to `it`, and the plain cases of the block "SEC-024 re-check" still pass (`Error`, `ECONNRESET`, and `-4077` must still be written).
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-12, seventh round, commit b40abf0. The lent `it.fails` case was changed to `it` and to nothing else (`diff` of the file before the fix with that replacement against the committed file: one header comment line).
- Code read: the two patterns are the ones of the remediation, without the `m` flag, so a line end never passes. The property is read once, inside a `try`; only a primitive string, or for `code` a finite number, is looked at, so no `toString`, `valueOf`, or `Symbol.toPrimitive` of the source's error is run; the length is judged before the pattern.
- `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, block "SEC-027 re-check" of `tests/security/round7-recheck.test.ts` (5 plain cases, all pass): 20,000 generated labels of up to 69 characters from an alphabet with blanks, punctuation, Thai letters, line separators, and surrogate halves, each given as `name` and as `code`: a field is written exactly when the label has the form, and then it is the label itself; 17 hostile texts (prose with an address, a phone number with blanks, an email, a piece of JSON, a class name followed by a line break and a forged log line, 65 and 5,000 characters) write neither field and no marker, in one line; objects with conversion methods, a boxed string, an array, a function, a symbol, a big integer: nothing written and no conversion run; getters read once, throwing getters and a throwing `Proxy` give the same answer; a number is never a name, and a numeric code is written only when its digits have the form (`-4077`, `0.5`; not `1e+21`, `NaN`, infinity); neither label is in the response.
- Without the control (changes to `src/server/errors.ts`, restored each time): the pattern not applied, 3 cases fail. Accepting a number as a name is not noticed by the tests, because no finite number written as digits has the form of a name; the two are the same behaviour.
- **Deviation accepted (security owner, 2026-10-12): a label over 64 characters and a name that is a number are dropped whole.** The earlier code cut a long label to 64 characters; a cut label is a piece of text the form was never judged on as a whole, so dropping is the stricter reading of the remediation. Nothing records that a label was dropped; the line is still written, with the fixed message, the code, and the status.
- What remains, accepted and listed under residual risks: the form admits any single token of its alphabet, so a digits-only code can be a phone number without separators and a dotted one an address. Both properties are set by the runtime today. Condition for T-041 there. The comment above the patterns in `src/server/errors.ts` says the form keeps a phone number out; that holds only for one written with blanks (request to backend-api: correct the comment).

### SEC-028 A string that is not well-formed Unicode reaches the caller through a JSON escape
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-12)
Fixed by: backend-api, commit b40abf0, after the orchestrator ruled that it is refused in `readJsonBody` (remediation (a))
Owner: backend-api (where it is refused is to be agreed with the orchestrator, because it adds a case to the contract)
Found by: the security re-check of the SEC-022 fix on 2026-10-12
Location: when found, src/server/http.ts:507-512 (`JSON.parse(text)` is returned as it is). After the fix (commit b40abf0): src/server/http.ts:453-478 (`holdsStringNotWellFormed`) and 566 (the refusal), src/server/errors.ts:264-281 (`JSON_STRING_NOT_WELL_FORMED_FIELD_CODE`, `jsonStringNotWellFormed`)
Description: SEC-022 closed the door for bytes that are not UTF-8. The same kind of text still comes in through the front: `"\ud83d"` is valid JSON for one half of a surrogate pair, the body that carries it is plain ASCII, and `JSON.parse` returns a string holding that half. Such a string cannot be written as UTF-8. Whatever encodes it later (the database driver, a file, a hash, a search key) writes U+FFFD in its place, so the value that is stored is not the value that was validated, and two different bodies, `"A\ud83dB"` and `"A\ud83eB"`, become one stored value. That is the outcome SR-05 item 11 names as its reason. The same holds for object keys. No way to gain anything from it was found, as for SEC-022: no schema, no service, and no column exists that compares or stores such a value, and whether PostgreSQL would receive U+FFFD or refuse the statement was not tested. Hence Low. It is registered so that it is settled in one place before the first schema, not in each of them.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, block "SEC-028" of `tests/security/sec022-024-json-body-recheck.test.ts`. The plain case shows that the strict decoder accepts the body and that encoding the parsed value gives the bytes `41 EF BF BD 42`. The `it.fails` case sends five bodies (a lone high half, a lone low half, a half inside a key, two high halves, a reversed pair) and requires each to be refused with `400` or not to be handed out with the half in it; all five are returned as they are.
Remediation: one of two, to be chosen by backend-api with the orchestrator and written into `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` section 1.1. (a) In `readJsonBody`: after parsing, walk the value without recursion (it is at most 32 levels deep and at most the size limit) and refuse with `invalid_json` when a string value or a key is not well formed (`String.prototype.isWellFormed`, available in the pinned Node.js). (b) As a rule for every schema: one shared string type that refuses a string that is not well formed, used for every text field, with a test that enumerates the schemas. (a) is the one place and needs no convention, which is why the nesting limit was put there; SR-05 item 11 is extended to say so when the choice is made. A complete pair written as two escapes (`"😀"`) stays accepted.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-12, seventh round, commit b40abf0. The lent `it.fails` case was changed to `it` and to nothing else.
- Code read: after parsing, the value is walked with a list of the arrays and objects still to be looked at; nothing calls itself. Every string value and every key is judged once with `isWellFormed`; a root string is judged before the walk. Keys are read with `Object.keys` on what `JSON.parse` made, so a key `__proto__` is an own property and is looked at, and no code of the value runs. The refusal is `validationError` with a constant message at `path` `""`, with no cause; `errorResponse` logs nothing for it.
- `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, block "SEC-028 re-check" of `tests/security/round7-recheck.test.ts` (4 plain cases, all pass): 28 bodies with a half of a pair (lone high, lone low, reversed, a high half followed by a letter or by an escaped letter, after a raw astral character; at the root, in arrays, as a key, under `__proto__` and `constructor`, as the later of two equal keys, at depth 32 in arrays and objects, after a megabyte of ordinary strings, and as the last of 50,000 keys) are each refused with exactly the documented body, no cause, no log line at any level, and nothing of the body in the response or the stack; 9 well-formed bodies (a pair as two escapes in either letter case, raw, as a key, the last code point, an escaped backslash in front of `ud83d`, a half that the parser discards because the key occurs again) are handed out unchanged; no prototype is changed.
- Cost: 14 documents within 4 KiB of the 1 MiB ceiling (flat arrays of numbers and of empty strings, combs of empty arrays, of empty objects, and of one-string arrays, objects of about 100,000 keys, one string and many strings of escaped pairs, a comb at depth 31, and four with the half at the very end or the very start) are each answered in under 4 seconds; the whole case takes about 0.3 seconds. With one linear search of the pending list added for every child, the case took more than 14 seconds and failed. **Whether the walk recurses cannot be shown by a test through `readJsonBody`**: the nesting limit of SR-05 item 12 is applied before parsing, so the walk never sees more than 32 levels and a recursive walk would behave the same. It was read; it does not recurse; and nothing depends on that while item 12 holds.
- Without the control (changes to `src/server/http.ts`, restored each time): no check, 3 cases fail; keys not judged, 2; the root string not judged, 1; the quadratic walk, 1.
- **Deviation accepted (security owner, 2026-10-12): the field error stands at the root.** A path to the string would repeat keys of the body in the response, and may run through a key that is itself not well formed. The client learns that the body holds such a string and not where; that is enough to correct a client that never should have sent one. SR-05 item 11 is extended to say all of this.
- Not covered: U+0000 and noncharacters are well-formed and are handed out (SR-05 item 11, requirement on the first schema). The field code is provisional (contract item 8.2-12). Not verified: anything through the framework.

### SEC-029 One variable in the server's environment switches the start-up validation off, and nothing else judges DATABASE_URL
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN
Owner: database (the client, code-review finding CR-23) and devops (the hook and `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`)
Found by: the security re-check of the start-up hook on 2026-10-12, seventh round
Location: node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/router-utils/instrumentation-globals.external.js:92 (Next.js 16.4.0: `registerInstrumentation` returns at once when `process.env.NEXT_PHASE === 'phase-production-build'`), src/instrumentation.ts:37 (the same test in the hook), src/server/db/client.ts:47 (the client takes `process.env.DATABASE_URL` as written)
Description: Every refusal of `src/server/env.ts` (SEC-009, SEC-025, `DATABASE_URL_TEST`, the SR-01 rules) runs in one place, the start-up hook. The framework decides whether the hook runs by an environment variable. `next build` sets `NEXT_PHASE` for itself; `next start` does not set or clear it, so a value left in the server's environment (an `ENV` line of an image's build stage, a CI variable, a copied process definition) makes `next start` skip the hook, and the hook's own early return would skip the validation even if it were called. The server then starts in production mode on whatever the environment holds: the published placeholder password, a `DATABASE_URL_TEST`, no `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS`. T-015 puts the scheduled jobs in the same hook, and they would not start either, silently. Nothing an outsider can cause: it takes that one variable in the deployment. Hence Low, as SEC-009 is: a guard against a mistake that one more mistake removes.
Reproduction: (1) Production build, 2026-10-12, from Git Bash with the local `.env` (a copy of `.env.example`): `node node_modules/next/dist/bin/next start -p 3197` exits with code 1 and names `DATABASE_URL` and `DATABASE_URL_TEST`. The same command with `NEXT_PHASE=phase-production-build` in front reports "Ready", writes no validation line, and answers `GET /api/health` with 200 `{"status":"ok","database":"up"}`: connected to the local development database with the published password, in production mode. The same with the encoded placeholder of SEC-025. Stopped afterwards; no node process and no listener left. (2) `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, the `it.fails` case "SEC-029 the database client refuses a placeholder password in production by itself" of `tests/security/round7-recheck.test.ts`: with `NODE_ENV=production` and a placeholder in `DATABASE_URL`, the first use of the database client creates it without complaint.
Remediation: do not let the hook be the only place. (a) database, CR-23: `createClient` in `src/server/db/client.ts` takes its connection string from `readDatabaseUrl()`, so the SEC-009 and SEC-025 refusals hold at the first use of the database whoever started the process (this is what the `it.fails` case pins, and it also covers the seed and the scripts that use the client). (b) backend-api and auth, when the first handler needs configuration: take it from `getEnv()`, which validates on first use, never from `process.env`. (c) devops: say in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` that `NEXT_PHASE` must not be set in a server's environment, and, when T-015 adds the jobs, make their absence visible (the health check or a start-up line that a monitor expects). The early return at `src/instrumentation.ts:37` may stay: the framework makes the same test before it. The fix is complete when the `it.fails` case turns red and is changed to `it`, and the run of step (1) with `NEXT_PHASE` set no longer answers 200 from the placeholder database.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-030 The rate limiter counts per copy of its module, and one process can hold several copies
Severity: Medium
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-12, eighth round; commit f66ab8c)
Owner: backend-api
Found by: backend-api and devops, by reading, after devops measured the two copies with Next.js 16.4.0; confirmed with two copies in a test on 2026-10-12, seventh round
Location: when found, src/server/rate-limit.ts:405 (`export const rateLimiter = createRateLimiter()`: the store and the saturation-log map are created when the module is evaluated), 323-325
Description: Measured by devops with Next.js 16.4.0, in development and in production mode: the start-up hook and the route handlers are compiled separately and each gets its own copy of a module under `src/server`. The limiter keeps its counters in a `MemoryRateLimitStore` that belongs to the copy. Every copy that consumes a rule therefore counts for itself: a subject gets the limit once per copy, the documented bound of 200,000 windows (SR-02) becomes 200,000 per copy, and each copy writes its own saturation warning. For sign-in that is the brute-force limit of TM-01 multiplied by the number of copies that take part: 5 attempts a minute per address become 10 or 15 when the auth library's route, a route handler under `/api/v1`, and the proxy file each consume `sign-in-address`. The factor is the number of copies, a small constant, not a bypass; and nothing consumes a limit today. Hence Medium, a defence-in-depth gap, with a deadline: it must be closed before T-015 wires the sign-in limits, because after that the limit holds only by the accident of which bundles the framework builds. **Not measured by anyone: whether two route handlers, or a route handler and the proxy file, get separate copies.** If every route handler has its own, the factor is the number of routes that consume the same rule.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, the `it.fails` case "SEC-030 a limit used up through one copy of the limiter is used up for every copy" of `tests/security/round7-recheck.test.ts`: the module is loaded twice in one process (`vi.resetModules()`), `sign-in-address` is used up for one subject through the first copy, and the second copy still allows the same subject.
Remediation: one store per process. Keep the `MemoryRateLimitStore` of the process-wide limiter, and the map of saturation-log times, on `globalThis` under a key from the symbol registry, created on first use, as `src/server/db/client.ts` and the logger's level do. Share data only: the store's windows, not functions or classes that are compared with `instanceof` in another copy. `instanceof AppError` is false across copies (tested: an `AppError` made by one copy is a generic 500 for another), so nothing that is shared through `globalThis` may throw an `AppError` for another copy to map; `consume` returns a result today, which is right. The sweep timer of the store must exist once per process too. Before T-015: a test with two copies as above, and one measurement in a running server of which entry points (hook, proxy file, two route handlers) share a copy, recorded in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`. For T-015 itself: a timer started in the hook runs the hook's copies of every module it imports; whatever a job shares with request handlers must come from `globalThis` (the database client already does) or from the database. The fix is complete when the `it.fails` case turns red and is changed to `it`.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-12, eighth round, commit f66ab8c. After the fix: src/server/rate-limit.ts:472 (`PROCESS_STATE_KEY = Symbol.for("marketplace.rateLimit.processState")`), 483-507 (shape check and creation on first use), 510-529 (the process store and `rateLimiter`).
- Test diff: `git show f66ab8c -- tests/security` changes only the `it.fails(` of this case and of SEC-031 to `it(`, with the formatter's line joins; no assertion changed.
- Code read: the slot holds one plain object of built-in `Map`s, plain window objects, and numbers; each copy runs its own functions over it, so no class, function, or `AppError` crosses copies. The windows, the per-rule sizes, the sweep time, and the saturation-log times are all in it, so the window bound of SR-02 (200,000, per rule `maxKeys`), the sweep schedule, and the once-a-minute saturation line hold per process. `createRateLimiter()` still gets a store of its own; only the exported `rateLimiter` is shared, which is what production code must use (nothing calls `createRateLimiter` outside tests today). No timer exists. A slot whose value fails the shape check (not an object, another `version`, a missing `Map`, a non-number sweep time) is replaced by an empty table; reading or replacing a slot that throws makes `consume` reject, inside the promise.
- `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, file `tests/security/sec030-031-recheck.test.ts`, block "SEC-030 re-check" (9 plain cases, all pass): two copies together hold at most `maxKeys` windows of a rule, the next new subject is refused as saturated by either copy, and one saturation line is written for both; one subject hit 5,000 times holds one window, a 1,000,000-character subject and a subject in digest form are stored under keys of at most rule name plus 128 characters, and only registered rule names reach `ruleSizes` and the saturation map (unbounded growth: none found); a rule named `__proto__` or `constructor`, or a known name with other settings, is refused before the table is touched; a non-writable slot with junk, and a frozen table when the sweep falls due, make `consume` reject every time, never allow; a sweep time of `NaN` stops the sweep and a full rule stays refused (fails closed); the saturation line at level `error` has exactly the keys `level`, `message`, `rule`, `ruleSize`, `storeSize`, `timestamp`, and no part of a subject that held an email and a newline.
- Against the code before the fix (`git show f66ab8c^:src/server/rate-limit.ts` and `logger.ts` into a scratch folder, the test pointed at them, folder deleted afterwards): the shared-capacity case fails, as do the others that read the table.
- **Accepted, recorded under residual risks (security owner, 2026-10-12):** (1) a well-formed table that other code in the process puts into the slot is trusted as it is, and (2) a slot that fails the shape check, for example after a `version` change, is replaced and the counters it held are lost, which resets every limit once. Both need code running in the server process, which can do more than that anyway; a client cannot reach the slot (a parsed body has no symbol keys). Both cases are pinned as plain tests so that a change is noticed. If two builds with different `PROCESS_STATE_VERSION` ever share a process (not possible with one build), they would replace each other's table on every hit.
- Not verified: that the entry points of a running server (start-up hook, proxy file, route handlers) are separate copies sharing this one table, and that the proxy file runs in the same JavaScript realm (a separate realm has its own `globalThis` and so its own table, and the limit would again be multiplied). That is the module-copy measurement still due before T-015 (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`).

### SEC-031 LOG_LEVEL=error removes log lines that security rules require
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-12, eighth round; commit f66ab8c)
Owner: backend-api (the logger and the limiter), with devops (`src/server/env.ts`)
Found by: the security re-check of the process-wide log level on 2026-10-12, seventh round
Location: when found, src/server/logger.ts:833 (every line below the configured level is dropped), src/server/rate-limit.ts:353 (the saturation warning is a `warn` line of the process-wide logger), src/server/env.ts:172-174 (`LOG_LEVEL=error` is accepted in every environment) and 408 (it is applied to the process)
Description: Until commit b40abf0 the validated `LOG_LEVEL` reached nothing. Now it reaches every logger of the process, as intended, and `error` is an accepted value in production. At that level every `warn` and `info` line is dropped. SR-02 item 4 requires the saturation warning "so operators can alert on it": it is the only sign that a rate-limit rule is refusing new subjects. SR-04 lists the security events (a rate-limit refusal, a failed or successful sign-in, a sign-up, an `Origin` check failure, an admin action); none exists yet, and none can be written at `error`, because nothing a client can cause at will is logged at that level (SR-05 item 10). So one setting, valid by every rule of the configuration, leaves an attack on sign-in without a line in the log. Nothing a client can set; it is the operator's own choice, made for quieter logs. Hence Low. The SR-01 start-up warning is not affected: devops applies the level after it.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, the `it.fails` case "SEC-031 the saturation warning of SR-02 is written at every configured level" of `tests/security/round7-recheck.test.ts`: with the process level at `error`, a limiter on the process-wide logger refuses a subject as saturated and writes nothing. The plain case in front of it records what else the level hides: the line for a body that broke off and the health check's recovery line (both allowed to be hidden), while the outage line is written.
Remediation: the rule added to SR-04 on 2026-10-12, "Lines that a security rule requires are not subject to `LOG_LEVEL`". Either a method of the logger that the level does not filter, used for the saturation warning, the SR-01 warning, and every security event; or a refusal of `LOG_LEVEL=error` when `NODE_ENV=production` together with writing every such line at `warn` (then the `it.fails` case is rewritten by the security agent to state that form). The first is preferred. Due before T-015 writes the first sign-in event. The fix is complete when the `it.fails` case turns red and is changed to `it`.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-12, eighth round, commit f66ab8c. After the fix: src/server/logger.ts:67-88 (`SecurityLogLevel`, `StructuredLogger.security`), 853-855 and 867-868 (the level is skipped only when `filtered` is false), 900-901 (`security`), src/server/rate-limit.ts:403-407 (the saturation warning through `security("warn", ...)`).
- Test diff: as SEC-030; only `it.fails(` to `it(`.
- Code read: `security` calls the one `emit` of the logger with `filtered = false`, so redaction, string scrubbing, escaping, the entry budget, and the never-throw guards are the same code as for every line. Fields are spread first and `level`, `timestamp`, `message`, and a bound `requestId` are set after them. A level other than `info`, `warn`, `error` becomes `warn`. Children share the method. The limiter uses `security` when the logger has it and falls back to `warn` for a logger with `warn` only (a test stub); the default and every `createLogger` logger have it.
- `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`, file `tests/security/sec030-031-recheck.test.ts`, block "SEC-031 re-check" (16 plain cases, all pass): at each of the four configured levels `security()` writes `info`, `warn`, and `error` and a child's line, while `debug` to `error` stay filtered as before; the process-wide logger and its child write `security()` lines with the process level at `error` and drop `warn`; seven non-levels (`debug`, `fatal`, `__proto__`, `toString`, `undefined`, `7`, `null`) are written as `warn`; fields named `level`, `message`, `timestamp`, `requestId` do not replace the real ones on a child with a request id; LF, CR, U+2028, U+2029, U+202E, U+0085, U+009B, U+200B, U+2066 in the message, a field, and an array produce one line with none of them raw, and parse back to the original; an email, a URL password, a `Bearer` token, `password=`, `password`, `sessionToken`, `cookie`, `authorization`, `headers`, `body`, `name`, and `ip` are masked exactly as on a `warn` line of the same input (the two lines are equal apart from `timestamp`), while `clientIp` and `userId` are written; a circular value, a `BigInt`, a throwing getter, a message whose `toString` throws, and a throwing sink do not make it throw.
- Against the code before the fix: every case of the block fails (`security` does not exist).
- SR-04 now names `security()` and the rules for its callers.
