The SR-01 start-up warning in `src/server/env.ts:403` still uses `warn`; it is written before the level is applied and is therefore not lost today, but it should move to `security()` (handed to devops; not a finding while the order holds).
- Not verified: anything through a running server; the volume of `security()` lines once T-015 writes per-request events (residual risks).

Findings SEC-032 to SEC-036 come from the review of T-015 and T-016 on 2026-10-13. Their full text (description, reproduction, remediation) is in [reviews/T-015-T-016-security.md](reviews/T-015-T-016-security.md); the reproductions are in `tests/security/t015-t016-auth.itest.ts`, which runs with `pnpm test:integration` because it needs the test database. Each `it.fails` case there was shown to fail on its flaw assertion when changed to `it`.

### SEC-032 One address can keep any account, an admin's included, from signing in
Severity: Medium
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-14 at unit and route level; the integration cases with a real sign-in ran on 2026-10-15 and pass, see "Integration run" at the end of this entry). The SR-02 capacity split is not accepted as final: see SEC-050
Owner: auth (decision with the security owner); fix built by backend-api
Location: src/server/auth/routes.ts:214; src/server/rate-limit.ts:150 (before the fix); after the fix `RATE_LIMITS.SIGN_IN_EMAIL` and `RATE_LIMITS.SIGN_IN_ACCOUNT` in src/server/rate-limit.ts, `signInEmailChecks` in src/server/sign-in-rate-limit.ts, the loop after `parseInput(signInBodySchema, ...)` in `handleSignIn` (src/server/auth/routes.ts)
Description: `sign-in-email` is keyed by the email alone and used up by every attempt. Five wrong passwords a minute from one address keep the owner, from any address and with the right password, at 429. Costs the attacker nothing; availability only.
Reproduction: `it.fails` "after five wrong passwords from one address, the owner signs in from another address" in `tests/security/t015-t016-auth.itest.ts`.
Remediation: strict limit per email and address together, plus a looser per-email limit over all addresses (a change of the SR-02 split); or accept with a written reason and correct the residual-risk sentence on per-email lock-out.
Owner decision (D-036 item 5): fix, do not accept. Proposal by auth (2026-10-13), NOT BUILT: it needs a new rule in `src/server/rate-limit.ts` (backend-api) and a new SR-02 capacity split (security), neither of which auth may change.
1. `sign-in-email` keeps its name and limit (5 per 60 s) but its subject becomes `<client address>|<normalised email>`. An address's failures then use only its own allowance for that account.
2. New rule `sign-in-account`, subject the normalised email, **60 per 300 s**, consumed only after rule 1 allowed the request and still before the password. Why these numbers: one address reaches rule 2 at most 5 times per 60 s (rules 1 and `sign-in-address`), and at most 6 such windows overlap one 300 s window, so one address can add at most 30 hits to a window of 60 and **can never exhaust it**: the owner's attempt is at most the 31st. It takes at least two addresses at full rate all the time (a limit of 90 would require three, at 1,080 guesses an hour). Distributed guessing on one account stays bounded at 720 an hour (today 300).
3. Capacity (SR-02, for security to rule): `sign-in-account` needs its own share; one key per email tried per 5 minutes. Suggested: `sign-in-email` 30,000, `sign-in-account` 10,000, the rest unchanged (total 200,000). The 5-minute window makes saturation of `sign-in-account` five times cheaper than of a 1-minute rule; security weighs that.
4. Order and answer unchanged: a `429` still does not say which limit refused; both per-email subjects are consumed only after the per-address check (SR-01).
5. Tests when built: the `it.fails` above flips to `it`; the case "pins the cost" (expects 429) changes to expect 200 for the owner and 429 only after two addresses; new cases: rule 2 is not exhausted by one address over 10 simulated minutes, and is by two.
Residual after the fix: two or more addresses (or one IPv6 /48, SR-02) can still keep an account from signing in from new places; an attacker on the owner's own address (carrier-grade NAT) shares the owner's rule-1 allowance. A per-device allowance for browsers that have signed in before (an OWASP "device cookie") would close the first; not proposed for the MVP. (Corrected by the fix below: as built, it takes three addresses.)
Fix (backend-api, 2026-10-13, commit 9f039fb), items 1 to 4 as proposed: `RATE_LIMITS.SIGN_IN_EMAIL` keeps 5 per 60 s with `maxKeys` 30,000 and is consumed on `signInEmailSubject(clientIp, email)` = `<client address>|<normalised email>` (an SR-01 address never holds `|`; an empty address or one holding `|` throws). New `RATE_LIMITS.SIGN_IN_ACCOUNT` (`sign-in-account`, 60 per 300 s, `maxKeys` 10,000) on the normalised email. `handleSignIn` consumes, in order, `sign-in-address` (before the body), then the two checks of `signInEmailChecks` (src/server/sign-in-rate-limit.ts), each only after the one before allowed, all before the password; only the exported `rateLimiter` (through the runtime) is used, and the `rate-limited` security event is written only on a refusal, as before; a refusal by either per-email rule happens only after `sign-in-address` allowed the request, so those events stay behind the per-address limit. The 429 body is unchanged; its `Retry-After` is capped at 60 s for every sign-in refusal (`signInRetryAfterSeconds`), because a value above 60 could only come from `sign-in-account` and would tell which limit refused (item 4); a client that retries early is refused again. SR-02 table updated; the split is not ruled. `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` section 6 updated.
Correction to item 2 (found while building the tests): with fixed windows one address adds at most **29** hits to one `sign-in-account` window, not 30: at most six of its 60 s windows overlap a 300 s window, and if six do, the first began with a hit before the account window did. So two addresses add at most 58 and the owner's attempt is at most the 59th: **two addresses cannot exhaust the rule; it takes three** (or 12 addresses at an ordinary pace, 5 a minute each, inside one window). Item 5's "and is by two" is therefore tested as "not by two, by a third". The bound on distributed guessing is unchanged (60 per 300 s, 720 an hour). Not changed: the numbers (60, 300 s); the owner or security may lower the limit to 55 or fewer if "two addresses" is the intended threshold.
Tests: `tests/security/t015-t016-auth.itest.ts` describe "SEC-032 ...": "after five wrong passwords from one address, the owner signs in from another address" (`it.fails` flipped to `it`, body unchanged); "pins the cost: five requests a minute from one address no longer keep an admin out" (was the 429 case: now the attacker's 6th is 429, the owner's sign-in is 200 with one session); "sign-in-account is not exhausted by one address over 10 simulated minutes" (one address at its strongest timing, 31 password checks over minutes 0 to 10, owner 200 at 599 s); "two addresses at their strongest timing do not exhaust sign-in-account; a third does" (owner 200 after 58, 429 with `Retry-After` at most 60 after a third address's 60th, 200 again at 600 s). `tests/integration/auth/limits-and-cookies.test.ts` "sign-in: the per-account limit holds across addresses, an unknown email is counted the same way, and its 429 does not say which limit refused" replaces the case that asserted the old per-email limit across five addresses. Unit: `src/server/sign-in-rate-limit.test.ts` (11 cases: subject form, order, one address over 10 simulated minutes, 29 per window at the strongest timing, 58 for two, exhausted by a third and by 12 at an ordinary pace, Retry-After cap); `src/server/rate-limit.test.ts` (limits table, capacity split adds to 200,000); `tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts` re-check of SR-02 isolation now floods `sign-in-account` too (anonymous total still 120,000). With `signInEmailChecks` changed back to the old single email-only check, 7 of the 11 unit cases fail. The integration cases were **not run** (Docker was not running on 2026-10-13); against the old code each new or changed integration case fails by reading (the owner's attempt is the 6th in the old email-only window).
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-14; report [reviews/SEC-032-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-032-recheck.md)): `pnpm exec vitest run src/server/sign-in-rate-limit.test.ts src/server/rate-limit.test.ts tests/security/t019-server-utilities.test.ts`: 3 files, 287 passed. New `tests/security/sec032-recheck.test.ts` runs `handleSignIn` with the real limiter, SR-01 with one trusted hop, and a stub library (401 = reached the password check): 7 passed. Covered: after one address is refused, the owner from another address still reaches the password check; one address at full rate for 10 simulated minutes never refuses the owner; email spellings (case, ASCII space, U+00A0, U+3000) share one `sign-in-email` and one `sign-in-account` counter and name one account in the library (better-auth 1.7.7 lower-cases the email itself; the schema refuses full-width letters, U+212A, U+0130, U+200B); client-written `X-Forwarded-For` entries left of the trusted hop give no new address; plus-addressing is another account by design; a full `sign-in-account` rule keeps the owner's live counter through a sweep and refuses a new email with `Retry-After` of at most 60. ESLint and `pnpm typecheck` exit 0. **Not run:** `pnpm test:integration` (Docker down), so the flipped case and the three new or changed SEC-032 integration cases have never run; no mutation run of the new file against the old code (`src/` is not security's to change). Rulings: the arithmetic (29 per address, 58 for two, a third fills the window) is correct for the owner's **first** attempt in a window; the owner's own attempts count in the same window, so against two addresses at their strongest timing the owner's third attempt in that window is refused. The 60 s `Retry-After` cap is accepted under item 4, not SR-01. It conceals little, because a 429 to a fresh address's first request can only come from `sign-in-account` or saturation, and it weakens nothing, because the limiter state is unchanged. The `t019` edit floods one more rule, keeps every assertion, and is not a weakening.
Integration run (security, 2026-10-15, during the T-040 review; replaces the "not run" notes above): Docker up. `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.integration.config.mts tests/security/t015-t016-auth.itest.ts tests/integration/auth/limits-and-cookies.test.ts -t "SEC-032|per-account limit"`: 2 files, 5 passed (the four SEC-032 cases, the flipped one included as plain `it`, and the per-account case), 32 skipped, exit 0. The full `pnpm test:integration` the same day: 38 files, 1072 passed, 2 expected fail (SEC-053, SEC-058), exit 0. The auth agent's run of 2026-10-14 had already passed (37 files, 1062 passed, 1 expected fail). Still not run: a mutation of `signInEmailChecks` against the integration cases.

### SEC-033 Sign-up display name accepts bidirectional control characters
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-14). Schema part re-checked 2026-10-13. Rendering part re-checked 2026-10-14 at commit 4fa4352 for store names, the display name, address fields, and the search query ([reviews/SEC-033-rendering-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-033-rendering-recheck.md)). Product titles are not isolated and are registered as SEC-057
Owner: auth (schema), frontend (rendering)
Location: src/modules/auth/schema.ts:21, 26 before the fix; the rule is now `NAME_FORBIDDEN_CHAR` (schema.ts:33, General_Category `Cf` holds all twelve characters), `NAME_ALLOWED_TEXT` (schema.ts:47-50), applied to the sign-up name at schema.ts:55 (lines as of commit 20ee58d)
Description: U+061C, U+200E, U+200F, U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069 are accepted in names that sellers and admins will see; impersonation by reordered display.
Reproduction: six `it.fails` cases "a name with U+... is 400 VALIDATION_ERROR and creates nothing".
Remediation: refuse those characters in every name field; keep U+200B and U+200C (used in Thai); render names isolated (`<bdi>`).
Fix (auth, 2026-10-13): `NAME_ALLOWED_TEXT` in `src/modules/auth/schema.ts` refuses C0, C1, and the bidirectional formatting characters listed above; U+200B and U+200C stay allowed. The sign-up name and the bootstrap admin name use it; later name fields are to reuse it. The six security cases are now `it` and pass; unit cases in `src/modules/auth/schema.test.ts` cover all twelve code points and the two Thai zero-width characters.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-13, [reviews/SEC-033-035-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-033-035-recheck.md)): `tests/security/sec033-name-recheck.test.ts`, run by `pnpm test` (49 files, 1956 passed, 14 expected fail, exit 0). The refused set equals the whole Unicode `Bidi_Control` property as enumerated by the engine over every scalar value (exactly the twelve code points); each is refused at the start, middle, end, alone, and padded, after trimming (U+00A0, U+FEFF, U+3000 around the name), and among Thai text with U+200B and U+200C; the bootstrap name refuses all twelve without echoing the value; no NFC, NFD, NFKC, NFKD form or case mapping of any accepted scalar produces a `Bidi_Control` character; the pattern is anchored and has the `u` flag; Thai, emoji ZWJ sequences, Arabic, and Hebrew names stay accepted. The only name entry points are the sign-up route and the bootstrap (`git grep signUpBodySchema`; the library's update-user route is not mounted). The six cases of `t015-t016-auth.itest.ts` were flipped from `it.fails` to `it` with the bodies unchanged (`git diff 15b9166 HEAD -- tests/security/t015-t016-auth.itest.ts`: two lines, `it.fails(` to `it(`) and pass. Other invisible and layout characters are still accepted: registered separately as SEC-037.
Rendering part, re-check (security, 2026-10-13, [reviews/T-020-T-018-security.md](reviews/T-020-T-018-security.md)): frontend added `src/components/user-name.tsx` in commit 54a070a. `UserName` renders its children inside one `<bdi>`, and React escapes the text: `tests/security/t020-t018-catalog-shell.test.ts` renders a name holding U+202E, U+2069, and `<img ... onerror=...>` with `renderToStaticMarkup` and gets exactly `<bdi>`, the escaped text, `</bdi>`. The component is correct. It has no caller yet: no page renders a user name, a store name, or an address name (T-018 renders only `th.site.name`), and nothing forces its use (no lint rule, no test that enumerates name fields). `fill()` (`src/i18n/fill.ts`) returns a string, so a name placed into a Thai sentence through a `{name}` placeholder cannot be isolated by `UserName`; today no template in `th.ts` has a placeholder for user text. Status of the rendering part stays OPEN until the first screen that shows a user-typed name (the store pages of T-021 are the first, through the catalog's `store.name`) renders it through `UserName`, with a test; a sentence that embeds a name must be built from parts (text, `<UserName>`, text), not through `fill()`.
Rendering part, second re-check (security, 2026-10-14, [reviews/SEC-033-rendering-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-033-rendering-recheck.md)): `tests/security/sec033-rendering-recheck.test.ts`, run with `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec033-rendering-recheck.test.ts`: 48 passed and 3 expected fail (SEC-057). The file renders `ProductCard` and `ProductDetailView` with `renderToStaticMarkup`, using a store name that holds U+202E and markup. In each, the name is one text node, escaped, directly inside one `<bdi>`. The store page `h1` (`store-section.tsx:50`) has `<UserName>{store.name}</UserName>` and has no other `{store.name}`. The search page's async content runs against a `q` holding U+202E and `<script>`, and its heading renders the Thai prefix, then a `<bdi>` that holds exactly the escaped `q`, then the suffix (`src/components/catalog/search-heading.tsx`, commit 4fa4352, CR-T021-07). A static scan covers every `.tsx` under `src/app/(storefront)` and `src/components` (except `ui/` and tests): each `<UserName>` holds one plain member expression, with no `fill()`, template, or literal text. The account pages render the display name, the recipient, and each address line alone in `<UserName>`. The email shown without isolation cannot hold a control, because `z.email()` refuses seven bidi and invisible controls in the local part and in the domain. Reviews read and show no author (`REVIEW_SELECT`). Against the search page of 506646b, a scratch copy with `it.fails` flipped failed both search cases on their flaw assertion: the `<bdi>` held `ผลการค้นหา “<q>”`, not `<q>`. The static scan also rejects that page's `<UserName>{fill(...)}</UserName>`. Not isolated, by design: the recipient name inside `aria-label` (an attribute; the schema refuses controls there); descriptions and review comments (free text, each the only content of its own `<p>`, which is a bidi paragraph boundary); category names (admin-written; isolation recommended when CR-T021-05 shows them in a heading). Still open: product titles (SEC-057). `searchTextSchema` does not refuse bidi controls in `q`. The isolation contains them, and refusing them is optional hardening.

### SEC-034 The production session cookie uses the `__Secure-` prefix, not `__Host-`
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-13). Re-check verdict: FIXED; a parser quirk of the library that could reopen it under an unverified browser behaviour is registered as SEC-038
Owner: auth
Location: src/server/auth/auth.ts:127-130; src/server/auth/cookies.ts:13-19
Description: a host under the same registrable domain can set the session cookie for the shop (login CSRF into an attacker's account). Deployment-dependent.
Reproduction: `it.fails` "the production session cookie carries the __Host- prefix".
Remediation: `__Host-` name in production (needs `useSecureCookies: false` with an explicit name and `secure: true` attribute in better-auth 1.7.7); or record in DEPLOYMENT.md that no other host under the domain is operated by anyone else.
Fix (auth, 2026-10-13): in production `advanced.useSecureCookies: false`, `advanced.cookiePrefix: "__Host-better-auth"`, `advanced.defaultCookieAttributes: { secure: true, path: "/" }` (a prefix rather than a per-cookie name, so the cookie-cache and "don't remember" cookies the library expires at sign-out are `__Host-` and `Secure` too). `SESSION_COOKIE_NAMES` in `cookies.ts` lists `__Host-better-auth.session_token` and the development name; `__Secure-better-auth.session_token` no longer counts for the proxy's presence check. The security case is now `it` and passes; the production-mode cookie test in `tests/integration/auth/limits-and-cookies.test.ts` asserts the `__Host-` name and checks every `Set-Cookie` line of the sign-out answer for `__Host-`, `Secure`, `Path=/`, and no `Domain`. No deployment existed, so no live session was invalidated by the rename.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-13, [reviews/SEC-033-035-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-033-035-recheck.md)): `tests/security/sec034-035-recheck.itest.ts`, run by `pnpm test:integration` (22 files, 831 passed, 5 expected fail, exit 0). Every `Set-Cookie` line of a production sign-in (exactly one, `__Host-better-auth.session_token`) and of a production sign-out is `__Host-`, `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/`, no `Domain`. Sign-up and the session route forward no `Set-Cookie`. The valid signed token under `__Secure-`, the development name, case variants (`__host-`, `__HOST-`, `Better-Auth`), percent-encoded spellings, and a `.0` suffix is not a session; a cookie planted under a non-`__Host-` name, sent first, does not displace the real one. Read: better-call 1.4.0 `_serialize` forces `Secure`, `Path=/` and drops `Domain` for a `__Host-` name in any case. The old case of `t015-t016-auth.itest.ts` was flipped to `it` with its body unchanged and passes; its `every` check would also pass on an empty list, which the new file's exact-name assertion closes.

### SEC-035 Response time tells a registered email from an unknown one
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-13). Re-check verdict: FIXED while the library's work stays under the floor (see residual risks)
Owner: auth
Location: better-auth 1.7.7 `dist/api/routes/sign-in.mjs:318-337` and `sign-up.mjs:192-240`, called from src/server/auth/routes.ts:219 and 171
Description: loopback medians, 2026-10-13: sign-in unknown email 60.1 ms, wrong password 61.9 ms (60 each, interleaved); sign-up new 66.9 ms, duplicate 63.8 ms (30 each).
Reproduction: measurement steps in the review file (a scratch test, deleted after the run; a timing test is not kept in the suite).
Remediation: a fixed minimum response time for failed sign-in and for every sign-up answer, applied after the rate-limit checks; re-measure.
Fix (auth, 2026-10-13): `AUTH_RESPONSE_FLOOR` in `src/server/auth/config.ts` (250 ms plus a random 0 to 25 ms from `node:crypto` `randomInt`), applied in `src/server/auth/routes.ts` to every 401 of sign-in (unknown email, wrong password, deactivated account) and to every 200 of sign-up (new, duplicate, concurrent duplicate), measured from the start of the password step, after the origin, content type, rate limits, body, and schema, so those refusals stay cheap. A successful sign-in is not padded (its answer differs anyway). When the library's work takes longer than the floor nothing is added (overload brings the difference back; the limits bound the samples). Tests: `tests/integration/auth/sign-up-sign-in.test.ts`, "response floor (SEC-035, TM-03)" (lower bounds only; no timing comparison is kept in the suite). Re-measured with the review's steps on 2026-10-13 (scratch file deleted), production floor, loopback: sign-in unknown email median 270.5 ms (min 254.5, max 289.2, n 30), wrong password median 270.4 ms (min 255.5, max 286.8, n 30); sign-up new median 273.2 ms (n 15), duplicate median 268.9 ms (n 15).
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-13, [reviews/SEC-033-035-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-033-035-recheck.md)): `tests/security/sec034-035-recheck.itest.ts`: eight sign-in states (unknown email; wrong password; wrong password with the email in capitals and padded; deactivated with the right and with a wrong password; a user without a credential account; a credential account without a password; a wrong password sent with another user's valid session cookie) all answer 401 with one body, no cookie, and at least the production floor; sign-up for a new email, a duplicate, a duplicate in capitals, and a padded duplicate answer with the same headers and body after at least the floor; the per-email 429 is identical for a registered and an unknown email and does not wait (it does not depend on the account). Independent measurement (scratch file, deleted), production floor, loopback, interleaved: sign-in unknown median 271.6 ms (min 254.1, max 291.6, n 30), wrong password 271.2 (253.1 to 287.0, n 30), deactivated 272.1 (253.8 to 286.2, n 30); sign-up new 269.5 (251.7 to 288.8, n 15), duplicate 272.4 (255.4 to 289.6, n 15). Read: every 401 path of `handleSignIn` (library 401, and the deactivated-after-hook path) waits; `startedAt` is taken before `readSession`; the refusals before the floor (origin, content type, rate limits, body, schema) do not read the account. Not padded: a successful sign-in (needs the password) and a non-401 library error (a 500, not caused by the email alone).

### SEC-037 Names accept line separators, other invisible format characters, and blank-looking names
Severity: Low
Status: schema part FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-13); rendering part (frontend, `<bdi>`) tracked under SEC-033. Re-check verdict: FIXED for every character and category the finding and its remediation named; characters outside those categories (unassigned, private use, default-ignorable marks, variation selectors) registered as SEC-039, and literal invisible characters in the rule's source as SEC-040
Owner: auth (schema); frontend (rendering)
Location: src/modules/auth/schema.ts:35-42 (`NAME_FORBIDDEN_RANGES`, before the fix); after the fix schema.ts:33 (`NAME_FORBIDDEN_CHAR`), :40 (`NAME_VISIBLE_CHAR`), :47-50 (`NAME_ALLOWED_TEXT`)
Description: the name rule refuses C0, C1, and the twelve `Bidi_Control` characters only. Still accepted: U+2028 and U+2029 (mandatory line breaks, so a name can show as two lines, for example "Admin" over "Verified seller"); U+FEFF, U+2060 to U+2064, U+206A to U+206F, U+FFF9 to U+FFFB, U+180E, U+00AD, and the tag characters U+E0001 and U+E0020 to U+E007F (invisible; tags can carry hidden ASCII text); and names that look blank: U+200B alone, U+3164, U+115F U+1160, U+2800, soft hyphens alone. An attacker gains look-alike or blank display names in seller and admin screens, and hidden text in a field others read. No layout is reordered, so this is weaker than SEC-033.
Reproduction: 14 `it.fails` cases in `tests/security/sec033-name-recheck.test.ts`, describe "SEC-037 ...".
Remediation: refuse General_Category `Cf` except U+200B, U+200C, U+200D (Thai and emoji sequences), and `Zl` and `Zp`; require at least one character that is not `Cf`, `Z*`, U+115F, U+1160, U+3164, U+FFA0, or U+2800 (a name that is visible). Reuse the rule for every later name field (store, address). Render names isolated (`<bdi>`), as for SEC-033.
Fix (auth, 2026-10-13, commit 20ee58d): `NAME_ALLOWED_TEXT` in `src/modules/auth/schema.ts` refuses, anywhere in a name, General_Category `Cc`, `Cf` except U+200B, U+200C, U+200D (Thai zero-width characters kept as decided for SEC-033; U+200D for emoji sequences), `Zl`, `Zp`, and `Cs` (lone surrogates), and requires at least one character of `L`, `N`, `P`, or `S` that is not U+115F, U+1160, U+3164, U+FFA0, or U+2800. So a name of only combining marks (for example a Thai vowel sign alone) is refused too. `NAME_FORBIDDEN_RANGES` was removed (no other user). The pattern is written with escapes only and keeps the `u` flag and `^...$` anchors. Tests: the 14 SEC-037 cases in `tests/security/sec033-name-recheck.test.ts` are now `it` with unchanged bodies and pass; `src/modules/auth/schema.test.ts` adds an enumeration of every `Cf`, `Zl`, `Zp` scalar value (all refused but U+200B to U+200D), lone surrogates, nine blank-looking names, and seven short visible names that stay accepted. With the fix taken out (old `schema.ts`), those 14 cases and 3 of the new unit cases fail. Commands: `pnpm test` exit 0 (51 files, 1990 passed, 0 expected fail).
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-13, [reviews/SEC-037-038-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-037-038-recheck.md)): `git diff 6cb51c9 HEAD -- tests/security/sec033-name-recheck.test.ts` shows the 14 SEC-037 cases changed from `it.fails(` to `it(` and two comments, bodies unchanged; they pass. New file `tests/security/sec037-038-recheck.test.ts` (run by `pnpm test`): no scalar value of `Cc`, `Cf`, `Zl`, `Zp` other than U+200B to U+200D survives in a parsed name at the start, middle, or end (enumerated over every scalar value; a character at an end that zod's `trim()` removes is not stored, so it does not count); lone high and low surrogates are refused anywhere; no single character of `Zs`, `Mn`, `Me`, `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point`, or the five fillers makes a name, alone, doubled, or with the kept zero-width characters; the pattern is anchored with the `u` flag; Thai with marks and U+200B, ZWJ emoji, a keycap, a flag, and Latin names stay accepted. Against the pre-fix `schema.ts` (`git show 20ee58d^:src/modules/auth/schema.ts`, restored afterwards, `git diff` empty) four of these plain cases fail. Both entry points use the rule: sign-up (`routes.ts:192`) and the bootstrap (`bootstrap-admin.ts:111`, `signUpBodySchema.shape`); no other name field exists. Engine: Node 24.20.0, Unicode 17.0. Not closed by the fix (outside the remediation's categories): SEC-039. The comment "written with escapes only" is false: SEC-040.

### SEC-038 The library reads a whitespace-padded cookie name as the `__Host-` session cookie
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-13). Re-check verdict: FIXED; no bypass found through the four call sites; holds only while every hand-over of request headers to the library goes through the filter (a static test now checks that)
Owner: auth
Location: better-call 1.4.0 `dist/cookies.mjs:32` (`parseCookies`, `str.slice(index, eqIdx).trim()`), reached through `src/server/auth/session.ts` `readSession` and the library's `getSession`
Description: the cookie parser trims names with `String.prototype.trim`, which also removes U+00A0, U+000B, U+000C and other characters that RFC 6265 does not allow around a name; the first pair with a name wins. A name that begins with such a character does not begin with `__Host-`, so a browser applies none of the prefix rules and a sibling host could set it with `Domain=` for the shop; the server then reads it as the session cookie, which undoes SEC-034 (login CSRF into the attacker's account). Limits found: browsers refuse a cookie with a control character (RFC 6265bis), which rules out U+000B and U+000C; a U+00A0 stored as UTF-8 reaches Node as U+00C2 U+00A0 (latin1 header decoding) and is not trimmed (plain test). What remains is a cookie name whose first byte is a raw 0xA0 (not valid UTF-8). Whether any browser stores and sends that was not verified (no browser in this review).
Reproduction: four `it.fails` cases in `tests/security/sec034-035-recheck.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-038 ...": names starting with U+00A0, U+000B, U+000C are read as the session; a padded attacker cookie sent first wins over the victim's real cookie.
Remediation: before the library reads cookies, drop every `Cookie` pair whose name contains a character outside the RFC 6265 token set (or whose name with only SP and HTAB removed differs from its `trim()` result), in one place used by every session read; or report upstream and pin a fixed version; or accept with a written reason after testing current Chrome, Firefox, and Safari with a raw 0xA0 byte.
Fix (auth, 2026-10-13, commit dea1fa6): `headersForAuthLibrary` in `src/server/auth/cookies.ts` returns a copy of the request headers whose `Cookie` header keeps only pairs whose name has nothing but SP and HTAB around it (a name that `String.prototype.trim` would shorten further is dropped, as is a pair without `=`); the pairs are rewritten as `name=value` joined with `; `, and the header is removed when none is left. Every call that hands request headers to the library goes through it: `getSession` in `session.ts` `readSession`, and `signUpEmail`, `signInEmail`, `signOut` in `routes.ts`. `hasSessionCookie` (the proxy's presence check and the no-cookie shortcut of `readSession`) applies the same rule. node_modules is not patched; the library's own strict parser (`better-auth/dist/cookies/cookie-utils.mjs`) is not involved. Tests: the four SEC-038 cases in `tests/security/sec034-035-recheck.itest.ts` are now `it` with unchanged bodies and pass; added there: a name padded at the end is not a session; sign-out with a padded attacker cookie sent first ends the victim's session and leaves the attacker's; sign-in with a padded cookie does not delete it as "the previous session". Unit: `src/server/auth/cookies.test.ts` (every character the engine's `trim` removes beyond SP and HTAB, at the start and end of the name, against a restatement of the better-call parser; pair rewriting; header removal; `hasSessionCookie`), and three padded names in `src/proxy.test.ts`. With the fix taken out (old `cookies.ts`, `session.ts`, `routes.ts`), the seven SEC-038 integration cases and the proxy case fail. Commands: `pnpm test:integration` exit 0 (22 files, 838 passed, 1 expected fail, SEC-032). Not verified: browsers (unchanged from the finding), `next start`.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-13, [reviews/SEC-037-038-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-037-038-recheck.md)): `git diff 6cb51c9 HEAD -- tests/security/sec034-035-recheck.itest.ts` shows the four SEC-038 cases changed from `it.fails(` to `it(` with bodies unchanged (one re-wrapped by the formatter) plus three added cases; all pass. Read: better-call 1.4.0 `parseCookies` splits on `;`, takes the name up to the first `=`, trims it with `trim()`, keeps the first pair of a name, and skips a segment without `=`; the filter tokenizes the same way and rewrites the header, so the library parses only pairs whose name `trim()` leaves unchanged. Call sites: `session.ts:47-50` (`getSession`), `routes.ts:198-201` (`signUpEmail`), `routes.ts:280-284` (`signInEmail`), `routes.ts:310-313` (`signOut`); no other `auth.api` call, no mounted handler, no direct use of a library cookie parser under `src`. New file `tests/security/sec038-cookie-recheck.itest.ts` (8 cases, run by `pnpm test:integration`): the latin1 characters `trim()` removes beyond SP and HTAB that a `Headers` value can hold are exactly U+000B, U+000C, U+00A0 (CR and LF are refused by the Headers API); each of them at the start, at the end, at both, and mixed with SP and HTAB, in the attacker's pair sent first, answers with the victim (18 combinations); a padded pair in a separate `Cookie` line (the Headers API joins lines with `; `), after a segment without `=` or with an empty name, or inside a quoted value of another cookie never answers with the attacker; the page path (`getActor` with a `Headers` object) drops the padded pair; sign-out with only a padded cookie is 401 and ends nothing; padded or odd neighbours do not drop the real cookie. With the drop line removed from `cookies.ts` (restored afterwards, `git diff` empty) four of the eight fail with the attacker's user id. New static cases in `tests/security/sec037-038-recheck.test.ts`: every `.api.<method>(` call under `src` that passes headers passes `headersForAuthLibrary(...)` and none passes a `request`; with `session.ts` changed to pass `headers` unfiltered (restored afterwards) the case fails naming `src/server/auth/session.ts getSession`.

Findings SEC-039 and SEC-040 come from the re-check of SEC-037 and SEC-038 on 2026-10-13 ([reviews/SEC-037-038-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-037-038-recheck.md)). Each `it.fails` case was shown to fail on its flaw assertion when changed to `it` (copy deleted).

### SEC-039 Names accept unassigned, private-use, and default-ignorable characters
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-13). Bypass attempts that the fix does not cover, and that the finding did not name, are registered as SEC-041 (the five kept invisible characters anywhere and in any number) and SEC-042 (combining marks without limit)
Owner: auth (schema)
Location: src/modules/auth/schema.ts:33 (`NAME_FORBIDDEN_CHAR` refuses `Cc`, `Cf`, `Zl`, `Zp`, `Cs` only) and :40 (`NAME_VISIBLE_CHAR`); after the fix :42 and :50
Description: the SEC-037 fix refuses the categories its remediation named, but these still pass anywhere in a name next to one visible character (Node 24.20.0, Unicode 17.0): 814,730 unassigned code points (`Cn`), among them the default-ignorable, invisible U+2065, U+FFF0 to U+FFF8, U+E0000, U+E0002 to U+E001F, U+E0080 to U+E0FFF, and the noncharacters U+FDD0 to U+FDEF, U+FFFE, U+FFFF, and the last two of every plane; 137,468 private-use characters (`Co`; U+F8FF is drawn as a logo by some vendors' fonts); the default-ignorable combining marks U+034F, U+17B4, U+17B5, U+180B to U+180D, U+180F; the variation selectors U+FE00 to U+FE0D and U+E0100 to U+E01EF; and the Hangul fillers next to a visible character. The 256 variation selectors map one-to-one onto bytes, so a name can carry up to 49 hidden bytes of any value within the 100-unit length limit, the same hidden-text channel as the tag characters SEC-037 closed (a tool or a later language-model feature that reads names would see them; a person does not). A name of U+1D159 alone (musical symbol null notehead, `So`, a blank glyph in common fonts) counts as visible and looks blank (font-dependent; not checked in a browser). Unassigned code points can be assigned later with any property. Invisible differences between two names (impersonation) were already possible through U+200B to U+200D, kept by owner decision for Thai, so the gain over that is the hidden-data channel and the blank-looking name.
Reproduction: nine `it.fails` cases and two enumerations in `tests/security/sec037-038-recheck.test.ts`, describe "SEC-039 ...". With `it.fails` changed to `it` in a copy: each fails with `expected true to be false`; the enumerations list 13 default-ignorable ranges and 952,198 `Cn` or `Co` scalar values.
Remediation: also refuse `\p{Cn}`, `\p{Co}`, and `\p{Default_Ignorable_Code_Point}` except U+200B, U+200C, U+200D, U+FE0E, U+FE0F (U+FE0F for emoji); add U+1D159 to the characters that do not count as visible; flip the cases to `it`. Keep the rule shared with later name fields.
Fix (auth, 2026-10-13, commit 6cec19c): `NAME_FORBIDDEN_CHAR` in `src/modules/auth/schema.ts` now refuses, anywhere in a name, `Cc`, `Cf`, `Zl`, `Zp`, `Cs`, `Cn`, `Co`, and `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point`, except U+200B, U+200C, U+200D (kept as decided for SEC-033), U+FE0E, and U+FE0F (emoji presentation). So the Hangul fillers U+115F, U+1160, U+3164, U+FFA0 are now refused next to a visible character too (before, only a name of fillers alone was refused); the unit case that accepted "U+3164 A" was removed and U+3164 and U+1160 next to "Admin" are now in a refused list. `NAME_VISIBLE_CHAR` also excludes U+1D159. The rule stays one shared `NAME_ALLOWED_TEXT`. Tests: the nine cases and two enumerations in `tests/security/sec037-038-recheck.test.ts` describe "SEC-039 ..." are now `it` with unchanged bodies and pass; `src/modules/auth/schema.test.ts` adds 15 refused characters next to "Admin" and three names with U+FE0E or U+FE0F that stay accepted, and adds U+1D159 alone to the blank-name list. With the pre-fix `schema.ts` (`git show HEAD:src/modules/auth/schema.ts` before the commit, restored afterwards) the 12 flipped cases and 2 unit cases fail. Engine: Node 24.20.0. Commands: `pnpm test:integration` exit 0 (23 files, 846 passed, 1 expected fail, SEC-032).
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-13, [reviews/SEC-039-040-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-039-040-recheck.md)): `git show 6cec19c -- tests/security/sec037-038-recheck.test.ts` changes only `it.fails(` to `it(` (with the formatter's line joins) and the header comment; no assertion changed. `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec037-038-recheck.test.ts tests/security/sec033-name-recheck.test.ts`: 2 files, 42 passed. Against the pre-fix rule (`git show 6cec19c~1:src/modules/auth/schema.ts` copied into `tests/security` with a copy of the test file pointing at it, both deleted afterwards): 12 failed, among them all 11 SEC-039 cases (`expected true to be false`; enumerations `expected [ 'U+034F', 'U+115F-U+1160', ...(11) ]` and `expected 952198 to be +0`), the twelfth being the hygiene case that saw the copy's literal characters. New plain cases in `tests/security/sec039-040-cr66-recheck.test.ts` describe "SEC-039 re-check" pass: every `Cf`, `Cn`, `Co`, and `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point` scalar value (more than 900,000) is refused after a letter, and every 61st also at the start and in the middle, except exactly U+200B, U+200C, U+200D, U+FE0E, U+FE0F; no blank-looking character (U+115F, U+1160, U+3164, U+FFA0, U+2800, U+1D159, U+3000, U+00A0) makes a name alone, doubled, or wrapped in kept characters; no accepted scalar value produces a forbidden one under NFC, NFD, NFKC, or NFKD; Thai with marks and U+200B, emoji with U+FE0F and U+FE0E, keycaps, ZWJ families, flags, and CJK stay accepted; the bootstrap name uses the same schema. Not covered by the fix and not named by the finding: SEC-041, SEC-042.

### SEC-040 The name rule's source holds literal invisible characters while its comment says it holds none
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-13) for the files named. Literal invisible characters in four test files outside auth's paths are registered as SEC-043
Owner: auth
Location: src/modules/auth/schema.ts:33 (literal U+200B and U+200D are the ends of the range in `NAME_FORBIDDEN_CHAR`), :40 (literal U+115F, U+1160, U+3164, U+FFA0, U+2800 in `NAME_VISIBLE_CHAR`); the comment at :44-45 says "The source is written with escapes only, so this file never contains one of these characters itself". Test files with the same: src/modules/auth/schema.test.ts:110, :118
Description: in an editor or a diff the range in line 33 shows as `[-]` and the list in line 40 as blank or near-blank boxes, so a reviewer cannot see which characters the security rule allows or excludes, and a later edit can change them without a visible change (the Trojan Source class of review failure). Probable cause, observed in this re-check: the agents' file-writing tool turns a backslash-u escape written in its input into the character itself, so an escape an agent means to write lands as a literal character. The same happened to `tests/security/sec037-038-recheck.test.ts` while it was written, and to six other files under `tests/security` from earlier rounds (rewritten as escapes by script in this re-check).
Reproduction: `it.fails` "no non-test source file under src holds one (the name rule is written with escapes only)" in `tests/security/sec037-038-recheck.test.ts`; with `it` it fails listing `src/modules/auth/schema.ts:33 U+200B` and `src/modules/auth/schema.ts:40 U+115F`.
Remediation: rewrite lines 33 and 40 (and the two test lines) with backslash-u escapes inside the `String.raw` templates, which `RegExp` with the `u` flag reads as escapes; produce the escapes with a script, not the editing tool, and check the bytes afterwards (no character above U+007E in the file); flip the case to `it`. Optionally a lint check over `src` for `Cf`, `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point`, and non-ASCII space characters.
Fix (auth, 2026-10-13, commit 6cec19c): `src/modules/auth/schema.ts` was rewritten with a marker in place of each backslash-u and a Node script turned the markers into backslash-u escapes (the editing tool was not used to write an escape); the file now has no byte above U+007E. The same was done for `src/modules/auth/schema.test.ts` (the two lines named, plus the new SEC-039 cases) and `src/server/auth/cookies.test.ts` (eight literal U+00A0, U+FEFF, U+3000, U+2028 inside string and template literals, now escapes with the same values). A scan of every `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.mts`, `.js`, `.mjs`, `.cjs` file under `src` and `tests` for `Cc` (except tab and CR), `Cf`, `Zl`, `Zp`, `Co`, `Cn`, `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point`, U+00A0, U+1680, U+2000 to U+200A, U+202F, U+205F, U+3000, the fillers, and U+1D159 finds none in auth-owned files. It still finds literal characters in test files outside the paths this fix may edit: `src/proxy.test.ts:74` (U+00A0), `src/server/http.test.ts:1549` (U+D7FF, U+E000) and `:2034` (U+2028), `src/server/logger.test.ts:1633`, `:1642` (U+2028), `tests/integration/harness/test-database-url.test.ts:348` (U+00A0). The hygiene case covers non-test files under `src` and every file under `tests/security`, so it does not see these. The case "no non-test source file under src holds one" is now `it` with an unchanged body and passes. No lint rule was added.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-13, [reviews/SEC-039-040-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-039-040-recheck.md)): byte count by PowerShell `[IO.File]::ReadAllBytes`: `src/modules/auth/schema.ts`, `src/server/auth/cookies.test.ts`, `tests/security/sec037-038-recheck.test.ts`, and `src/server/env.ts` have 0 bytes above 0x7E and no control byte other than LF and CR; `src/modules/auth/schema.test.ts` has 132 such bytes, all visible Thai letters (the scan below finds no invisible character in it). The exception class and the blank list are spelled as escapes in the file (case "the regex source spells every exception as an escape"). A scan of every tracked and untracked, not ignored, text file of the repository (`git ls-files -co --exclude-standard`, 233 text files, Markdown, JSON, YAML, agent definitions, and scripts included) for `Cc` other than tab, LF, CR, `Cf`, `Zl`, `Zp`, `Co`, `Cn`, `Cs`, `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point`, non-ASCII spaces, the fillers, U+2800, and U+1D159 finds exactly the seven lines the fix named as outside its paths: `src/proxy.test.ts:74` U+00A0, `src/server/http.test.ts:1549` U+D7FF (unassigned) and U+E000, `:2034` U+2028, `src/server/logger.test.ts:1633` and `:1642` U+2028, `tests/integration/harness/test-database-url.test.ts:348` U+00A0. No document or agent definition is affected. Those seven are registered as SEC-043. Observed again in this re-check: the file-writing tool turned the backslash-u escapes of a scratch script into literal characters (91 bytes above 0x7E), so the new test file was written with `String.fromCodePoint` and numbers only (0 bytes above 0x7E).

Findings SEC-041 to SEC-045 come from the re-check of SEC-039, SEC-040, and CR-66 on 2026-10-13 ([reviews/SEC-039-040-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-039-040-recheck.md)). Each `it.fails` case is in `tests/security/sec039-040-cr66-recheck.test.ts`; a copy with `it.fails(` changed to `it(` (`zz-flip.test.ts`, deleted) failed all seven on the assertion that states the flaw.

### SEC-041 The five kept invisible characters are accepted anywhere in a name and in any number
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN. Partly fixed (run limit of 3, ae8e0d3; re-checked 2026-10-14): long runs are refused; a variation selector after a letter with no emoji form and runs of two or three are still accepted
Owner: auth (schema); the Thai part needs an owner decision
Location: src/modules/auth/schema.ts:42 (`NAME_FORBIDDEN_CHAR`, the negative lookahead that exempts U+200B to U+200D, U+FE0E, and U+FE0F, written there as escapes)
Description: the SEC-039 fix keeps U+200B, U+200C, U+200D (owner decision for Thai, SEC-033) and U+FE0E, U+FE0F (emoji presentation) without any condition on where they stand or how many follow each other. So "Admin" followed by 95 of them, which looks like "Admin", is accepted: a five-symbol alphabet carries about 220 hidden bits (about 27 bytes) in one name, the hidden-data channel SEC-039 named as its reason, smaller than the 49 bytes of the variation selectors but not closed. U+FE0E and U+FE0F after a Latin letter change nothing a reader sees. Invisible duplicates of a name ("Admin" and "Admin" plus U+200B) were already accepted and noted under SEC-039. Gain for an attacker: a hidden payload or tracking marker in a name that a later tool, export, or language-model feature reads; two names that look the same.
Reproduction: `it.fails` "a variation selector is refused after a character that has no emoji form" and "a run of kept invisible characters is refused (hidden data, invisible duplicates)" in `tests/security/sec039-040-cr66-recheck.test.ts`; with `it` each fails with `expected true to be false`.
Remediation: accept U+FE0E and U+FE0F only directly after a character with the `Emoji` property (keycap digits included); refuse two kept invisible characters in a row; with the owner, decide whether U+200B and U+200C are accepted only between two Thai letters and U+200D only between two `Extended_Pictographic` characters or inside scripts that need it. Flip the two cases to `it`.
Fix note (auth, 2026-10-13, PARTIAL, awaiting re-check): the owner chose a limit of 3 in a row (D-036 item 7): a run of more than three characters from `\p{M}` (U+FE0E and U+FE0F are `Mn`) and U+200B to U+200D is refused, so the 95-character payload after "Admin" is refused. Not done, because the owner answer does not cover them: U+FE0E or U+FE0F after a letter with no emoji form ("Admin" plus U+FE0F), and two kept characters in a row ("Admin" plus two U+200B). The two `it.fails` cases each hold one of those assertions and therefore stay `it.fails`. The hidden channel is now bounded by runs of three between visible characters inside 100 characters.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-14, [reviews/SEC-042-041-CR57-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-042-041-CR57-recheck.md)): the part the fix claims holds. In `tests/security/sec041-042-cr57-recheck.test.ts` all 625 runs of four from the five kept characters are refused after "Admin" and inside it, and the 95-character payload is refused; mutants that drop U+200B to U+200D, or U+200D alone, from the run class each make these cases fail (schema.ts restored byte-identical). The fix note's reading of the two `it.fails` cases is correct: with `it`, "a variation selector is refused after a character that has no emoji form" still fails on its first assertion ("Admin" plus U+FE0F is accepted), and "a run of kept invisible characters is refused" still fails on `Admin` plus two U+200B (its first assertion, the 95-character run, now passes). Both stay `it.fails`; neither body was touched. What remains: about one to three hidden symbols after every visible character (a run of 0 to 3 from five characters is 156 choices, about 7.3 bits, so a 14-letter name carries about 100 bits), and invisible duplicates such as "Admin" and "Admin" plus U+200B. Status stays OPEN until the owner answers the two rules D-037 lists.

### SEC-042 Combining marks stack on one base character without limit
Severity: Low
Status: schema part FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-14); display part (frontend clipping of name containers) OPEN
Owner: auth (schema); frontend for the display part
Location: src/modules/auth/schema.ts:58-61 (`NAME_ALLOWED_TEXT` sets no bound on `Mn` or `Me` marks per base); the only bound is `NAME_MAX` at :17
Description: one letter followed by 95 U+0301, or "Admin" followed by 90 U+0336, is accepted. A browser draws such a stack far above and below the line, so a name can paint over the rows next to it (a "verified" badge, a price, a button label) wherever it is shown without clipping. This is the "Zalgo" form of combining-mark abuse. Thai needs at most three marks on one consonant (a vowel, a tone mark, and rarely U+0E4C or U+0E4D).
Reproduction: `it.fails` "more than four combining marks on one base character are refused" in `tests/security/sec039-040-cr66-recheck.test.ts`; with `it` it fails with `expected true to be false`. The case "Thai with a vowel and a tone mark on one consonant stays accepted" passes.
Remediation: refuse a name in which one base character carries more than four combining marks (`\p{M}`), for example with `/\P{M}\p{M}{5}/u` as a refusal; flip the case to `it`. Independently, frontend clips every name container (`overflow: hidden` with a fixed line height) so a stack cannot paint outside its box.
Fix note (auth, 2026-10-13, awaiting re-check): owner limit of 3 (D-036 item 7). `NAME_ALLOWED_TEXT` refuses any run of more than `NAME_MAX_MARK_RUN` = 3 characters from `\p{M}` plus U+200B to U+200D, anywhere in the name; this is stricter than the remediation's four. Thai words with a vowel above, a tone mark, and a vowel below (three marks) pass; four marks, or three marks followed by U+200B, are refused [unit: `src/modules/auth/schema.test.ts`, describe "runs of combining marks and kept invisible characters"]. The `it.fails` case was flipped to `it`. The frontend clipping is still open.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-14, [reviews/SEC-042-041-CR57-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-042-041-CR57-recheck.md)): `git show ae8e0d3 -- tests/security` shows only `it.fails(` to `it(` and a two-line comment in the SEC-042 case; the body is unchanged and passes. New file `tests/security/sec041-042-cr57-recheck.test.ts` (Vitest 5.0.3, `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec041-042-cr57-recheck.test.ts`, 28 passed): three accepted and four refused for Mn, Mc (U+0903), Me (U+20DD, U+20E3), a mark outside the BMP (U+1D167), half marks, extended diacriticals, variation selectors with joiners, and marks mixed with U+200B, at the start, middle, and end of a name; the profile name and the five address text fields refuse the same; 3,000 seeded random names are accepted exactly when their longest run is 3 or less; four hostile inputs of about 200,000 characters are judged in under 1 s each. Mutation (one line of `src/modules/auth/schema.ts` at a time, file restored byte-identical by SHA-256): limit 4, lookahead removed, `\p{Mn}` for `\p{M}`, kept characters dropped, U+200D dropped, and a run of 3 refused: each makes 4 to 19 cases fail. The `\p{Mn}` mutant was caught only by the new cases. Remaining, not a new finding: the rule counts code points, so a precomposed letter followed by three marks draws 5 or 6 marks on one base (U+1EC7, U+01D5: 5; U+1FA2: 6, measured), above the remediation's "more than four" but bounded; that is why the display part stays open.

### SEC-043 Literal invisible characters remain in test files outside tests/security, and no check covers them
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-13)
Owner: the owners of the files: backend-api (`src/server/http.test.ts`, `src/server/logger.test.ts`), auth (`src/proxy.test.ts`), qa or database (`tests/integration/harness/test-database-url.test.ts`); devops for a repository-wide check
Location: seven lines, each named by its statement (pinned by statement, not number, in `tests/security/sec043-045-recheck.test.ts`; line numbers moved with later edits): `src/proxy.test.ts`, the cookie string `"theme=dark; <U+00A0>better-auth.session_token=abc",` (U+00A0); `src/server/http.test.ts`, the array entry that pairs the JSON text of the escapes for U+D7FF and U+E000 with the string of those two characters (U+D7FF, U+E000) and the string `"Error<U+2028>",` (U+2028); `src/server/logger.test.ts`, the `log.security("warn", "Sign-in failed for somchai@example.com<U+2028>", {` call and the `expect(raw).not.toContain("<U+2028>");` line (U+2028); `tests/integration/harness/test-database-url.test.ts`, the case `"a no-break space in the name"` with `urlFor("127.0.0.1", "shop<U+00A0>_test")` (U+00A0)
Description: the same review failure as SEC-040 in test files: a reviewer sees a plain space, nothing, or a line break where the test feeds a specific code point to a security control (the logger's escaping, the request helpers, the proxy's cookie handling, the URL refusals). A later edit can change which character a security test uses without a visible diff. The hygiene case of `sec037-038-recheck.test.ts` checks only non-test files under `src` and files under `tests/security`, so it does not see these. The probable cause, the file-writing tool turning escapes into characters, was observed again in this re-check.
Reproduction: `it.fails` "no tracked or untracked text file of the repository holds one" in `tests/security/sec039-040-cr66-recheck.test.ts`; with `it` it fails listing the seven lines above.
Remediation: rewrite those literals as escapes by script and check the bytes (as done for SEC-040); flip the case to `it`, which then covers every text file of the repository, documents included. Optionally a lint rule (`no-irregular-whitespace` with `skipStrings: false`, `skipTemplates: false`, plus a custom rule for `Cf` and `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point`).
Fix (auth, 2026-10-13, commit 395ec6e): a Node script (scratchpad, not the editing tool) read each of the four files, applied the `isInvisible` test of the re-check file to every character, refused to touch any line other than the seven named, and wrote each hit as a backslash-u escape of the same code point (upper-case hexadecimal) inside the existing double-quoted string literal: 1 in `src/proxy.test.ts`, 3 in `src/server/http.test.ts`, 2 in `src/server/logger.test.ts`, 1 in `tests/integration/harness/test-database-url.test.ts`; `git diff` shows only those seven lines changed. The values the tests use are unchanged. The case "no tracked or untracked text file of the repository holds one" is now `it` with an unchanged body and passes (`pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec039-040-cr66-recheck.test.ts`). Before the fix the same case failed listing exactly these seven lines (re-check evidence above). No lint rule was added.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-13, security, [reviews/SEC-043-045-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-043-045-recheck.md). (1) Commit attribution corrected: 395ec6e changes six lines in three files (`src/proxy.test.ts`, `src/server/http.test.ts`, `src/server/logger.test.ts`); the seventh line (`tests/integration/harness/test-database-url.test.ts:348`) and the flip of the hygiene case to `it` were committed in 59ed184, labelled CR-59, CR-61, CR-63. Both diffs show only the named lines changed. (2) Same values: a scratch script (outside the repository, ASCII only, checked by `grep -P '[^\x00-\x7F]'`) compared each old line with the new line after decoding its four-digit escapes: identical for all seven lines; old literals U+00A0, U+D7FF and U+E000, U+2028 (three), U+00A0; none left at HEAD. (3) Whole repository, by bytes: an independent scan of the 238 files of `git ls-files -co --exclude-standard` (236 read; `run-agents.ps1` deleted, one binary) with strict UTF-8 decoding and a wider class (adds `Zs`, `Cc`, `Bidi_Control`, U+00AD, U+FEFF, U+FFFD): no invalid UTF-8, no NUL byte in a text file, no invisible or bidi character; the only hits are 8 lines with U+FFFD (the visible replacement glyph, in `src/server/http.test.ts:1413,1550` and in two files under `tests/security`), and `src/server/http.test.ts:2051` holds a literal U+0301 (a visible accent). Both are visible and therefore outside this finding; noted for consistency only. (4) Gap in the regression case closed: the scan of `sec039-040-cr66-recheck.test.ts` skips a file with a NUL byte as binary and decodes leniently; the new case "no tracked or untracked file with a text extension holds a NUL byte or bytes that are not UTF-8" in `tests/security/sec043-045-recheck.test.ts` covers both and passes. (5) The four files and the seven lines are pinned by the case "the four files hold none of the seven code points as a literal, and each line now spells an escape" (passes). Fixed.

### SEC-044 A low-entropy or published AUTH_SECRET of 32 characters starts a production server
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-13); the remaining bypasses are SEC-047 (fixed 2026-10-13) and SEC-049 (open)
Owner: devops (`src/server/env.ts`)
Location: src/server/env.ts:167-169 (`AUTH_SECRET`: length only) and :296-300 (`checkAuthSecretForProduction`: the `not_a_secret` marker only)
Description: with NODE_ENV=production, `parseEnv` and `readAuthSecret` accept 32 times "x", 32 spaces, the library's published default `better-auth-secret-12345678901234567890`, and the secret of `tests/integration/auth/support.ts:19`. The library itself would warn about low entropy, but its logger is configured at level `error` (src/server/auth/auth.ts:95), so nothing is written; it refuses its own default only when its context is created on the first auth request (a 500, not a refused start). The secret signs the session cookie; sessions are also checked against the database, so a known secret does not by itself forge a session, but every later use of the secret (signed or encrypted values, verification tokens) inherits the weakness. Depends on an operator choosing such a value.
Reproduction: `it.fails` "production refuses a secret of one repeated character, of blanks, or one published in this repository or the library" in `tests/security/sec039-040-cr66-recheck.test.ts`; with `it` it fails at the first value with `expected [] to not deeply equal []`.
Remediation: in production also refuse a secret with fewer than 16 distinct characters or of only blanks, and the published values above (the library's default and every secret string in `tests/` and `src/**/*.test.ts`, kept as a list in `src/server/env.ts`); flip the case to `it`. DEPLOYMENT.md already gives a generation command.
Fix (auth, 2026-10-13, commit c46c52e): `checkAuthSecretForProduction` in `src/server/env.ts` (used by both `parseEnv` and `readAuthSecret`) now also refuses, when NODE_ENV=production, a value with fewer than `AUTH_SECRET_MIN_DISTINCT_CHARACTERS` = 8 distinct code points (one repeated character, blanks), a value that is a shorter unit repeated (`"0123456789abcdef"` twice), and a value in `PUBLISHED_AUTH_SECRETS` (the library's `better-auth-secret-12345678901234567890` and `integration-test-secret-0123456789-abcdefghij`), also with blanks around it. New rule text `ENV_RULES.authSecretWeak`, no value in it; a placeholder is still reported once with the placeholder rule. Development and test are unchanged, so every stub keeps working. Deviations from the written remediation, with reasons: (1) 8 distinct characters, not 16: 32 hexadecimal digits from a random source have fewer than 16 distinct characters most of the time (simulated over 1,000,000 values: fewer than 12 in 1.7 %, fewer than 10 in 0.01 %, fewer than 8 in none), so 16 would refuse correctly generated secrets; the repetition test covers the low-diversity patterns 8 lets through. (2) The list does not hold every secret string of the test files: `Zq7canary-auth-secret-0123456789abcdef` is the valid production secret of several test files outside auth's paths (`src/instrumentation.test.ts`, `tests/security/t011-env-health-recheck.test.ts`, `tests/security/round7-recheck.test.ts`), so listing it would break those tests; it is a residual, as is every other public value not on the list. Tests: `src/server/env.test.ts` describe "AUTH_SECRET in production: values that are plainly not random (SEC-044)" (refused by both readers; accepted in development and test; 8,000 random base64url, hex, and base64 values accepted) and the `loadEnvAtStartup` case "stops the start in production when AUTH_SECRET is weak"; all fail against the pre-fix `env.ts` (checked by restoring it from `HEAD~1`). The re-check case "production refuses a secret of one repeated character, ..." is now `it` with an unchanged body and passes.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-13, security, [reviews/SEC-043-045-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-043-045-recheck.md). `git show c46c52e -- tests/security`: the SEC-044 case changed only `it.fails(` to `it(` and the formatter's line joins. Read `src/server/env.ts` `checkAuthSecretForProduction`, `isWeakAuthSecret`, `isRepetition`: the same function serves `parseEnv` and `readAuthSecret`; the placeholder rule wins over the weak rule, so one issue per value. Tests in `tests/security/sec043-045-recheck.test.ts`, all pass: the four values of this finding refused by both readers with `authSecretWeak`; both published values with space, tab, LF, CRLF, U+00A0, U+3000, U+FEFF, U+2028 before, after, or around them refused (24 forms each); exact repetitions, 32 U+00A0, 32 U+200B, and 7 distinct code points without a period refused; no refusal carries the value; 12,000 random values (base64url of 32 bytes, hex of 16 and 32 bytes, base64 of 24 bytes) accepted. The threshold of 8 and not 16 distinct code points is accepted: measured here on 200,000 random values each, fewer than 16 distinct occurs in 92.7 % of 32-digit and 23.5 % of 64-digit hexadecimal secrets (`openssl rand -hex 16` and `-hex 32`), fewer than 8 in none; 16 would refuse correct secrets, so the remediation's 16 was wrong. The second deviation (the canary test secret not listed) is accepted as documented. Bypasses found, each plainly not random and accepted in production: one character added to a refused repetition (`"password"` four times plus `"1"`, `"abcdefgh"` four times plus `"i"`), a skewed value with exactly 8 distinct (`"a"` 25 times plus `"bcdefgh"`), the alphabet in order, invisible padding (25 U+200B plus 7 letters), and the library's default in upper case, with `"!"` added, or with U+200B added: registered as SEC-047 (Low), because the finding's four values and its stated principle for blanks and published values are met and the rest needs a different kind of check. Observation for the owner: the comment at `src/server/env.ts:206` says the length counts UTF-16 code units; zod 4.6.5 `.min` counts code points (16 astral characters, 32 units, are refused as too short). Stricter than stated, so not a finding; the comment and the CR-66 review text should say code points.

### SEC-045 BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS in the environment replaces the validated AUTH_SECRET, and a malformed entry is written to the error log
Severity: Medium
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-13); remediation item 3 not done, registered as SEC-048 (Low); the telemetry variables are SEC-046 (Low); both fixed and re-checked 2026-10-13
Owner: auth (`src/server/auth/auth.ts`), devops (`src/server/env.ts`)
Location: src/server/auth/auth.ts:84-89 (`createAuth` passes `secret` but not `secrets`); better-auth 1.7.7 `dist/context/create-context.mjs:70-77` (`options.secrets ?? parseSecretsEnv(env.BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS)` wins over `options.secret`), `dist/context/secret-utils.mjs:18` (the error message quotes the entry); src/server/http.ts:640-643 (`errorResponse` logs the cause of a 500 with its message)
Description: when `BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS` is set in the server's environment (the library documents it for secret rotation; this project neither reads nor refuses it), the library signs with its first entry and keeps the validated `AUTH_SECRET` only as a legacy fallback. A value of `1:x` makes the one-character "x" the signing secret, with no refusal and no warning (the library's warning is filtered by its `error` log level). An entry without a version (for example the new secret pasted alone) makes every auth request fail with a `BetterAuthError` whose message contains the whole entry, and the route's `errorResponse` writes it at `error` level: observed line `{"code":"INTERNAL_ERROR","err":{"name":"BetterAuthError","message":"Invalid BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS entry: \"Zq7SECRETcanary-rotated\". ..."},"level":"error",...,"message":"Unhandled error"}`. The logger's redaction does not match it (the text before the colon is `entry`). The client gets a generic 500 (checked). Both need an operator to set a variable this project does not document, so the rating is Medium, not higher; the second puts a secret into logs (SR-04). `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, `AUTH_SECRET`, `BETTER_AUTH_URL`, and `BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` are read by the library too; the first three lose to the explicit options, the last adds trusted origins to the library's own origin check (not tested; the application's routes check `Origin` themselves first).
Reproduction: `tests/security/sec039-040-cr66-recheck.test.ts` describe "SEC-045 ...": the control case (no variable, signing secret is the validated one) passes; `it.fails` "a one-character BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS entry does not become the signing secret" fails with `expected 'x' to be 'Zq7SECRETcanary-...'`; `it.fails` "a malformed BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS entry does not reach the server log" fails with `expected '{"code":"INTERNAL_ERROR","err":{"name...' not to contain 'Zq7SECRETcanary'`.
Remediation: (1) `src/server/env.ts` refuses, at start-up and in `readAuthSecret`, any of `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, `BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS`, `BETTER_AUTH_URL`, `BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` being set (rule text "must not be set; this application reads AUTH_SECRET and APP_URL"), naming the variable only; (2) `createAuth` passes `secrets` explicitly built from the validated value (or another way that makes the library ignore the variable), so the refusal does not depend on start-up order; (3) a library error is logged by class name only, as the library logger in `auth.ts:98-104` already does, not with its message. Flip the two cases to `it`.
Fix (auth, 2026-10-13, commit c46c52e): (2) `createAuth` in `src/server/auth/auth.ts` passes `secrets: [{ version: AUTH_SECRET_VERSION (1), value: settings.secret }]` beside `secret`. The library takes `options.secrets` before it would parse `BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS`, so the variable is never read: no other signing secret, no `BetterAuthError` quoting an entry, nothing to log. The signing secret is the validated one; `secret` stays the legacy fallback with the same value. The versioned key set (`secretConfig`) is used by the library only for the cookie cache, the OAuth account cookie, and plugins, none of which is enabled. `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` and `AUTH_SECRET` in the process environment already lost to the explicit `secret`. (1) `src/server/env.ts` refuses `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, `BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS`, `BETTER_AUTH_URL`, `BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` when set (non-empty) in every NODE_ENV, in `parseEnv` (so the start-up hook stops the process with one `error` line naming the variable) and in `readAuthSecret` (so a lazily created instance obeys the same rule); rule text `ENV_RULES.authLibraryVariable`, the value is never in the error or the log. (3) was not needed for this finding (no library error carries the entry any more) and `src/server/http.ts` is not an auth path; `errorResponse` still logs the message of any other unexpected error. No new runtime security event was added, so no new `logger.security()` call: the refusal is a start-up configuration error, written once by the existing `loadEnvAtStartup` line. `.env.example` says not to set the four variables. Tests: `src/server/auth/auth.test.ts` (new; `BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS` of `1:x`, a versioned list, and a malformed entry are ignored and nothing of them reaches the logger; `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` and `AUTH_SECRET` in the environment ignored), `src/server/env.test.ts` describe "the auth library's own variables are refused (SEC-045)" and the `loadEnvAtStartup` case "stops the start when BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS is set". Against the pre-fix `auth.ts` the `auth.test.ts` case and both re-check cases fail; against the pre-fix `env.ts` every new env case fails. In `tests/security/sec039-040-cr66-recheck.test.ts` the case "a one-character BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS entry does not become the signing secret" is now `it` with an unchanged body. The case "a malformed BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS entry does not reach the server log" had to change its body, not only `it.fails` to `it`: it asserted that the library throws (`expect(thrown).toBeDefined()`), which the fix removes. It now asserts that no error is thrown and the validated secret is in use, and keeps the former log assertions for any error that were thrown; a fix note in the file says so. A new case there, "the variable is refused at start-up by name, with no part of its value", checks both readers.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-13, security, [reviews/SEC-043-045-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-043-045-recheck.md). Library read again (better-auth 1.7.7 `dist/context/create-context.mjs:70-80`): `const secretsArray = options.secrets ?? parseSecretsEnv(env.BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS)`; with `secrets` given, the variable is never parsed and the signing secret is `secretsArray[0].value`; `options.secret || env.BETTER_AUTH_SECRET || env.AUTH_SECRET` keeps the explicit value first. Every `env.*` read in `better-auth/dist` (plugins excluded, none enabled) and in `@better-auth/core` and `@better-auth/telemetry` was listed by `grep`: `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` in `cookies/index.mjs` is only in the exported helper `getCookieCache`, which nothing in the library or `src` calls; `BETTER_AUTH_URL` and the five other URL variables of `utils/url.mjs` lose to the explicit `baseURL`; `BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` is appended on every call of `getTrustedOrigins` whatever the options, so for it the start-up refusal is the only control (it is in the list and refused by both readers); `BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY` and `BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY_ENDPOINT` override the explicit `telemetry: { enabled: false }` and are not in the list: SEC-046. Tests in `tests/security/sec043-045-recheck.test.ts`, all pass: nine environment forms (`1:x`, a two-entry list, an entry without version, a non-numeric version, an empty value, only separators, `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, `AUTH_SECRET`, all three at once) leave the signing secret the validated one, with no error; `BETTER_AUTH_URL` does not change the library's base URL; `createAuth` passes `secrets` built from `settings.secret` (source pinned); each of the four variables is refused by both readers in development, test, and production with one issue naming it and no part of the value, and an empty value is not refused. The changed body of "a malformed BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS entry does not reach the server log" (c46c52e) was compared with the original: the property "the entry does not reach the log" is kept and now holds more strongly (no error at all, validated secret in use); the log assertions are kept but can no longer run, so the case no longer exercises `errorResponse` with a library error. Not weakened for SEC-045; the uncovered path is item 3, now SEC-048 with its own `it.fails` case. Item 3 is confirmed not done: `src/server/http.ts:639-643` logs `err: appError.cause`, whose `message` the logger writes after pattern redaction (the SEC-048 case shows a canary in a `BetterAuthError` message reaching the line). Against the code before the fix (`git show c46c52e~1:` of `src/server/env.ts` and `src/server/auth/auth.ts` copied into a scratch folder under `tests/security`, relative imports pointed at `@/server/...`, the new file's imports pointed at the copies; folder deleted afterwards, `src` untouched): the signing-secret case fails with `expected 'x' to be 'Zq7SECRETcanary-...'`, the four-variables case fails, and the four SEC-044 cases fail (for example `"xxxx...": expected [] to deeply equal [...]`). The cases that read files by path failed there only because the copy sat one folder deeper.

### SEC-046 The library's telemetry variables override `telemetry: { enabled: false }` and are not refused
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-13)
Owner: devops (`src/server/env.ts`)
Location: src/server/env.ts:114-119 (`AUTH_LIBRARY_VARIABLES` lacks the telemetry variables) and its comment at :104-113 (says the application passes every value explicitly); src/server/auth/auth.ts:109 (`telemetry: { enabled: false }`); @better-auth/telemetry 1.7.7 `dist/node.mjs:437` (`ENV.BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY_ENDPOINT`) and :449 (`getBooleanEnvVar("BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY", false) || telemetryEnabled`)
Description: when `BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY` is true and `BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY_ENDPOINT` names a URL, the library posts an `init` event to that URL when its context is created in production, although `createAuth` disables telemetry explicitly: the variable is OR-ed with the option. The event holds the auth configuration flags, runtime, database and framework names, a hash of the base URL and package name, and `systemInfo` (CPU model and count, memory, platform); not the secret (checked). The start-up refusal added for SEC-045 does not list these two names, so nothing stops them. Needs an operator to set two variables this project does not document; the effect is an outbound request with host details to a chosen address, and a configuration that contradicts what the code says. Low (hygiene). `BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY_DEBUG` writes the event to the library's `info` log instead, which the `error` level filters.
Reproduction: `tests/security/sec043-045-recheck.test.ts`, `it.fails` "parseEnv and readAuthSecret refuse BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY and BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY_ENDPOINT by name" (with `it` it fails with `BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY: expected [] to deeply equal [...]`). The plain case "library behaviour pinned: ..." starts a listener on 127.0.0.1 and a child Node process with NODE_ENV=production and the library's options as in `createAuth` (secret, secrets, base URL, telemetry disabled): no request without the variables, one `init` POST with them.
Remediation: add `BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY`, `BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY_ENDPOINT`, and `BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY_DEBUG` to `AUTH_LIBRARY_VARIABLES`, correct the comment, list them in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` beside the four; flip the case to `it`. When the library is updated, list its `env.*` reads again (`grep -rhoE "env\.[A-Z_]+" node_modules/better-auth/dist node_modules/@better-auth/*/dist`).
Fix (devops, 2026-10-13, commit 544d2ee): the three names were added to `AUTH_LIBRARY_VARIABLES` (seven names), the comment was corrected, `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` and `.env.example` list them, and the case was flipped to `it`.
Re-check evidence: 2026-10-13, security, [reviews/SEC-046-047-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-046-047-recheck.md). `git show 544d2ee -- tests/security`: the case changed only `it.fails(` to `it(` and the formatter's reindent; the seven-name pin in `sec043-045-recheck.test.ts` gained the three names. New file `tests/security/sec046-047-recheck.test.ts`, describe "SEC-046 re-check", 5 cases, all pass: the export holds exactly the seven names (written out in the test); each name with five values (`1`, `true`, `false`, `0`, a URL with a canary) in development, test, and production is refused by `parseEnv` and `readAuthSecret` with exactly one issue (its name, `authLibraryVariable`) and no canary in `String()`, stack, JSON, or issues; empty is not refused; all seven at once give seven issues in list order and one start-up log line naming them without a value; `getAuth` passes `readAuthSecret()` into `createAuth` (source pinned), so a refused variable stops the library before its telemetry could start; every `BETTER_AUTH_` name in the `dist` trees of better-auth, @better-auth/core, and @better-auth/telemetry (versions pinned at 1.7.7) is one of the seven or known inert (`BETTER_AUTH_TELEMETRY_ID`, read by nothing, asserted; three framework URL names that lose to `baseURL`). Eleven mutated copies of `env.ts` (each name dropped, either reader without the check, the wrong rule, empty refused) each fail 2 to 5 cases. Fixed.

### SEC-047 The weak-secret floor lets near-repetitions, skewed values, and altered published values through
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-13); periodic values with a counter, interleaved runs, and other lag patterns still pass: SEC-049
Owner: devops (`src/server/env.ts`)
Location: src/server/env.ts:346-367 (`isRepetition`, `isWeakAuthSecret`)
Description: the SEC-044 rule refuses exact repetitions, fewer than 8 distinct code points, and the two published values with blanks around them. One extra character defeats the first two, and any other change the third. Accepted in production: `"password"` four times plus `"1"`; `"abcdefgh"` four times plus `"i"`; `"0123456789abcdef"` twice plus `"X"`; `"a"` 25 times plus `"bcdefgh"`; the alphabet in order; 25 U+200B plus 7 letters (an invisible value); the library's default in upper case, with `"!"`, or with U+200B appended. Each is guessable by anyone who tries common mistakes. Depends on an operator choosing such a value against the documented generation command; sessions are also checked against the database (SEC-044 description). Low (hardening).
Reproduction: `tests/security/sec043-045-recheck.test.ts`, `it.fails` "production refuses plainly non-random values that differ from the refused shapes by one character"; with `it` it fails at the first value with `"passwordpasswordpasswordpassword1": expected [] to deeply equal [...]`.
Remediation (suggested, measured): in production also refuse (a) any code point outside printable ASCII U+0021 to U+007E (refuses blanks, invisible padding, and look-alike tricks; every documented generator emits ASCII), (b) any substring of 8 characters that occurs twice, (c) a run of 8 characters with a constant code-point step (`abcdefgh`, `12345678`), and (d) compare the published list without regard to case and as a substring. A simulation in this re-check (500,000 values each) refused 0 base64url-43, 0 base64-44, 0 hex-32, and 1 hex-64 value with (a) to (c), and refused every bypass above except a keyboard row (`qwerty...`). Keep 8 distinct as the floor. Flip the case to `it`. No list of rules makes a chosen value random; the generation command in DEPLOYMENT.md stays the control.
