- Wider than D-042 (documented case, not a finding): any combining mark repeated within a run is refused, in every script and every field that uses the rule (Latin `e` with two acutes, U+20DD twice). The owner ruled on "a repeated tone mark".
- Backend-api's edits to the SEC-042 run-limit cases in `sec041-042-cr57-recheck.test.ts` keep their intent: the Mc, Me, and outside-BMP rows now use four distinct marks of the named category (checked with Node's `\p{gc=..}`), and the random oracle adds "no mark repeated within a run". The reviewer added a distinct-mark version of "a run at the start counts too".
- Bypass: SEC-061 (U+FE0E or U+FE0F between the two sara e).

### SEC-061 Sara e, a variation selector, sara e passes the name rule and keys like doubled sara e
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN (owner decision needed: extend the D-042 point 2 rule, or accept)
Owner: owner (D-042 point 2 scope) with backend-api (`src/modules/auth/schema.ts`)
Location: src/modules/auth/schema.ts:85 (`NAME_DOUBLED_SARA_E` allows only U+200B to U+200D between the two U+0E40); prisma/migrations/20261015090000_store_name_key/migration.sql:33 (the key removes U+FE00 to U+FE0F)
Description: D-042 point 2 refuses a doubled sara e because it reads as sara ae. The rule lets only U+200B to U+200D stand between the two. U+FE0E and U+FE0F are allowed after any letter (SEC-041, by owner decision), and `store_name_key` removes them. So U+0E40 U+FE0F U+0E40 passes the name rule, and the store key holds U+0E40 U+0E40. A variation selector that starts no defined sequence is drawn as nothing, so the name reads as sara ae. This copies any Thai store name that contains sara ae. It also defeats the reserved match for the Thai entry for "admin" and for the current site name: neither key contains the entry. The same text passes every other field that uses the rule (sign-up and profile names). Gain and limits as SEC-058 to SEC-060: a copied or reserved-looking store name, no access to another store. Low.
Reproduction: `tests/security/t040-sellers.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-061": the documented case renames a store to the Thai "admin" and the site name with sara e, U+FE0F or U+FE0E, sara e in place of sara ae, and to such a copy of an existing store's name: all 200. The `it.fails` case, flipped in a scratch copy (deleted), fails with all three names listed `-> 200`.
Remediation (owner chooses): (1) in `NAME_DOUBLED_SARA_E`, allow every kept invisible character between the two, that is U+200B to U+200D, U+FE0E, and U+FE0F; (2) more generally, refuse U+FE0E and U+FE0F after any character that has no variation sequence (closes the rest of SEC-041 as well); or (3) accept in writing. With (1) or (2) the `it.fails` case flips to `it` unchanged (the secure answer may be 400 or 409).
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-062 A narrow space inside a word becomes an ordinary space in the store key, so a reserved word or a store name can be split invisibly
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN (owner decision needed: refuse narrow spaces in names, remove them in the key, or accept)
Owner: owner (D-041 point 1 and D-042 point 1 scope) with backend-api (name rule) or database (`store_name_key`)
Location: prisma/migrations/20261015090000_store_name_key/migration.sql:39 (every White_Space run becomes U+0020); src/modules/sellers/store-name.ts:79 (`containsReservedName`: a one-word entry is not matched across a space, by design); src/modules/auth/schema.ts:47 (`Zs` is not refused)
Description: the name rule accepts every space separator. NFKC and the key turn U+2009 thin space, U+200A hair space, U+202F narrow no-break space, and U+205F (and the wider U+2000 to U+2008) into U+0020. The reserved match keeps spaces so that `Pad Mint` is not `admin`. So `Ad<U+200A>min` has the key `ad min` and is not reserved. `Fam<U+200A>ous 062` has a key other than `famous 062`, so a store can take a copy of another store's name. A hair space is about a twenty-fourth to a sixth of an em wide, depending on the font. Thai is written without spaces inside a phrase, so the site name with a hair space inside reads as the site name. Gain and limits as SEC-058: a reserved-looking or copied store name, no access. Low.
Reproduction: `tests/security/t040-sellers.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-062": the documented case renames a store to `Ad<U+200A>min`, `Offi<U+2009>cial Store`, `Sup<U+202F>port`, the site name with U+200A after its fourth code unit, and `Fam<U+200A>ous 062` while `Famous 062` exists: all 200. The `it.fails` case, flipped in a scratch copy (deleted), fails with all five listed `-> 200`.
Remediation (owner chooses): (1) in `NAME_ALLOWED_TEXT`, refuse every `Zs` except U+0020 (and U+00A0 and U+3000, if wanted; the schema's trim and the key handle those). This needs no migration; existing names keep their keys. (2) In `store_name_key`, remove U+2000 to U+200A, U+202F, and U+205F instead of turning them into a space. That needs a migration and the collision rename. (3) Accept in writing.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-063 Thai look-alike letters make a store name copy that the key and the reserved match miss
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN (owner decision needed: D-041 point 1 accepted look-alike letters of other scripts only)
Owner: owner with backend-api (name rule or a folding step before the reserved match) or database (`store_name_key`)
Location: prisma/migrations/20261015090000_store_name_key/migration.sql:26 (`store_name_key`: NFKC keeps each pair apart); src/modules/sellers/store-name.ts:79 (`containsReservedName`)
Description: three Thai letter pairs look alike in common fonts and are different after NFKC: lakkhangyao U+0E45 for sara aa U+0E32, kho khuat U+0E03 for kho khai U+0E02, and kho khon U+0E05 for kho khwai U+0E04. U+0E03 and U+0E05 are obsolete and appear in no modern word; U+0E45 appears only after U+0E24 and U+0E26. `ทๅงกๅร` is not reserved. A store can take `ขๅยค้ๅ`, `ฃายค้า`, or `ขายฅ้า` beside `ขายค้า`. The members of each pair are not identical: lakkhangyao has a tail below the line, and kho khuat and kho khon have a notch or zigzag at the top. A buyer who reads quickly does not see the difference. This is the same class as the Thai copies of SEC-060, but the letters are look-alikes, not identical draws, so it needs its own decision. Gain and limits as SEC-058. Low.
Reproduction: `tests/security/t040-sellers.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-063": the documented case renames a store to the four copies (one reserved, three of an existing name): all 200. The `it.fails` case, flipped in a scratch copy (deleted), fails with all four listed `-> 200`.
Remediation (owner chooses): (1) in `NAME_ALLOWED_TEXT`, refuse U+0E03 and U+0E05 everywhere, and U+0E45 except directly after U+0E24 or U+0E26. This needs no migration. (2) Fold the three pairs in `store_name_key` (migration and collision rename) and in the reserved match. (3) Accept in writing, as D-041 did for other scripts.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-064 Uploaded images are stored and served byte for byte, camera location (Exif GPS) included
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN (owner decision needed: re-encoding needs an image library, a new dependency)
Owner: owner (decision), then backend-api (`src/modules/uploads`)
Location: src/modules/uploads/reader.ts:58 (`readUploadRequest` returns the bytes as sent); src/modules/uploads/service.ts:83 (`storage.put` of those bytes)
Description: the upload is checked by its leading bytes and then stored and served unchanged. A phone photo carries Exif metadata, often with GPS coordinates, the device, and the time it was taken. A seller who works from home and photographs products there publishes the location of the home to anyone who opens the picture; the file is public by design (section 5.8). Any data after valid leading bytes (a polyglot) and any image dimensions also pass; the serving headers stop a browser from running a polyglot, and a decompression bomb is not decoded on the server, but a huge-dimension PNG can still stall the browsers of buyers who view it. What an attacker gains: nothing directly; the harm is to the seller's privacy (PDPA personal data, published by the platform). Low.
Reproduction: `tests/security/t041-uploads.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-064": the documented case uploads a JPEG with an APP1 Exif block naming `GPSLatitude` and a canary and receives both back from `GET /api/files/{key}`. The `it.fails` case, flipped in a scratch copy (deleted), fails with `expected '<FF D8 FF E1 ...>Exif...MM GPSLati...' not to contain 'GPSLatitude'`.
Remediation (owner chooses): (1) decode and re-encode every upload on the server (for example with `sharp`), which drops metadata and trailing data, caps dimensions, and turns every polyglot into a plain image; this also closes the polyglot residual of TM-17. (2) Strip the Exif and XMP segments of JPEG, the text and `eXIf` chunks of PNG, and the `EXIF` and `XMP ` chunks of WebP without re-encoding; smaller, but polyglots and dimensions stay. (3) Accept in writing and tell sellers in the dashboard that photos keep their metadata.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-065 An upload that nothing references is kept on disk and served for ever, with no quota
Severity: Medium
Status: PARTIAL (re-checked 2026-10-20, commit 1f38501, [reviews/SEC-065-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-065-recheck.md)): remediation items 2 and 3 FIXED; item 1 built, verified, and scheduled (wired in 37d07c8; the scheduled deletion was security-reviewed on 2026-10-20 by reading and in-process tests, [reviews/SEC-065-cleanup-security.md](reviews/SEC-065-cleanup-security.md), not observed in a running `next start`; SEC-073 new); item 4 not built (T-050/T-051); item 5 deployment. Both attacker gains are now bounded: (a) one store keeps at most 50 unreferenced rows, at most 250 MiB, whether or not the cleanup runs (files without a row aside, SEC-066); (b) an unattached picture is public for at most 24 hours. Until the schedule was wired (37d07c8), a store whose 50 unattached uploads had expired could not upload again (no endpoint deletes an upload); with the schedule, such a store waits at most until the next hourly run
Owner: backend-api (cleanup job of section 5.8 "Lifetime", quota) with devops (scheduling, disk monitoring)
Location: src/modules/uploads/service.ts:122 (`openFile` serves any registered upload); src/modules/uploads/repository.ts:6 ("the orphan cleanup of section 5.8 is a later job"); no cleanup job exists
Description: section 5.8 says an upload that nothing references for 24 hours is removed by a cleanup job. No job exists, nothing limits how much one store keeps, and `GET /api/files/{key}` serves every registered upload whether or not it is attached. One approved seller can upload 30 files of 5 MB an hour (the `uploads` rule), about 150 MB an hour or 3.5 GB a day, never attach any of them, and keep doing so; several accounts multiply it. What an attacker with an approved seller account gains: (a) the upload directory, and anything on the same volume, fills up, after which every upload fails and, if the database shares the volume, more than uploads fails; (b) free public hosting of any JPEG, PNG, or WebP picture on the marketplace's own origin, with no moderation step, since product moderation applies to products and never to an unattached upload, and no endpoint lets an admin remove an upload. The account needed is one an admin approved, which limits who can do it; a suspension stops new uploads but not the serving of the old ones. Medium.
Reproduction: `tests/security/t041-uploads.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-065": the documented case uploads a picture, sets its row 25 hours back, and shows no product, logo, or order item references it and `GET /api/files/{key}` still answers 200. The `it.fails` case, flipped in a scratch copy (deleted), fails with `expected 200 to be 404`.
Remediation: (1) build the cleanup job of section 5.8 (delete orphans older than the grace period, row first under a lock that excludes a concurrent attach, then the bytes; log counts only) and schedule it; (2) until then, or in addition, answer 404 for an upload that is unattached and older than the grace period; (3) a per-store cap on unattached uploads (for example 50, or a byte total), refused with a 4xx before the body is read where possible; (4) an admin action that removes an upload's link and bytes for content takedown (T-050/T-051 scope); (5) devops: put `UPLOAD_DIR` on its own volume and alert on free space. The case may be adapted to call the job's entry point first if (1) alone is the fix; the security agent re-checks that change.
Re-check evidence (2026-10-20, [reviews/SEC-065-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-065-recheck.md)): `git show 1f38501 -- tests/security` shows the `it.fails` flipped to `it` with its body unchanged, and the documented case's status changed from 200 to 404 with its row assertions unchanged (the change the finding allowed). Code read of `src/modules/uploads/{repository,service,cleanup,storage,schema,errors}.ts` and both routes. New file `tests/security/sec065-recheck.itest.ts` (17 plain cases, 2 `it.fails` for SEC-066 and SEC-067): twelve parallel POSTs with three places left give exactly three 201 and nine 409 `UPLOAD_LIMIT`, 50 rows, no rowless file; two stores racing at one place each get one each; an expired orphan is the same 404 for anonymous, customer, admin, another seller, and the owner; the grace boundary is the database clock (60 s either side); another store cannot attach the orphan as its logo, the owner can and it is served again and kept; the cleanup keeps uploads whose logo update or order item insert is in flight, uploads 60 s inside the grace period, and an order picture three years old; two concurrent runs delete each orphan exactly once; rows whose `storageKey` is `../escape-canary`, `../../escape-canary`, `..\escape-canary`, an absolute path to a canary, upper-case hex, a `.jpg` suffix, a trailing slash, or empty are deleted with every canary intact and one warn line each; a pattern-valid key that is a directory keeps the directory and its content. `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.integration.config.mts tests/integration/uploads tests/security/t041-uploads.itest.ts tests/security/sec065-recheck.itest.ts` -> exit 0, 3 files, 67 passed, 3 expected fail (SEC-064, SEC-066, SEC-067). The two `it.fails` flipped in a scratch copy (deleted) fail on their assertions: `expected true to be false` (SEC-066) and `expected 'Index Scan using "Upload_storageKey_k...' not to match /Seq Scan on "OrderItem"/` (SEC-067). The parallel cases were not mutation-tested against a copy without the advisory lock (no edit of `src`).

### SEC-066 A stored file whose row is gone, or never written, stays on disk for ever
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN
Owner: backend-api (`src/modules/uploads`), with devops (disk monitoring)
Location: src/modules/uploads/cleanup.ts:70 (only keys returned by deleted rows are deleted; a failed delete is a warn line and the file stays); src/modules/uploads/service.ts:114 and :126 (bytes are written inside the transaction before the row; a failed clean-up delete is a warn line; a process that dies between `put` and the end of the transaction runs no clean-up at all); src/modules/uploads/cleanup.ts:67-79 (added 2026-10-20: the scheduled cleanup commits a batch's row deletion before it unlinks the files, so a process stopped in between leaves up to 100 files without a row and without a warn line)
Description: the cleanup walks `Upload` rows, never the storage root. A file without a row is not served (`openFile` needs the row), but no job ever removes it, and it does not count against the per-store cap, which counts rows. It arises when a delete fails (permissions, a locked file on Windows, a full or read-only volume), and when the process stops after `storage.put` and before the commit or the clean-up delete (crash, out-of-memory kill, redeploy). What an attacker gains: nothing direct; an approved seller cannot choose to cause either case. The harm is slow, silent disk growth outside the 250 MiB per store bound that SEC-065 relies on, and leftover bytes of uploads that were refused. Low.
Reproduction: `tests/security/sec065-recheck.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-065 re-check: the cleanup and paths outside the storage root": the documented case stores a file with no row, shows it is 404, runs the cleanup, and shows the file is still there. The `it.fails` case ages such a file past the grace period by its modification time; flipped in a scratch copy (deleted), it fails with `expected true to be false`.
Remediation: (1) a sweep, in the same schedule as the cleanup, that lists the storage root, keeps only names matching `STORAGE_KEY_PATTERN` in a two-digit directory named by the key's first two digits, and deletes those older than the grace period (by modification time) that have no `Upload` row, never following a link (`lstat`; skip a two-digit directory or entry that is a link or junction, since `unlink` through a linked directory deletes outside the root, shown in `tests/security/sec065-cleanup-schedule.test.ts`), and bounded per run (stream with `opendir`, a cap on entries examined and files deleted, resume across runs), because it would be the first code to list a root of any size; or (2) write the bytes under a temporary name in a separate directory and rename them into place after the commit, and sweep the temporary directory; and in either case (3) devops alerts on the warn lines "could not be deleted".
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-067 The order item reference check is a full scan of OrderItem, reachable without a session or a rate limit
Severity: Medium
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-20, commit c55c575)
Owner: database (index migration), backend-api (`src/modules/uploads/repository.ts`)
Location: src/modules/uploads/repository.ts:110 (`findUploadByKey`, run by every `GET /api/files/{key}`), :82 (`countUnreferencedUploads`, twice per upload request), :144 and :156 (`deleteExpiredOrphans`); prisma/schema.prisma `model OrderItem` (indexes on `sellerOrderId` and `productId` only; none on `productImageUploadId`); the `Restrict` foreign key on `OrderItem.productImageUploadId` makes every `Upload` delete check `OrderItem` the same way
Description: SEC-065 added "nothing references this upload" to the statement that serves every file. Its `OrderItem` condition has no index to use, so PostgreSQL scans the whole `OrderItem` table for it. The scan is reached for every upload older than 24 hours that is neither a product picture nor a logo: an expired orphan not yet deleted, and a picture that only an order still references. `GET /api/files/{key}` takes no session and has no rate limit, and an order picture's key is shown to its buyer, so anyone with one order, or any approved seller with one expired orphan, can make each request cost one pass over every order item ever written. The table only grows (order pictures are kept for ever), so the cost per request grows with the business. What an attacker gains: database CPU and I/O exhaustion at a rate the attacker controls, which slows or stops checkout and every other page. At MVP data sizes each scan is cheap, which is why this is Medium and not High.
Reproduction: `tests/security/sec065-recheck.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-067": the documented case runs `EXPLAIN` of the `findUploadByKey` statement with `SET LOCAL enable_seqscan = off` and shows the plan still holds `Seq Scan on "OrderItem"` (no index exists to choose instead). The `it.fails` case, flipped in a scratch copy (deleted), fails with `expected 'Index Scan using "Upload_storageKey_k...' not to match /Seq Scan on "OrderItem"/`.
Remediation: add `@@index([productImageUploadId])` to `OrderItem` (a migration, owner database) and re-run the `EXPLAIN` case; consider in addition a rate limit or a cache for `GET /api/files/{key}` (TM-17 residual already lists the missing limit).
Re-check evidence (2026-10-20, [reviews/SEC-067-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-067-recheck.md)): `git show c55c575` adds `@@index([productImageUploadId])` to `OrderItem` and migration `20261020090000_order_item_upload_index` with one statement, `CREATE INDEX "OrderItem_productImageUploadId_idx" ON "OrderItem"("productImageUploadId")`; `src/modules/uploads/repository.ts` is unchanged, and its four statements (lines 82, 110, 144, 156) were read again. In `tests/security/sec065-recheck.itest.ts` the documented seq-scan case was removed and the `it.fails` case flipped to `it` with its assertion kept; the describe now also checks that the index exists in `pg_indexes` leading with `productImageUploadId`, and that the plans of all four statements (serving, cap count, cleanup lock, cleanup delete), with `enable_seqscan = off`, hold no `Seq Scan on "OrderItem"` and name `OrderItem_productImageUploadId_idx`. `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.integration.config.mts tests/security/sec065-recheck.itest.ts` -> 1 file, 20 passed, 1 expected fail (SEC-066). Negative control in a scratch file (deleted): inside a rolled-back transaction, `DROP INDEX "OrderItem_productImageUploadId_idx"` then the same `EXPLAIN` gives `Seq Scan on "OrderItem"` and no index name (1 passed), so the new cases fail without the index. `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.integration.config.mts tests/integration/uploads tests/security/t041-uploads.itest.ts tests/security/sec065-recheck.itest.ts` -> 3 files, 70 passed, 2 expected fail (SEC-064, SEC-066). Limits: the plans were taken on near-empty test tables with sequential scans disabled, which shows the index can serve each lookup, not the plan the planner picks on production statistics (on a large table an equality lookup on an indexed column is expected to use it). The `Restrict` foreign key check on `Upload` delete is internal to PostgreSQL and was not planned; it filters on the same leading column. `GET /api/files/{key}` still has no rate limit (TM-17 residual, unchanged).

### SEC-068 Pictures of products no admin approved are served to anyone, and attaching uploads to throw-away drafts lifts the upload cap
Severity: Medium
Status: OPEN. Item 1 (serving rule) FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-20 on branch `sec-068-serving-tests`, commit 1413db6, not merged to main; it is in force only once merged). Items 2 to 4 not re-checked. Item 1 amended by BR-41 (SEC-090, commit 027edfa, re-checked 2026-10-23): a file that is not public is served to the owning `SELLER` and, when a `ProductImage` row attaches it to a product, also to an active `ADMIN`; everyone else still gets the unknown-key 404, and an admin still gets it for an unattached upload, an orphan, and the logo of a store that is not public.
Owner: backend-api (`src/modules/uploads`, `src/modules/inventory`); owner or orchestrator for the product limit value
Location: src/modules/uploads/service.ts:155 (`openFile` serves any registered upload that is not an expired orphan); src/modules/uploads/repository.ts:71 and :101 (a `ProductImage` row of any product, whatever its status, makes an upload "referenced"); src/modules/inventory/service.ts:265 (`createProduct`: no limit on products per store, no rate limit)
Description: SEC-065 bounded the two attacker gains of an approved seller (disk use and free public hosting) by capping uploads that nothing references at 50 per store and by not serving them after 24 hours. T-042 adds a reference that costs nothing: a picture of a `DRAFT` product. A draft is never moderated (it enters the approval queue only when published, BR-36), no limit exists on how many products one store holds, and product writes have no rate limit. So a seller uploads 50 pictures, attaches them 8 at a time to 7 drafts, and the cap is free again; at the `uploads` rule of 30 an hour this is about 150 MB an hour, 3.5 GB a day, kept for ever, and every picture is served at `/api/files/{key}` to anyone, on the marketplace's own origin, without an admin ever seeing it. The same serving rule keeps the picture of a `REJECTED` or `TAKEN_DOWN` product public: an admin's takedown hides the product but not its picture, whose URL may already be shared (SEC-054 shows it to cart holders too). What an attacker with an approved seller account gains: SEC-065's gains (a) and (b) back, unbounded. Medium, as SEC-065 was: it needs an account an admin approved, and a suspension stops new uploads.
Reproduction: `tests/security/t042-inventory.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-068": documented case 1 creates a draft with a picture (status `DRAFT`, moderation `PENDING`) and a `TAKEN_DOWN` product with a picture; `GET /api/files/{key}` without a session answers 200 for both. Documented case 2 fills a store to 50 unattached uploads (upload POST is 409 `UPLOAD_LIMIT`), attaches them to 7 drafts through `POST /seller/products`, and the next upload POST is 201. The two `it.fails` cases assert 404 for an anonymous request of a draft's picture and of a taken-down product's picture; flipped in a scratch copy (deleted), both fail with `expected 200 to be 404`.
Remediation: (1) serve a picture to a request without the owning seller's session only when it is a picture of a product that is visible (BR-05), a logo of a public store, or an order item picture; the owning seller's dashboard reads its own pictures with its session (or through a short-lived signed URL); (2) a per-store limit on products that are not `ARCHIVED` (a value for the owner to rule, for example 1,000) and on total upload bytes, refused with a 409 before anything is written; (3) a rate limit on `POST /seller/products` (section 6); (4) an admin takedown of an upload (SEC-065 item 4). (1) alone removes the hosting gain; (2) is needed for the disk gain. The `it.fails` cases may be adapted to the chosen serving rule (for example, call with the owner's cookie and expect 200, without a session expect 404); the security agent re-checks that change.
Re-check evidence, item 1 (2026-10-20, commit 1413db6 on branch `backend/sec-068-serving`, tests on branch `sec-068-serving-tests`, **not merged to main**): status of item 1 FIXED on that branch; the finding stays OPEN as a whole (item 2 per-store product limit built in a0605df, not re-checked here; total upload bytes, item 3 rate limit, and item 4 not built). Read: `git show 1413db6` (`src/modules/uploads/{service,repository,index}.ts`, `src/app/api/files/[key]/route.ts`, API_CONTRACTS 5.8), `src/modules/catalog/visibility.ts`, `src/server/auth/session.ts` (`getActor`, `loadActor`: an inactive user is no actor; `storeId` from the user's own store), and `resolveOwnUploads` in `src/modules/inventory/repository.ts` (an upload is attached only by its own store). `fileLookupStatement` reuses `VISIBLE_PRODUCT_SQL` with the aliases it requires, and `isLogoOfPublicStore` reuses `PUBLIC_STORE_WHERE`, so no second definition of "visible" exists; `mayServe` allows a non-public file only to `role === "SELLER"` with `actor.storeId === upload.storeId`; the route passes `() => getActor(request)` and nothing from the request but the key. Changed seven assertions that encoded the old rule "every registered upload is public" (`t041-uploads.test.ts` route guard: `getActor` now allowed, only as the resolver handed to `openFile`, `requireActor`/`cookies()`/`headers()`/role or store checks still refused; `t041-uploads.itest.ts`: owner cookie plus an anonymous 404 in each case, the SEC-064 `it.fails` now asserts the 200 first so it cannot pass on a 404 body, SEC-065 cases also ask as the owner so the expiry is what answers; `sec065-recheck.itest.ts` grace boundary read as the owner, anonymous 404 on both sides). New `tests/security/sec068-recheck.itest.ts`, written from this remediation, not from the author's cases: for an unattached upload and for pictures of DRAFT, PENDING, REJECTED, TAKEN_DOWN, ARCHIVED products and of an approved product in an inactive category, anonymous, customer, admin, another seller, and a forged session cookie get a 404 identical to an unknown key's, and the owner gets 200 with `nosniff`, sandbox CSP, `no-store`, no `Set-Cookie`, no CORS header; a suspended store's picture and logo are read by its suspended owner only (BR-14); a deactivated owner's files are 404 to everyone, the owner's remaining session included; a seller demoted to `CUSTOMER` loses access; another seller naming the owner's store in the query or in headers gets 404; a visible product's picture, a public store's logo, and an order item's picture are 200 to all six callers; takedown and re-approval, suspension and reinstatement, category off and on each switch public serving at once, with the `Upload` row unchanged; one upload cannot be linked to a second, visible product. Commands: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/t041-uploads.test.ts` -> 24 passed; `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.integration.config.mts tests/integration/uploads tests/security/t041-uploads.itest.ts tests/security/sec065-recheck.itest.ts tests/security/t042-inventory.itest.ts tests/security/sec068-recheck.itest.ts` -> 5 files, 106 passed, 3 expected fail (SEC-064, SEC-066, SEC-071). Negative controls in scratch copies (deleted): with `findUploadByKey` mocked by `vi.mock` to report every upload public (the old rule), 15 of the 15 owner-only cases fail with `expected 200 to be 404`; the SEC-071 `it.fails` flipped to `it` fails with `expected 200 to be 404`. Limits: route handlers called in process, not through `next start`; the admin moderation screens (T-050) cannot show pictures of products awaiting approval (API_CONTRACTS 5.8 records this; a rule is needed with T-050); an order item's picture stays public after a takedown (new SEC-071).
Re-check evidence, BR-41 amendment (2026-10-23, commit 027edfa on main, tree 263d89b): `tests/security/sec068-recheck.itest.ts` was changed by backend-api. `expectOwnerOnly` gained an `admin` argument: "admin reads (product picture)" for the six product-state cases, the suspended store's picture, the takedown, the inactive category, and the one-upload-two-products case; "admin refused" for the unattached upload and both logos. In the deactivated-owner case the admin reads the product picture and is refused the logo. Every other caller (anonymous, customer, another seller, forged cookie) keeps the identical unknown-key 404, and the admin's 200 is checked by the same `expectServed` as the owner's (type, `nosniff`, sandbox CSP, `no-store`, no `Set-Cookie`, no CORS header, 500 bytes). Judged a faithful translation of BR-41, not a weakening. Result: `pnpm test:integration tests/security/sec068-recheck.itest.ts ...` passes (see SEC-090 re-check evidence).

### SEC-069 One seller can fill the admin approval queue: no limit on products, publishes, or product writes
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN
Owner: backend-api (`src/modules/inventory`, `src/server/rate-limit.ts`); owner for the limit values
Location: src/modules/inventory/service.ts:265 (`createProduct`) and :413 (`changeProductStatus`; publishing a `PENDING` product sets `moderationRequestedAt`, its place in the queue); docs/API_CONTRACTS.md section 6 (no rule for seller product endpoints)
Description: every product a seller publishes while it is `PENDING` enters the admin approval queue (BR-36), and nothing limits how many products one store creates, publishes, or re-submits, or how fast. One approved seller can put thousands of products in the queue in minutes (each edit of moderated content or unpublish and publish also re-queues it), so the products of honest sellers wait behind them, or an admin approves in bulk without looking. What an attacker gains: delay of other sellers' listings, and cover for a harmful product among many harmless ones. No data of another tenant is reached and an admin can suspend the store, so Low.
Reproduction: `tests/security/t042-inventory.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-069": 60 creates and 60 publishes in a row from one seller are all accepted (no 429, no 409), and 60 products are `PUBLISHED` and `PENDING`, that is, in the queue.
Remediation: the per-store product limit of SEC-068 item 2; a rate limit on product create and on publish per seller (for example 60 an hour), with `Retry-After`; and in T-050, an admin queue that can be filtered by store so one store's flood can be set aside.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-070 The public slug is made from the first title and never changes, so words no admin approved stay in the product's URL
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN
Owner: backend-api (`src/modules/inventory`); admin moderation (T-050/T-051) for showing the slug
Location: src/modules/inventory/service.ts:286 (`slug: generateSlug(input.title)` on create); `updateProduct` never changes the slug (API_CONTRACTS 5.7.2.1 "Slug")
Description: a product's slug is its first title, lower-cased with letters and digits kept, plus a random suffix, and it is never changed. A seller can create a draft titled, for example, "Pay LINE scamid123 direct", never publish it (so no admin sees that title), change the title to "Plain shirt", and publish. The admin approves "Plain shirt"; the public URL is `/products/pay-line-scamid123-direct-<suffix>`, shown in the browser's address bar, in shared links, and in search results. What an attacker gains: up to 40 characters of unmoderated text (a contact handle, an off-platform payment instruction, an insult) on every view of an approved product. Letters and digits only, no markup, no link, so Low.
Reproduction: `tests/security/t042-inventory.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-070": the documented case creates the product with that title (slug starts `pay-line-scamid123-direct-`), changes the title, and shows the slug unchanged.
Remediation: either (1) while a product has never been `APPROVED`, regenerate the slug from the title on every title change (no public URL exists yet, so nothing breaks), and freeze it at the first approval; or (2) make the slug suffix-only, or (3) show the slug in the admin approval view (T-050) and treat it as moderated content. (1) and (3) together are the smallest change that keeps readable URLs.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-071 An ordered picture stays public after its product is taken down
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN
Owner: backend-api (`src/modules/uploads`); owner for who may read an order's picture
Location: src/modules/uploads/repository.ts `fileLookupStatement` (`EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "OrderItem" o WHERE o."productImageUploadId" = f."id")` makes the file public with no other condition), on branch `backend/sec-068-serving` at 1413db6; src/modules/checkout/repository.ts:330 (checkout stores the product's own primary upload id, not a copy)
Description: SEC-068 item 1 serves an order item's picture to anyone. Checkout records the product's primary picture by its upload id (DATA_MODEL 6.12: the same upload, kept for ever by a `Restrict` key), so once any order holds a product's picture, that picture is public for good: an admin takedown, a store suspension, or a deactivated owner no longer stops its serving, although API_CONTRACTS 5.8 "Who may read" states that a takedown "stops the public serving of the pictures at once". The key was public while the product was visible (product pages, shared links, SEC-054 cart holders), so it is widely known. What an attacker gains: a picture an admin took down (for example a counterfeit or illegal image, approved by mistake) stays reachable on the marketplace's own origin by anyone with the URL; a seller needs only one order of the product, which it can place itself while the product is visible. Low: the picture passed an admin approval once, and the gain is persistence of content, not access to data of another tenant.
Reproduction: `tests/security/sec068-recheck.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-071": the documented case creates a visible product with a picture, an order item holding that picture, takes the product down, and shows the picture still 200 to anonymous, customer, admin, another seller, a forged cookie, and the owner. The `it.fails` case asserts 404 for a request without a session; flipped in a scratch copy (deleted), it fails with `expected 200 to be 404`.
Remediation: serve an order item's picture without conditions only while it is also otherwise public; else serve it only to the buyer of an order that holds it, to the selling store's seller, and (with T-050) to an admin, and 404 to everyone else. Which callers keep it is for the owner to rule; the `it.fails` case asserts only the minimum (no session, 404). Alternatively, an admin takedown of the upload itself (SEC-065 item 4) could mark it and refuse it to everyone but the order's parties.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-072 A seller marks an order delivered in seconds, which unlocks reviews for orders it placed through a second account
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN
Owner: owner (rule for when a seller may set `DELIVERED`); backend-api (`src/modules/orders`); the reviews task (BR-11)
Location: src/modules/orders/state.ts:60 (`SHIPPED` to `DELIVERED` by `SELLER`, no other condition); src/modules/orders/seller-service.ts:105 (`changeSellerOrderStatus`, no time or confirmation check); REQUIREMENTS BR-11 (a review is allowed once the item's seller order is `DELIVERED`)
Description: shipping is simulated and the fulfilment table lets the seller make every move from `PENDING` to `DELIVERED` with nothing between them: no carrier event, no buyer confirmation, no minimum time after `SHIPPED`. BR-11 makes `DELIVERED` the one condition for a review. BR-16 stops a seller buying from its own account only; a second customer account costs one sign-up, and with the mock provider any account gets `PAID` orders at no cost (SEC-056). So a seller can buy its own product from a second account, take the seller order to `DELIVERED` in three requests within a second, and, once reviews exist, post a review for each such item. What an attacker gains: fake reviews and rating at the price of the goods (nothing with the mock provider; with a real provider the price minus what the store receives), and fake "delivered" orders in its own figures. The review endpoint is not built yet, the gain is reputation, not data or money of another tenant, and an admin can suspend the store, so Low.
Reproduction: as one approved seller S with a published, approved product P, and a second customer account C: (1) C adds P to the cart and checks out; with the mock provider the order becomes `PAID`; (2) S sends `POST /api/v1/seller/orders/{id}/status` with `{"to":"PROCESSING"}`, then `{"to":"SHIPPED"}` (no carrier or tracking needed), then `{"to":"DELIVERED"}`; each answers 200 and the third sets `Shipment.deliveredAt` to the same second. The author's integration case `tests/integration/seller-orders/endpoints.test.ts` "moves PENDING, PROCESSING, SHIPPED (simulated shipment), DELIVERED" makes these three moves back to back with no delay and passes (run 2026-10-20, 35 passed). `tests/security/t043-seller-orders.test.ts` "five moves ..." pins the table that allows it.
Remediation: for the owner to choose one or more: (1) a seller may set `DELIVERED` only a minimum time after `SHIPPED` (for example 24 hours, compared with `Shipment.shippedAt` from the database clock in the guarded statement); (2) the buyer confirms receipt, or the system sets `DELIVERED` after N days without a complaint, and the seller cannot; (3) the reviews task adds its own guard (for example a minimum age of the order, or reviews held for moderation when buyer and seller share signals such as sign-up address). (1) is the smallest change in this module; (3) is needed anyway once a real provider exists.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-073 A missing or wrong upload root is reported as a successful cleanup, and the bytes on the real volume stay for good
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-21, commit 8a57d53). Items (1) and (2) of the remediation are fixed. Item (3), alerting on `filesMissing` and on the failure line, belongs to devops and is not built. The residual of an upload made while the volume is not mounted is an owner decision (residual risks, "Upload lifetime")
Owner: backend-api (`src/modules/uploads/cleanup.ts`, `storage.ts`); devops (volume mount, alerting)
Location: src/modules/uploads/cleanup.ts:66-79 (rows deleted first, then `storage.delete(key)` per key; success counted as `filesDeleted`); src/modules/uploads/storage.ts:126-133 (`delete` treats `ENOENT` and `ENOTDIR` as success); src/modules/uploads/cleanup.ts:43 ("a file already missing counts as deleted")
Description: the scheduled cleanup (wired in 37d07c8) deletes up to 1,000 `Upload` rows per run and process, then unlinks each file under `UPLOAD_DIR`. It never checks that the root it unlinks in is the one that holds the files. When `UPLOAD_DIR` is absolute and valid but wrong for this process (the volume is not mounted yet, so the path is an empty directory or absent; a typo; a second instance with its own local disk, which DEPLOYMENT.md already calls wrong), every `unlink` is `ENOENT`, counted as a deleted file, with no warn line; the result line reads like a healthy run. The real files lose their rows for good: no job removes a file without a row (SEC-066), and no log line names them. With a root of `/` or `C:\` every path is refused (`TypeError`) and counted failed, but the rows are still deleted first. Only expired orphans are affected, never a referenced upload, so no data is exposed or lost that was meant to be kept. What an attacker gains: nothing directly; the harm is silent disk growth outside the per-store bound of SEC-065, on a misconfiguration the logs do not reveal. Low.
Reproduction: `tests/security/sec065-cleanup-schedule.test.ts`, describe "SEC-073": the documented case points `LocalDiskStorage` at an absolute directory that does not exist, stubs the row deletion with two keys, and shows `deleteRows` called and the result `{ rowsDeleted: 2, filesDeleted: 2, filesFailed: 0 }` with no warn line; the file system root case shows two rows deleted, two files failed. The `it.fails` case asserts that no row is deleted when the root does not exist; flipped in a scratch copy (deleted), it fails with `expected "vi.fn()" to not be called at all, but actually been called 1 times`.
Remediation: (1) before the first batch, check the root: it exists, is a directory, and holds a marker the upload path writes once (for example `<UPLOAD_DIR>/.marketplace-uploads`); if not, delete no row, log one error line (class only), and stop the run; (2) count a missing file separately (`filesMissing`) and log one warn line per run when it is above zero, so a wrong root shows in the logs; (3) devops: alert on `filesMissing` above zero and on the failure line, and document that `UPLOAD_DIR` must be the same shared volume for every process that runs the job. (1) alone makes the `it.fails` case pass; the case may be adapted to call the marker check first, and the security agent re-checks that change.
Re-check evidence (2026-10-21, commit 8a57d53; contract API_CONTRACTS.md section 5.8.1 "Storage check first"):
- Code read. `src/modules/uploads/cleanup.ts:75-76`: the run throws `UploadStorageNotReadyError` when the adapter has no `assertReady`, then awaits `assertReady()`. Both happen before `deleteRows` is first called. `storage.ts:144-155`: `assertReady` stats the root and `<root>/.marketplace-uploads` and passes only for a directory holding a regular file. Every other outcome gives one error with no path in it. `storage.ts:134-141,159`: `put` calls `prepareRoot()` after the key check, which writes the marker with `wx`, so an existing marker is never overwritten. `storage.ts:186-195`: `delete` resolves `false` on `ENOENT` and `ENOTDIR`. `cleanup.ts:88,99-103`: `false` is counted as `filesMissing`, and one warn line per run holds `{ filesMissing }` only. An adapter that resolves nothing is still counted as deleted, which is documented and harmless because no such adapter exists.
- The test change was not weakened. `git show 8a57d53 -- tests/security/sec065-cleanup-schedule.test.ts`: the SEC-073 `it.fails` was changed to `it` with its body unchanged. The three "(documented)" cases were rewritten to the fixed behaviour. The old "rows deleted, filesDeleted 2" case became "a directory without the marker: no row deleted, throws". The file-system-root case now expects no row deleted. A new case covers a marked root whose files are missing. Each rewritten case asserts at least as much as before. The link cases call `prepareRoot()` first, which only adds the marker, so they test the same link behaviour. Commit 177be85 (`git show 177be85`) changed 2 lines of `sec065-recheck.itest.ts`: `filesDeleted >= 1` became `filesMissing >= 1` for a row whose bytes are gone, which is the intended change. It also added 1 line to `tests/integration/uploads/endpoints.test.ts`, passing `assertReady` through on the wrapping stub. Neither change weakens a test.
- Baseline: `pnpm exec vitest run src/modules/uploads/cleanup.test.ts src/modules/uploads/storage.test.ts tests/security/sec065-cleanup-schedule.test.ts` gave 3 files and 38 of 38 passed.
- Mutation run. Script `mutate073.ps1` in the session scratchpad. Each control was removed one at a time with the same three files run after each. After each run the file was restored from its saved bytes and the SHA-256 compared: identical all 11 times, and `git status --short -- src/modules/uploads` was clean afterwards. Results:
  - C1, drop the "no `assertReady`" refusal: 1 failed.
  - C2, drop `await deps.storage.assertReady()`: 5 failed.
  - C3, count a missing file as deleted: 2 failed.
  - C4, never write the `filesMissing` warn line: 2 failed.
  - S1, drop `prepareRoot()` from `put`: 2 failed.
  - S2b, drop `marker.isFile()`: 1 failed.
  - S3, make `assertReady` return at once: 5 failed.
  - S4, make `delete` of a missing file return `true`: 2 failed.
  - S5, write the marker with `w` instead of `wx`: 1 failed.
  - S6, rethrow the raw `stat` error instead of the path-free class: 4 failed.
  - S2a, drop `root.isDirectory()`: 38 passed. This is an equivalent mutant. When the root is a file, `stat(<file>/.marketplace-uploads)` throws (`ENOENT` on Windows, checked with node; `ENOTDIR` on POSIX), so the marker check alone already refuses it.
- New independent file `tests/security/sec073-recheck.test.ts`, 7 plain cases, all pass:
  - Through `runUploadCleanup` with a real `LocalDiskStorage`, three roots are refused: a missing root, an empty directory (an unmounted mount point), and a directory with files but no marker. Each run writes exactly one error line `{ component: "upload-cleanup", errorClass: "UploadStorageNotReadyError" }` and no info line, deletes no row, and logs no part of the temporary path.
  - An adapter without `assertReady`, and one whose `assertReady` throws its own error class, both delete no row.
  - A marked root with three missing files gives `{ rowsDeleted: 3, filesDeleted: 0, filesMissing: 3, filesFailed: 0 }`, one warn line `{ filesMissing: 3 }`, and an info line that carries `filesMissing`.
  - "(documented)": the first `put` into an existing empty mount point writes the marker, and the cleanup then runs there. This is the residual left for the owner.
- Integration: `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.integration.config.mts tests/integration/seller-isolation/endpoints.test.ts tests/integration/uploads/endpoints.test.ts tests/security/sec065-recheck.itest.ts` gave 3 files, 100 passed and 1 expected fail. The upload cleanup cases against a real database and real temporary roots pass with the marker rule in place.
- What remains:
  - Devops: alert on `filesMissing > 0` and on the cleanup failure line (remediation item 3).
  - Owner decision, recorded and not fixed: a `put` made while the volume is unmounted writes the marker into the empty mount point (residual risks, "Upload lifetime").
  - Not observed under `next start` or on a deployment volume.

### SEC-074 The e2e driver-override check reads env files with another parser than the e2e server, and skips `.env.test*`
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-22)
Owner: qa (`e2e/support/environment.ts`, `playwright.config.ts`)
Location: e2e/support/environment.ts:39 (`.env` read with `node:util` `parseEnv`), :47 (`OTHER_NEXT_ENV_FILES` lists `.env.local`, `.env.development`, `.env.development.local` only), :54 (same parser for those); playwright.config.ts:34-40 (`webServer.env` sets neither `PGOPTIONS` nor `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE`)
Description: the e2e harness refuses to run when `PGOPTIONS` or `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE` is set in the environment or in an env file (CR-41, CR-146), because the e2e `next dev` server reads those files itself and `pg` applies both on top of the validated URL. The check parses the files with Node's `parseEnv`. The server parses them with `@next/env` 16.4.0, which uses dotenv. The two parsers disagree, so a file can set a variable for the server that the check does not see. Three differences were shown with Node 24.20.0: (a) a UTF-8 byte order mark before the first line: `parseEnv` keeps it in the key (`U+FEFFPGOPTIONS`), `@next/env` yields `PGOPTIONS`; (b) lone CR line ends: `parseEnv` reads `A=1\rPGOPTIONS=...\rNODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE=1` as one variable `A`, `@next/env` yields all three; (c) the colon form `PGOPTIONS: -c role=x`: `parseEnv` yields a key `PGOPTIONS: -c role`, `@next/env` yields `PGOPTIONS`. CRLF and `export ` lines parse the same in both. Also, when `NODE_ENV=test` is in the environment of `pnpm test:e2e`, `next dev` keeps it (`node_modules/next/dist/bin/next`: `process.env.NODE_ENV = process.env.NODE_ENV || defaultEnv`), and `@next/env` then loads `.env.test.local`, `.env.test`, and `.env`, not `.env.local` (shown: a `.env.test` holding `PGOPTIONS` is loaded). The check reads neither `.env.test` file. What can go wrong: a `PGOPTIONS` such as `-c role=...` or a forced libpq binding reaches the e2e server's connection, which the validated URL then no longer describes. The files are local and git-ignored, so only someone who already controls the developer's working tree can plant one; the gain is an e2e run against a session the checks did not approve, not access an attacker lacked. Low.
Correction to the reported scope: the same byte order mark gap was suspected in tests/integration/setup/global-setup.ts:72. It does not hold there. That check uses `parseEnv`, and the only loader of `.env` it guards is `process.loadEnvFile` in prisma7.config.ts:13, which is the same Node parser. Shown: `process.loadEnvFile` on a file starting with a byte order mark sets `U+FEFFPGOPTIONS` (10 characters), not `PGOPTIONS`, so `pg` never sees it. The integration check and its loader agree, so this part is not part of the finding.
Reproduction: scratch scripts (not committed) loading `node_modules/.pnpm/@next+env@16.4.0/node_modules/@next/env/dist/index.js`. `loadEnvConfig(dir, true, logger, true)` on a temporary directory whose `.env` is `U+FEFFPGOPTIONS=-c role=x\nA=1\n` gives `combinedEnv.PGOPTIONS = "-c role=x"`, while `parseEnv` of the same text gives `{"A":"1","U+FEFFPGOPTIONS":"-c role=x"}`. With `.env` `A=1\rPGOPTIONS=-c role=x\rNODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE=1\n`: `@next/env` gives both variables, `parseEnv` gives `{"A":"1PGOPTIONS=-c role=xNODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE=1"}`. With `.env` `PGOPTIONS: -c role=x`: `@next/env` gives `PGOPTIONS`, `parseEnv` gives `{"PGOPTIONS: -c role":"x"}`. In a fresh process with `NODE_ENV=test` and a directory holding `.env.test` with `PGOPTIONS=-c role=x` and `.env.local` with `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE=1`, `loadEnvConfig(dir, true)` loads `[".env.test"]` and gives `PGOPTIONS`. In each case `assertNoDriverEnvironmentOverride` on the `parseEnv` result finds no `PGOPTIONS` or `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE` key, so it does not refuse. Not run end to end through `pnpm test:e2e`.
Remediation: either (1) read the files for the check with `loadEnvConfig` of `@next/env` (dev mode, the same `NODE_ENV` the server gets), so the check sees exactly what the server loads, including the `.env.test*` set; or (2) set `PGOPTIONS: ""` and `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE: ""` in `webServer.env`. `@next/env` does not override a variable that is already present, and `pg` 8.23.1 treats an empty `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE` as false (`!!process.env.NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE`, lib/index.js:41). Shown: an in-process `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE = ""` survived `loadEnvConfig` over a file that set it to `1`, and a `spawnSync` child on Windows received `PGOPTIONS` as present and `""`. Not shown: that the empty values survive Playwright's shell, `pnpm exec`, and `next dev` on Windows. Re-check this end to end. (2) is the smaller change and does not depend on the parser. (1) keeps the refusal message. Both may be combined. Also consider refusing `NODE_ENV` values other than unset or `development` for the e2e run.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-22, commit d729de0): both remediation options were taken. Option 2 is the control that closes the finding; option 1 has a gap, registered as SEC-078.
- Code read. `e2e/support/server-environment.ts:35-38`: `webServer.env` sets `PGOPTIONS: ""`, `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE: ""`, and `NODE_ENV: "development"`. `playwright.config.ts:30,37-42` passes `e2eServerCommand()` and `e2eServerEnvironment(...)`. Playwright 1.64.0 merges `{ ...process.env, ...webServer.env }` and spawns with `shell: true` (`node_modules/.pnpm/playwright@1.64.0/node_modules/playwright/lib/runner/index.js:870-879`). `next dev` forks its server with its initial environment (`next/dist/cli/next-dev.js:362,393-401`). `pg` 8.23.1 sends `options` only when truthy (`lib/client.js:567`) and forces native only on a truthy value (`lib/index.js:41`). `e2e/support/environment.ts:47-79` now scans all six `NEXT_ENV_FILE_NAMES`, including `.env.test` and `.env.test.local`, with `scanEnvText`.
- Launch chain, the part the finding left open. A scratch script in the session scratchpad (`s74/chain.cjs`) spawns `pnpm exec node` with `shell: true`, then forks, then runs `@next/env` `loadEnvConfig(dir, true)`. The directory holds a `.env.development.local` that sets `PGOPTIONS` in a form the scanner misses (SEC-078). Control, without the empty values: the leaf reads `PGOPTIONS` from the file. With the values of `e2eServerEnvironment`: `PGOPTIONS` and `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE` are present and `""` after `loadEnvConfig`, and `NODE_ENV` is `development` even when the parent has `NODE_ENV=test`. This is now the plain test "the empty driver values survive the launch chain" in `tests/security/sec074-077-recheck.test.ts`. Mutation: deleting the `PGOPTIONS: ""` line, or setting `NODE_ENV: "test"`, in `server-environment.ts` makes that test fail (1 failed each). The file was restored and its SHA-256 compared: same before and after.
- End to end through `pnpm test:e2e`. A git-ignored `.env.development.local` was planted in the repository root. Its single line is `SEC074_PROBE='1'`, then U+2028, then `PGOPTIONS=-c default_transaction_read_only=on`. The scanner does not refuse it (SEC-078), and `@next/env` reads `PGOPTIONS` from it. Positive control (`s74/control.cjs`): with those options, `pg` against `marketplace_e2e` answers `25006 cannot execute UPDATE in a read-only transaction`; without them the write succeeds. Run with the file planted: `19 passed (33.9s)`, exit 0. The sign-up and seed writes succeeded, so the server's connection did not carry the planted `PGOPTIONS`. The file was removed afterwards (`Test-Path` False). An earlier run with `-c search_path=...` also passed, but proves nothing: Prisma qualifies table names with the schema.
- Scanner against `@next/env` (`s74/diff.ts`, 22 texts). The byte order mark, lone CR, colon form, tabs, `export` with a tab, a backtick value, a lower-case name, a leading U+2028, and a leading no-break space are all refused where the server reads them. Exception: a quoted value followed by U+2028 or U+2029 on the same line (SEC-078). Ten of those cases are plain tests in the new file.
- Tests: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/unit/e2e/env-file-scan.test.ts tests/unit/e2e/server-environment.test.ts tests/unit/e2e/server-identity.test.ts` gave 3 files, 33 passed. `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec074-077-recheck.test.ts` gave 18 passed and 2 expected fail (SEC-078).
- Not shown: the same chain under a POSIX shell. The test runs on whatever platform runs the security project.

### SEC-075 The e2e server uses the development `UPLOAD_DIR` and `AUTH_SECRET`
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-22)
Owner: qa (`playwright.config.ts`)
Location: playwright.config.ts:34-40 (`webServer.env` sets `DATABASE_URL`, `APP_URL`, `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED`, `NEXT_DIST_DIR` only; line numbers are as of the finding, before d729de0); `.env.example`, entries `UPLOAD_DIR` and `AUTH_SECRET` (named rather than numbered because the file changes; re-checked 2026-10-28 against c711239: `UPLOAD_DIR` is still present, empty, with instructions to set an absolute directory, and `AUTH_SECRET` still holds the local throwaway value)
Description: the e2e `next dev` server has its own database and its own build directory, but it takes `UPLOAD_DIR` and `AUTH_SECRET` from the parent environment or from `.env` (and `.env.local` and the other files `@next/env` loads), the same values `pnpm dev` uses. Playwright 1.64.0 merges `process.env` under `webServer.env` (lib/runner/index.js). Effects: (a) an e2e upload writes its bytes into the development upload root. global-setup empties every e2e table each run, so those files lose their rows, and the orphan cleanup, which walks rows only (SEC-066), never removes them. They stay in the development root with no row in any database. The cleanup's root check (SEC-073) cannot tell the two servers apart, because both write the same marker into the same root. (b) Cookies on `localhost` are not scoped by port, so the e2e and development servers receive each other's session cookies, signed with the same secret. Session lookups go to each server's own database, and `cookieCache` is disabled (src/server/auth/auth.ts:145). So such a cookie authenticates nobody on the other server today. The isolation then rests on the database lookup alone, not also on the signature. What an attacker gains: nothing today. No e2e spec uploads yet (`e2e/stacked-table.spec.ts` only, and tests/e2e-support/database.ts writes no upload). The harm is leftover test files in the development upload root once upload journeys land, and a weaker separation if a cookie-only session feature is turned on later. Low.
Reproduction: read playwright.config.ts:34-40: no `UPLOAD_DIR` or `AUTH_SECRET` key. With `UPLOAD_DIR` and `AUTH_SECRET` set in `.env` (as `.env.example` instructs), `next dev` loads both for any port. The e2e server therefore resolves the same root (src/modules/uploads/config.ts:31) and the same secret (src/server/env.ts:540) as the development server. Not run: no e2e journey uploads today.
Remediation: in `webServer.env`, set `UPLOAD_DIR` to an e2e-only absolute directory under the repository (for example `<REPO_ROOT>/.e2e-uploads`, git-ignored), emptied by global-setup together with the tables. Set `AUTH_SECRET` to a value generated per run in playwright.config.ts (32 or more random characters, never logged). Optionally use `127.0.0.1` for the e2e origin, so its cookies are not sent to the `localhost` development server.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-22, commit d729de0):
- Code read. `e2e/support/server-environment.ts:31-32`: `webServer.env` sets `UPLOAD_DIR` to `E2E_UPLOAD_DIR` and `AUTH_SECRET` to the value from the config. `playwright.config.ts:41` makes that value `randomBytes(32).toString("hex")` per load of the config: 64 characters, above `AUTH_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH` (32, `src/server/env.ts:85`). It is not logged anywhere in the config or global-setup. `e2e/support/constants.ts:31`: `E2E_UPLOAD_DIR` is `<REPO_ROOT>/e2e/.auth/uploads`. `e2e/global-setup.ts:116-117` removes and recreates that directory after the tables are emptied, so no e2e file outlives its row and the marker is rewritten by the first `put`. `@next/env` does not override a set variable, so neither `.env` value reaches the e2e server.
- `git check-ignore -v e2e/.auth/uploads/x.webp e2e/.auth/uploads/.marketplace-uploads` gave `e2e/.gitignore:2:.auth/` for both.
- Cookies: the e2e origin is now `http://127.0.0.1:3100` (`constants.ts:19-20`). Browsers do not send a `localhost` cookie to `127.0.0.1`, or the reverse, so the two servers no longer receive each other's cookies. Their secrets differ in any case.
- Tests: `tests/unit/e2e/server-environment.test.ts` (part of the 33 passed above). In `tests/security/sec074-077-recheck.test.ts`, the upload root is checked with `git check-ignore` and against `process.env.UPLOAD_DIR`, and the config text is checked for `randomBytes(32)`. All pass.
- The e2e run (`19 passed`) started a server with this environment, so `src/server/env.ts` accepted the generated secret and the upload root.
- Not observed: an e2e upload. No spec uploads yet, so the bytes landing in `e2e/.auth/uploads` is shown by code reading only.

### SEC-076 The e2e `next dev` server listens on every network interface
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-22)
Owner: qa (`playwright.config.ts`)
Location: playwright.config.ts:28 (`command: \`pnpm exec next dev --port ${E2E_PORT}\``, no `--hostname`); e2e/support/constants.ts:19-23 (the seller's email and password, in source)
Description: `next dev` without `-H`/`--hostname` binds `0.0.0.0` (Next 16.4.0 CLI reference, `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/06-cli/next.md:71`, "Default: 0.0.0.0"). During `pnpm test:e2e`, any host on the same network can reach port 3100, if the host firewall allows it. That host reaches a development-mode server (verbose errors, source maps, the dev overlay) whose database holds a seller account. The seller's email and password are committed in e2e/support/constants.ts. global-setup gives that account an approved store and products. What an attacker on the same network gains, during a run: a signed-in seller session on a throwaway database, and dev-mode error detail of the current source. It can also change data the running specs read, which makes a run fail or pass for the wrong reason. No production or development data is reachable. The `pnpm dev` script (package.json:7, `next dev`) has the same default; it is outside this finding's scope and is not recorded separately here. Low.
Reproduction: read playwright.config.ts:28 and the CLI reference above. Not run: binding was not observed with `netstat` during a live run.
Remediation: `command: \`pnpm exec next dev --hostname 127.0.0.1 --port ${E2E_PORT}\``. Playwright's port probe tries both `127.0.0.1` and `::1` (lib/runner/index.js `isPortUsed`), so readiness still works. `E2E_ORIGIN` is `http://localhost:3100`, so check that Chromium and the API request context reach the IPv4 listener on Windows (Node's `autoSelectFamily` and Chromium both fall back from `::1`). Otherwise change `E2E_ORIGIN` and `APP_URL` to `http://127.0.0.1:3100` together. Re-check with `Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 3100 -State Listen` during a run: only `127.0.0.1` is listed.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-22, commit d729de0):
- Code read. `e2e/support/server-environment.ts:12-14`: the command is `pnpm exec next dev --hostname 127.0.0.1 --port 3100`. `E2E_ORIGIN` and `APP_URL` are `http://127.0.0.1:3100`.
- Observed during a live `pnpm test:e2e` run: `Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 3100 -State Listen` listed one listener, `127.0.0.1:3100`. There was none on `0.0.0.0` or `::`. The process tree showed `next dev --hostname 127.0.0.1 --port 3100` under Playwright's `cmd.exe /d /s /c`. The run passed (19 of 19), so Chromium and the API request context reach the IPv4 listener on Windows.
- Tests: `tests/unit/e2e/server-environment.test.ts`, and "binds and names IPv4 loopback only" in `tests/security/sec074-077-recheck.test.ts`. Both pass.
- Unchanged and outside this finding: `pnpm dev` (`next dev`) still binds every interface.

### SEC-077 e2e setup signs up and signs in on port 3100 before anything proves it is the e2e server
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-22)
Owner: qa (`e2e/global-setup.ts`)
Location: e2e/global-setup.ts:112-128 (`POST /api/v1/auth/sign-up`, then seed, then `POST /api/v1/auth/sign-in`, all to `E2E_ORIGIN`); playwright.config.ts:31 (readiness is `port: E2E_PORT`, any listener); tests/e2e-support/database.ts:70 (seed promotes the user by email in the e2e database)
Description: global-setup's `check` step proves the database the child processes reach. Nothing proves that the process answering `http://localhost:3100` is the `next dev` that Playwright started with `DATABASE_URL = E2E_DATABASE_URL`. Playwright refuses a port that is already in use before it starts the server (`reuseExistingServer: false`; `isPortUsed` probes `127.0.0.1` and `::1`). After that, readiness is only "something listens on 3100". Another process can take the port in the window between that probe and the server binding: a second `pnpm dev --port 3100`, a second e2e run, or any local program. The started server then fails on `EADDRINUSE`. If the readiness probe succeeds against the other listener before Playwright sees the child exit, global-setup sends the seller sign-up (name, email, password) and later the sign-in to that process. If it is a development server, the e2e seller account is created in the development database. The seed step then fails, because the user is not in the e2e database, but only after that write. If it is not this application, the request and the password went to it. The account and password are throwaway constants committed to the repository, so nothing secret leaks; the harm is a write to the wrong database, which the e2e isolation (CR-41, CR-128, CR-134) exists to prevent. Low: it needs a local process on the port and a timing window.
Reproduction: read e2e/global-setup.ts:101-121. No request before the sign-up checks the identity of the server. Not run: the race was not staged.
Remediation: before the sign-up, prove that the server on `E2E_ORIGIN` reads the e2e database with a read-only request. Example: the `check` step, or a new child step, writes a canary that a public endpoint shows, using a random value created per run (such as an approved store or category with that name or slug). global-setup then reads it through `E2E_ORIGIN` and refuses to continue without it. Remove the canary afterwards. This needs no application change. Alternatively, pass a random per-run value in `webServer.env` and compare it with a value the server exposes only in development. That needs an application route, so the first option is preferred.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-22, commit d729de0):
- Code read, in order. `e2e/global-setup.ts:121-122`: a random slug `e2e-canary-<32 hex>` (`server-identity.ts:14-16`) is written as a category to `marketplace_e2e` by the child step `canary`. That step refuses any other shape (`tests/e2e-support/database.ts:73-76`, `isCanarySlug`). `:129-132`: `GET /api/v1/categories` through `E2E_ORIGIN`, and `assertServerShowsCanary` throws unless the answer is 200 and the body contains the slug in quotes. A failed request throws too. `:133`: the sign-up is the first `api.post`. The seed step deletes the canary (`database.ts:80`). A server reading another database cannot show a slug made of 128 random bits.
- Tests: `tests/unit/e2e/server-identity.test.ts` (part of the 33 passed above). In `tests/security/sec074-077-recheck.test.ts`, a real HTTP server on `127.0.0.1` stands in for the other listener. Its category list without the canary is refused, and it receives no request other than GET. Also refused: a slug that appears only as a prefix of a longer slug, a 500 that carries the slug, and a connection error. A list that holds the slug is accepted. A source-order check pins canary write, then check, then the first `api.post` (the sign-up). All pass.
- Live: the e2e run passed (19 of 19), so the e2e server showed the canary and the setup went on. Twice during this session, another agent's e2e run held port 3100. Each time, Playwright refused at once with `http://localhost:3100 is already used`, before global-setup ran. That is the first guard of the finding, observed live.
- Residual, accepted as outside the threat: a process of the same Windows user that takes `127.0.0.1:3100` after the canary check and before the sign-up, for example by binding with `SO_REUSEADDR` or after the e2e server dies, still gets the throwaway seller sign-up. The window is one request long. Such a process already controls the developer's account. A listener on `::1` is no longer reached, because the origin is an IPv4 literal.

### SEC-078 The e2e env-file scanner misses an assignment after a quoted value and U+2028 or U+2029
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-22)
Owner: qa (`e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts`)
Location: e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts:25 (`ASSIGNMENT`, whose `.` does not match U+2028 or U+2029), :44 (lines split on CR and LF only); the file comment at :7 says the scanner "accepts every form dotenv accepts"
Description: found in the SEC-074 re-check. `@next/env` 16.4.0 parses with dotenv's line pattern in multiline mode. In JavaScript, `^` and `$` in that mode also break at U+2028 and U+2029. A quoted value ends before such a character, and the next assignment is read. So `A='1'`, then U+2028, then `PGOPTIONS=-c role=x` sets `PGOPTIONS` for the e2e server. The scanner reads that whole line as one line. `ASSIGNMENT` fails on it, because `.` does not cross U+2028, so no name is seen and the run is not refused. Today the empty `PGOPTIONS` and `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE` in `webServer.env` win over any file value. That was shown end to end in the SEC-074 re-check, so nothing reaches the server's connection. The refusal layer is still weaker than its comment claims. It would be the only guard if the empty values were dropped, or for a refused name that `webServer.env` does not empty. Planting the file needs control of the working tree. Low.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec074-077-recheck.test.ts`. The two `it.fails` cases "SEC-078: refuses PGOPTIONS after a quoted value and U+2028" and the same for U+2029 show `@next/env` setting `PGOPTIONS` while the scanner does not refuse.
Remediation: split lines on CRLF, CR, LF, U+2028, and U+2029, with the last two written as escape sequences, never with literal characters (SEC-043). Better: let the scan call `loadEnvConfig` of `@next/env` on the repository root, in dev mode, with `NODE_ENV` set to `development`, so the check reads exactly what the server reads. Also correct the file comment. When it is fixed, change the two `it.fails` to `it`.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-22, commits a202868 and 914cb39):
- Code read. `e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts:28-30`: `LINE_END` splits on CRLF, CR, LF, U+2028, and U+2029; the last two are built with `String.fromCodePoint`, not typed as literals (SEC-043). `:52`: `scanEnvText` splits with it, so no line reaches `ASSIGNMENT` holding a character that its `.` does not cross. `:7-10`: the comment no longer claims every dotenv form; it says lenient and over-reporting. 914cb39 changes only how the refusal names its source and how development URLs are collected (`collectDevelopmentUrls`); every file in `NEXT_ENV_FILE_NAMES` still goes through `assertNoDriverOverrideForE2e` (`e2e/support/environment.ts:74-76`). The option of calling `loadEnvConfig` was not taken.
- The test change is sound. The two SEC-078 cases are now plain `it`. Their fixture file moved from `.env.local` to `.env`, because `@next/env` skips `.env.local` when `NODE_ENV` is `test`. Each case first asserts that `@next/env` really sets `PGOPTIONS` from the text, so a case cannot pass on a fixture the server ignores.
- The fix is what makes them pass. A scratch test ran the scanner of `a202868^` (copied from `git show`, import path adjusted) against the fixed one on both texts: the old scanner did not refuse, the fixed one refused. Scratch files `tests/security/zz-old-scan.tmp.ts` and `zz-old-scan.tmp.test.ts`, deleted afterwards.
- `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec074-077-recheck.test.ts --reporter=verbose`: 20 passed, 0 expected fail, including "SEC-078: refuses PGOPTIONS after a quoted value and U+2028" and the same for U+2029.
- New differential sweep `tests/security/sec078-recheck.test.ts`. It puts each of 26 code points (every code point JavaScript's `\s` matches, plus U+0085) into 10 positions where a whole assignment starts after the separator: leading, after a single-, double-, or backtick-quoted value, after an unquoted or empty value, after a comment, after a value and a comment, between `export` and the name, after a byte order mark. In 99 of the 260 texts `@next/env` sets a driver variable; the scanner refuses all 99. Plain `it`, passes.
- The same sweep with the separator inside one assignment (between name, `=` or `:`, and value) found a different gap, registered as SEC-079. SEC-078 as reported (an assignment that starts after U+2028 or U+2029) is closed.
- `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec078-recheck.test.ts tests/security/sec074-077-recheck.test.ts tests/unit/e2e/env-file-scan.test.ts`: 3 files, 46 passed, 1 expected fail (SEC-079), exit 0. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security`: 32 files, 1195 passed, 6 expected fail, exit 0. No e2e suite was run in this re-check.

### SEC-079 The e2e env-file scanner misses an assignment whose name, `=` or `:`, and value are on different lines
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-22). The fix opened a different gap in the same scanner, registered as SEC-080.
Owner: qa (`e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts`)
Location (before the fix): e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts:52-56 (`scanEnvText` judges each line on its own), :32 (`ASSIGNMENT`), :72 (`assertNoDriverOverrideForE2e` skips an empty value)
Description: found in the SEC-078 re-check by the differential sweep. `@next/env` 16.4.0 parses with dotenv's pattern, in which `\s` (around `=`, after `:`, and in front of a quoted value) also matches CR, LF, U+2028, and U+2029. So the name, the separator, and the value of one assignment can stand on different lines. Examples that set `PGOPTIONS` for the e2e server, using a plain LF: `PGOPTIONS`, LF, `=-c role=x`; `PGOPTIONS:`, LF, `-c role=x`; `PGOPTIONS=`, LF, `'-c role=x'`. The scanner reads line by line. It finds no assignment on a line that holds only the name, or reads `PGOPTIONS:` with an empty value, which the refusal skips. So the run is not refused. 24 of the swept texts are missed: CR and LF in 5 positions each, U+2028 and U+2029 in 7 each. Today the empty `PGOPTIONS` and `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE` in `webServer.env` win over any file value (shown end to end in the SEC-074 re-check; `@next/env` does not override a variable already in the process environment), so nothing reaches the server's connection. The refusal layer is still not reliable, and the same gap applies to `collectDevelopmentUrls`, which takes `DATABASE_URL` from the scanner's output. Planting the file needs control of the working tree. Low, as SEC-078.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec078-recheck.test.ts`. The `it.fails` case "SEC-079: refuses an assignment split across line terminators" lists the missed texts when changed to `it`. The case "the split-assignment fixtures reach the server" shows that `@next/env` sets `PGOPTIONS` from `PGOPTIONS`, LF, `=-c role=x` and from `PGOPTIONS:`, LF, `-c role=x`.
Remediation: stop modelling dotenv line by line. Preferred: have the scan call `loadEnvConfig` of `@next/env` (or the `parse` of the dotenv copy that `@next/env` bundles) on each file's text, with `NODE_ENV` set to `development`, so the check reads exactly what the server reads. Keep the lenient line scan as an extra layer if wanted. Alternatively, also refuse any line whose first token is a refused name, with or without `=`, `:`, or a value. When it is fixed, change the `it.fails` to `it`.
Fix (qa): `scanEnvText` no longer parses lines. It passes the text to `processEnv` of the `@next/env` that `next` resolves to (a fresh module copy per call, `process.env` cleared during the call and restored in a `finally`, `forceReload` on) and returns the values that parser read, so the check cannot disagree with the server. A parser error throws, so the run is refused (fail closed). The lenient line scan was removed, not kept as a second layer. The `it.fails` in `tests/security/sec078-recheck.test.ts` is now `it`: it failed with the 24 missed texts before the change and passes after. Correction (SEC-080): an earlier version of this note said that the difference in `${VAR}` expansion (scanner: empty process environment; server: real environment) could not matter for the refused names. That was wrong: a refused name's value can be a reference, and the scanner read it as empty. SEC-080 closes this: `scanEnvText` parses a second time with the real environment minus the refused names and takes the refused names from that pass.
QA run: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec078-recheck.test.ts tests/security/sec074-077-recheck.test.ts tests/unit/e2e/env-file-scan.test.ts`: 3 files, 47 passed, 0 failed.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-22, commit 76e43d8):
- Code read. `e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts:44-51`: `loadNextEnv` resolves `@next/env` from the repository's `next` (16.4.0, the copy the server uses), deletes its cache entry, and requires a fresh copy, so the module's initial environment and `__NEXT_PROCESSED_ENV` short cut do not carry over between calls. `:57-83`: `process.env` is emptied, `processEnv(..., forceReload = true)` parses the text with the bundled dotenv 16.3.1 `parse` and `dotenv-expand`, and the second element (the values read) is returned; a parser error reaches `log.error`, which throws (fail closed); `process.env` is emptied and restored in `finally`. The call is synchronous, so no other code sees the empty environment. Every file in `NEXT_ENV_FILE_NAMES` still goes through `assertNoDriverOverrideForE2e` (`e2e/support/environment.ts:56`, `:74-76`).
- The test change is sound. `git show 76e43d8 -- tests/security` shows only the `it.fails` to `it` flip of "SEC-079: refuses an assignment split across line terminators", the formatter's join, and the header comment; the body and the `serverReads > 20` floor are unchanged.
- The fix is what makes it pass: with `e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts` taken from `76e43d8^` (restored with `git checkout HEAD --` afterwards), the case fails with `expected [ ...(24) ] to deeply equal []`; at HEAD it passes.
- New file `tests/security/sec079-recheck.test.ts`: `process.env` is identical before and after a refused and an accepted text; each call stands alone; a driver name already set (empty) in the real environment is still read from the file. All pass.
- New gap, SEC-080: the server expands `$NAME` and `${NAME}` in a value against its real environment; the scanner expands against an empty one. `PGOPTIONS=${X}` with `X` set in the environment reaches the server as the value of `X`, and the scanner reads an empty value, which the refusal skips. The line scanner of `76e43d8^` refused these texts (it saw the literal `${X}`), so this is a regression of the second layer. QA's note under "Fix" says this "can only matter for a value that references another variable, not for the refused names"; that is not correct, a refused name's value can be a reference.
- `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec079-recheck.test.ts tests/security/sec078-recheck.test.ts --reporter=verbose`: 2 files, 7 passed, 1 expected fail (SEC-080). No e2e suite was run.

### SEC-080 The e2e env-file scanner reads a driver value that references another variable as empty
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-22, commit c2bf1f9). The `DATABASE_URL` part named in the description is not covered by the fix and is now SEC-082.
Owner: qa (`e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts`)
