Location: e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts:58-60 (`process.env` emptied before `processEnv`), :96 of the same file (`assertNoDriverOverrideForE2e` skips an empty value)
Description: found in the SEC-079 re-check. `@next/env` 16.4.0 runs `dotenv-expand` after parsing: `$NAME`, `${NAME}`, and `${NAME:-default}` in a value are replaced from `process.env` first, then from the file. The e2e server starts with the developer's real environment, so `PGOPTIONS=${X}` (or `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE="$X"`) in `.env.local` gets the value of `X`. `scanEnvText` empties `process.env` before it calls the parser, so for the scanner the reference expands to an empty string, and `assertNoDriverOverrideForE2e` skips empty values: the run is not refused. Before 76e43d8 the line scanner saw the literal `${X}` and refused. The same difference applies to `DATABASE_URL` in `collectDevelopmentUrls`: a development URL written as a reference is dropped from the comparison with the test URL. Today the empty `PGOPTIONS` and `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE` in `webServer.env` (`e2e/support/server-environment.ts:35-36`) win over any file value, so nothing reaches the server's connection; planting the file needs control of the working tree, and the attacker also needs an environment variable whose value is useful. Low, as SEC-078 and SEC-079.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec079-recheck.test.ts`. "the fixtures reach the server non-empty" shows `@next/env` (fresh copy, `SEC080_PROBE=-c role=x` in the environment) setting `PGOPTIONS` from `PGOPTIONS=${SEC080_PROBE}` and `PGOPTIONS=$SEC080_PROBE`, and `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE` from `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE="${SEC080_PROBE}"`. The `it.fails` case "the scanner refuses a driver value the server expands from the environment", flipped to `it`, fails with `expected [ 'braced', 'bare', 'double-quoted' ] to deeply equal []`.
Remediation: either (a) refuse a refused name that the parser returns at all, empty or not, when its raw text in the file holds a `$` (simplest: for the refused names, parse once without expansion, for example with the bundled dotenv `parse`, and refuse a non-empty raw value); or (b) give the parser the real environment for expansion, minus the refused names, so that it expands exactly as the server does, while still reading the refused names from the file. Keep the fail-closed behaviour. When fixed, flip the `it.fails` to `it`.
Fix (qa): `scanEnvText` runs the server's parser twice. Pass 1 uses an empty environment (so names the real environment already holds are still read from the file). Pass 2 uses the real environment minus the refused names (matched case-insensitively), so `$NAME` and `${NAME}` expand as the server expands them. The refused names are taken from pass 2, every other name from pass 1. Fail-closed behaviour is unchanged. The `it.fails` in `tests/security/sec079-recheck.test.ts` is now `it`. Awaiting security re-check; status stays OPEN until then.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-22, commit c2bf1f9):
- Code read. `e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts:57-105`: `parseWith` empties `process.env`, copies in the given environment, and runs a fresh `@next/env` `processEnv`; pass 1 with `{}`, pass 2 with the saved real environment minus the names that upper-case to `PGOPTIONS` or `NODE_PG_FORCE_NATIVE`; driver names are overwritten from pass 2, other names keep pass 1. Fail closed (the `log.error` throw) and the `finally` restore are unchanged. Taking the drivers out of pass 2 means a driver held empty in the real environment cannot hide the file's value; the scanner can only be stricter than the server here (for example `${D-x}` with `D` empty in the environment), never laxer.
- The test change is sound: `git show c2bf1f9 -- tests` is the `it.fails` to `it` flip and its comment, nothing else.
- The fix is what makes it pass: with `e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts` from `c2bf1f9^` (restored with `git checkout HEAD --`), `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec079-recheck.test.ts` gives 1 failed, 4 passed; at HEAD 5 passed.
- New file `tests/security/sec080-recheck.test.ts`, differential against `loadEnvConfig` of a fresh `@next/env` in the real environment: 14 shapes (braced, bare, prefix, `:-` and `-` defaults of an unset name, set name with default, chains through a file variable in both orders, double-quoted, `export`, colon form, single-quoted, escaped dollar, two references); all 14 reach the server non-empty (with `@next/env` 16.4.0 even the single-quoted and escaped-dollar forms are expanded) and every one is refused. Also a lower-case name, a driver already empty in the environment, a reference to the other driver name, no false refusal for an unset reference, and `process.env` identical after both passes. 9 passed, 1 expected fail (SEC-082).
- Flake hunt: `pnpm test --project security`, 14 runs. Runs 2 and 3 failed with `Test timed out in 5000ms` in `cr083-request-frame-recheck.test.ts` (first `BAD_CONFIGURATIONS` case) and `sec039-040-cr66-recheck.test.ts` ("without BETTER_AUTH_SECRETS ... (control)"); both are the first cold `await import` of the auth module after `vi.resetModules()` while 35 files run in parallel. No assertion failed. The 7 security files that re-import the auth module now set `vi.setConfig({ testTimeout: 30_000 })`; assertions unchanged. Runs 7 to 14 had no timeout. They each had one deterministic failure, which is not a flake: the SEC-057 pin "every product title read in storefront source is isolated or pinned" lists new uncommitted frontend files (`src/components/cart/cart-summary.tsx`, `src/components/cart/cart-view.tsx`, `src/components/checkout/checkout-view.tsx`, `src/components/page-frame.tsx`), from T-034 work in progress. That guard is working as intended; those places need review before the frontend commit.

### SEC-081 The rule 7 lint guard is bypassed by an inline disable comment and by indirect require calls
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN (fourth re-check 2026-10-23 against 84b93f6: remediation (a) to (e) of the third re-check is in place and its 19 spellings are rejected, but 15 spellings outside the written "Accepted residual" still pass: a single string literal naming `constructor` or a loader, wrapped in a TypeScript expression (`as`, `satisfies`, `<T>`, `!`) or bound as a default value, used as a computed key; registered as SEC-085, see "Fourth re-check evidence". Third re-check 2026-10-22 against 23720f2: the 14 spellings of the second re-check are rejected and the six `src` rewrites keep their behaviour, but 13 spellings outside the written "Accepted residual" still pass: the `globalThis` cast exemption applies to every listed global, the bundler loaders pass through any member, `globalThis.globalThis`/`.global`/`.module` build an alias, and `constructor` is reached by destructuring or by a plain string literal kept in a variable; see "Third re-check evidence". The name-based residual itself is accepted by the owner, see "Accepted residual" below. Second re-check 2026-10-22 against 0a7ea5e: remediation (a) to (e) is in place and all 42 registered spellings are rejected, but the inline disable still works in any file outside `src`, and 7 more loader spellings pass in `src`; see "Second re-check evidence". The reachable-importer gap found in the same re-check is SEC-083)
Owner: orchestrator (`eslint.config.mjs`); devops (`scripts/check-import-guards.mjs`, probe list)
Location: eslint.config.mjs:70-84 (`dynamicImportRules` matches only a call whose callee is the identifier `require`), :96-147 (no `linterOptions.noInlineConfig`); package.json `lint` script (no `--no-inline-config`)
Description: found in the CR-T032-10 probe. ESLint, as configured, rejects every direct spelling of an import of `src/server/db` from a forbidden file (29 spellings in 13 forbidden files, plus `require`, dynamic import, and TypeScript import-equals; see the test). Six spellings that load the db layer at run time pass `pnpm lint` without an error: `/* eslint-disable no-restricted-imports */` before the import; `// eslint-disable-next-line no-restricted-imports`; `createRequire(import.meta.url)("../../server/db")`; `const r = require; r("../../server/db")`; `module.require("../../server/db")`; `require.call(null, "../../server/db")`. The file header of `eslint.config.mjs` and `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs` already name the inline disable and `createRequire` as left to review, so this is a known limit, now pinned by tests. A page, route handler, or component that uses one of them gets the database client, which is what rule 7 forbids; the attacker is a contributor, and code review is the remaining control. Today the real tree holds none of them: no hand-written file under `src`, `scripts`, or `prisma/seed.ts` has an `eslint-disable` comment, and `createRequire` appears outside tests only in `src/server/db/connection-target.ts` (the db layer itself). Both facts are now checked by `tests/security/cr-t032-10-import-guards.test.ts` on every `pnpm test`. Low: hygiene of a defence-in-depth guard; nothing reachable from a request.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/cr-t032-10-import-guards.test.ts`. The six `it.fails` cases under "SEC-081: spellings that get past the rule 7 lint guard" each assert that ESLint reports an error for one spelling; they are expected to fail (6 expected fail).
Remediation: (a) set `linterOptions: { noInlineConfig: true }` for hand-written, non-test files under `src/**` (or add `--no-inline-config` to the `lint` script after checking that no file relies on an inline directive); (b) add `no-restricted-syntax` selectors for files outside the db layer: `CallExpression[callee.name='createRequire']`, `MemberExpression[object.name='module'][property.name='require']`, `MemberExpression[object.name='require']`, and an `Identifier[name='require']` that is not the callee of a call (for example `VariableDeclarator[init.name='require']`); (c) add the six spellings to `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs` as rejected. When fixed, flip the six `it.fails` to `it`.
Addendum (security, 2026-10-22): a further probe found 17 more spellings that pass the guard today, all from `src/app/x/probe.ts` or `.js` (or `src/components/probe.tsx`): `/* eslint-disable */` (no rule named), `// eslint-disable-line`, the config comment `/* eslint no-restricted-imports: "off" */`; `createRequire` imported under another name (`import { createRequire as cr }`), through a namespace (`mod.createRequire`), through the default export (`Module.createRequire`), from the bare specifier `"module"`, from `await import("node:module")`, from `process.getBuiltinModule("node:module")`, and from `require("node:module")`; `require` reached as `const { require: r } = module`, `require.apply`, `require["call"]`, `globalThis.require`; and code built from a string, `eval("require")` and `new Function("return require")`. Remediation (b) as written would miss most of the `createRequire` forms (it matches only a callee named `createRequire`). Additions to the remediation: refuse imports of `node:module` and `module` (all forms, including dynamic `import()` and `require`) outside the db layer with `no-restricted-imports` and the dynamic-import selectors already used for the db path, refuse `process.getBuiltinModule`, refuse any `MemberExpression` or `Property` whose object or key is `require` other than a direct call, refuse `globalThis.require`, and turn on `no-eval`, `no-implied-eval`, and `no-new-func` (or the equivalent `no-restricted-syntax` selectors, since the script counts only `no-restricted-imports` and `no-restricted-syntax` errors) for files outside the db layer. `noInlineConfig` covers all three comment forms.
Probes (security, 2026-10-22, at the orchestrator's instruction; the probe list is devops-owned): `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs` now holds the 6 spellings above and the 17 of the addendum, 23 in all, with the expectation "SEC-081": the script counts them as known gaps and does not fail while they pass, and fails with "SEC-081 gap is now rejected: change its expectation to true" once the guard rejects one (the same contract as `it.fails`). Three controls, `createRequire` in `src/server/db/`, `tests/security/`, and `e2e/support/`, are expected to stay clean after the fix. `node scripts/check-import-guards.mjs`: "137 of 137 import-guard probes behaved as expected (23 are known SEC-081 gaps, not yet rejected)", exit 0. A copy of the script with one SEC-081 probe replaced by a direct `import { db } from "@/server/db"` exits 1 with that message (copy deleted). The six `it.fails` in `tests/security/cr-t032-10-import-guards.test.ts` are unchanged; a new plain case "no other indirect loader appears outside the db layer" checks that none of the addendum's spellings appears in the real tree under `src`, `scripts`, or `prisma/seed.ts` outside `src/server/db/` (passes; the regular expression was checked on 9 positive and 5 negative samples).
Fix (orchestrator, commit 0f497a8): `eslint.config.mjs:88-129`, `:170-189`. Four `hardenedGuard` blocks for `src/**` (last in the config, so they win) set `linterOptions: { noInlineConfig: true }`, add `^(node:)?module$` to `no-restricted-imports`, and add `no-restricted-syntax` selectors for `process.getBuiltinModule`, any `require` identifier that is not the callee of a call, any `createRequire` identifier, and calls of `eval`, `Function`, and `new Function`. Files under `src/server/db/**` and every file whose name matches `**/*.test.*` or `**/*.itest.*` are exempt (`HARDENED_EXEMPT`, `:120`). The 23 probes and the six `it.fails` were flipped to expect an error.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-22, commit 0f497a8): **not closed; status stays OPEN.**
- What the fix closes, confirmed: `pnpm lint` (`eslint --max-warnings 0 && node scripts/check-import-guards.mjs`) exit 0, "137 of 137 import-guard probes behaved as expected", no known-gap line left. `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/cr-t032-10-import-guards.test.ts --project security`: 63 passed, 0 expected fail (the six SEC-081 cases now plain `it` and passing). `git show 0f497a8 -- tests scripts` changes only the expectations (`"SEC-081"` to `true`, `it.fails` to `it`) and comments; no probe body was weakened. No file under `src` (tests included) holds an `eslint-disable`, `eslint-enable`, or `/* eslint` comment today (Grep), so `noInlineConfig` breaks nothing.
- Gap 1, the exemption by file name. `HARDENED_EXEMPT` takes every `*.test.*` and `*.itest.*` file under `src` out of the hardened blocks, so those files keep the plain guard, where an inline comment is honoured. Such a file is an ordinary module to the bundler: a page can import `@/lib/x/leak.itest` like any other. Only `src/**/*.test.ts` and `src/**/*.test.tsx` are run by Vitest (`vitest.config.mts:23,31`); `src/**/*.itest.*`, `*.test.js`, `*.test.mts`, and the like are run by no configuration, so a file with that name does not even need to hold a test. The real-tree scans of `tests/security/cr-t032-10-import-guards.test.ts` (`SOURCE_FILES`, `:52-56`) also drop every test-named file. Probe: `src/lib/x/leak.itest.ts` with `/* eslint-disable */` and `import { db } from "@/server/db"; export { db };` lints clean; `src/lib/x/leak.test.mts` and `src/components/x/leak.test.tsx` (with `eslint-disable-next-line`) the same; `src/app/x/leak.itest.ts` with `createRequire` from `node:module` lints clean. The same file without the comment is an error (control). This is the inline-disable bypass of the original description, moved to a file name.
- Gap 2, name-based selectors. 15 spellings pass in `src/app/x/probe.ts` or `.js`: `module.constructor._load(...)`; `process.mainModule["require"](...)`; `process["getBuiltinModule"]("node:module")["createRequire"](...)`; an alias of `process` (`const p = process; p.getBuiltinModule(...)`); `const { getBuiltinModule: g } = process`; `await import("node:module")` followed by `mod["createRequire"]` (the module pattern is in `no-restricted-imports` only, not in the dynamic-import selectors); `require("node:module")["createRequire"]` (same reason); indirect eval `(0, eval)(...)`; `globalThis.eval(...)`; `(() => {}).constructor("return require")`; `Reflect.construct(Function, [...])`; `import vm from "node:vm"` with `vm.runInThisContext(...)`; and the specifiers `@/server/db?x`, `@/server/db#x` (static), `import("@/server/db?x")` (the path patterns end at `/` or the end of the string; whether Turbopack resolves a query suffix to the module was not tested). Which of the loader spellings return the db layer inside a Next.js bundle at run time was not tested; SEC-081 counts a lint pass as the finding, as before.
- Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec081-recheck.test.ts --project security`: 4 passed (controls), 19 expected fail (4 for gap 1, 15 for gap 2). Each `it.fails` asserts an ESLint error and turns red when the guard rejects that spelling.
- Remediation: (a) apply `noInlineConfig: true` to every file under `src`, test files included (none uses an inline directive today), or at least drop `**/*.test.*` and `**/*.itest.*` from `HARDENED_EXEMPT` for `noInlineConfig` and keep the exemption only for the loader selectors; (b) refuse a non-test file that imports a test-named module (`no-restricted-imports` pattern `\.(test|itest)(\.[cm]?[jt]sx?)?$`), and refuse `*.itest.*` and non-`.ts`/`.tsx` test names under `src` (or add them to a Vitest project so they must hold tests); (c) add `^(node:)?(module|vm)$` to the dynamic-import and `require` selectors as well as to `no-restricted-imports`; (d) refuse any member access, computed or not, whose property is `require`, `createRequire`, `getBuiltinModule`, `mainModule`, `_load`, or `constructor` called as a function, and any `Identifier` named `eval` or `Function` that is not a type position, plus `Reflect.construct`; (e) add `?` and `#` to `NOT_NORMAL`. Lint selectors over names cannot be complete; the stronger control is a module-graph check over resolved files (for example: resolve every import of `src/app/**` and `src/components/**` with the TypeScript resolver and fail when the graph reaches `src/server/db` other than through `src/modules/**`, `src/server/auth/**`, or `src/server/health.ts`). When a spelling is rejected, flip its `it.fails` to `it`.

- Remediation for what remains: (a) set `linterOptions: { noInlineConfig: true }` for `ALL_CODE`, not only `src` (or add `--no-inline-config` to the `lint` script; nothing in the repository uses an inline directive today); (b) for hardened files, refuse the identifiers `module`, `global`, `globalThis`, `Reflect`, `__non_webpack_require__`, `__webpack_require__` other than as the object of a non-computed member access with a literal property (same shape as the `process` selector), refuse any `.constructor` member access that is not in a type position or a `?.name` read (or refuse it outright and allow the one read in `library-error.ts` by moving it into a helper), and refuse any computed member access whose key is a `BinaryExpression` or `TemplateLiteral`; (c) add the 7 group 3 and 2 group 2 spellings to `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs` as rejected. When a spelling is rejected, flip its `it.fails` to `it`.
Fix (devops, session 105, after the second re-check): `eslint.config.mjs`: `noInlineConfig` for every file (`ALL_CODE`), generated client excepted (it carries its own disable header and is git-ignored); in hardened files the identifiers `module`, `global`, `globalThis`, `Reflect`, `__non_webpack_require__`, `__webpack_require__` are refused unless they are the object of a non-computed member access, a property or key name, a type position (`typeof globalThis`), or the cast form `(globalThis as T)[key]`; any non-computed `.constructor` access is refused; a computed key that is a `BinaryExpression` holding a string or template literal, or any template literal, is refused; two short string literals (16 characters or fewer each) joined by `+` in a variable initialiser are refused. A numeric key (`items[i + 1]`) stays allowed: the tree indexes arrays that way in 11 places, so a flat refusal of every `BinaryExpression` key was not usable. The one `.constructor` read (`src/server/auth/library-error.ts`) became `Reflect.get(prototype, "constructor")` with the same lookup, and six `const holder = globalThis as T` aliases in `src/server/rate-limit.ts`, `src/modules/orders/timeout-sweep.ts`, `src/modules/uploads/cleanup.ts`, `src/server/auth/security-events.ts`, `src/server/auth/session-purge.ts` became the inline cast, behaviour unchanged. `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs`: the 2 group 2 and 7 group 3 spellings, and the SEC-083 spellings, added as rejected, plus controls. `tests/security/sec081-recheck-2.test.ts`: the `it.fails` cases flipped to `it`.

Accepted residual (owner decision, session 105, D-045): the lint guard is a set of name-based selectors and stays incomplete. Known ways past it that the owner accepted, with code review as the control: a string key built from three or more pieces, from pieces longer than 16 characters, or from variables (`a + b`); `Reflect.get(fn, "constructor")` and the other `Reflect` helpers, which are allowed as `Reflect.name(...)` calls; the cast form `(globalThis as T)[key]` with a key held in a variable; a re-export through an allowed folder; and any loader reachable through a name the selectors do not list. A contributor who uses one of them gets the database client in a page, which rule 7 forbids; reviewers check new `globalThis`, `Reflect`, `constructor`, computed-key, and `eval`-like code in `src` (excluding Vitest tests) against rule 7. The stronger control, a module-graph check over resolved imports, was not commissioned; it can be added later and would close SEC-081 and SEC-083 without the residual.

Third re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-22, commit 23720f2): **SEC-081 stays OPEN.**
- The six `src` rewrites keep their behaviour. `git show 23720f2 -- src`: in `src/server/rate-limit.ts`, `src/modules/orders/timeout-sweep.ts`, `src/modules/uploads/cleanup.ts`, `src/server/auth/security-events.ts`, `src/server/auth/session-purge.ts`, each `const holder = globalThis as T` became the inline cast `(globalThis as T)[KEY]` at every use, `delete` included; the `tick` closures now read the slot from `globalThis` on each tick instead of from a captured alias of the same object, so nothing changes. `src/server/auth/library-error.ts:50-59`: `Reflect.get(prototype, "constructor")` is the same lookup as `prototype.constructor` (receiver is the prototype, inherited, getters and proxy traps run, still inside the `try`). A scratch script (session scratchpad, `ctor-diff.mjs`) ran the old and new lookups on 14 shapes (error, subclass, plain, null prototype, function, constructor as string, number, null, symbol, an object with a `name`, a throwing `constructor` getter, a throwing `name` getter, a proxy prototype, a proxy whose `getPrototypeOf` throws): 0 differences. The one difference found is stricter: with `String.prototype.name` polluted, the old code returned that name for a string `constructor`, the new returns nothing. The six files' tests: `pnpm exec vitest run` on their six test files, 100 passed. Risk of the `Reflect.get` form: none at run time; it is the spelling the accepted residual names, so the tree now holds one example of it, in the auth layer (allowed to import the db layer anyway).

Old config: 0 of 14 rejected (5 SEC-083, 2 group 2, 7 group 3). HEAD: 14 of 14. - About 80 further spellings were linted at HEAD. Inside the accepted residual (D-045), recorded, not pinned: a three-piece key in a variable; a key from `JSON.parse`; `Reflect.get(fn, "constructor")`; `(globalThis as T)[k]` with `k` in a variable; `Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(Object.getPrototypeOf(() => {}), "constructor").value`; `module.children`, `.parent`, `.paths`; unlisted names: `__turbopack_require__`, `__webpack_modules__`, `import { tsImport } from "tsx/esm/api"`, `import { createJiti } from "jiti"`, `import { vi } from "vitest"` with `vi.importActual(...)` in a non-test file, and `new Worker(code, { "eval": true })` from `node:worker_threads` (the identifier key `eval` is rejected, the string key is not). The package loaders (tsx, vitest, jiti) load source by path and are installed today; a cheap addition would be to refuse those package names in hardened files.

The cast exemption (`MemberExpression[computed=true] > TSAsExpression.object > Identifier.expression`, `eslint.config.mjs:115`) is written for `globalThis` (the residual names only `(globalThis as T)[key]`) but applies to all six names: `(module as T)[k]("../../server/db")`, `(__non_webpack_require__ as T)[k](null, ...)`, `(Reflect as T)[k]` pass with `k` in a variable. The member exemption (`:109`) lets the bundler loaders through any non-computed member: `__non_webpack_require__.call(null, "../../server/db")`, `__webpack_require__.c` (the module cache). The property exemption (`:110`) lets a fresh alias be built: `const g = globalThis.globalThis`, `globalThis.global`, `globalThis.module`, then `g[k]` with `k` in a variable. The comment at `:105` says an alias cannot be built.

Each gives the `Function` constructor.

The real tree uses none of these shapes (`src/server/errors.ts:408` passes `"constructor"` as a call argument, which (e) as written does not touch). Add the spellings to `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs` and flip the `it.fails`. Close SEC-081 when (a) to (e) are in, or when the owner extends the written residual to cover them.
Fix (devops, session 106; status stays OPEN until the security re-check): `eslint.config.mjs` (a) the cast exemption in `GLOBAL_ALLOWED` is limited to `Identifier.expression[name='globalThis']`; (b) new selector `Identifier[name=/^__(non_)?webpack_|^__turbopack_/]` in every position; (c) the `Identifier.property` exemption is removed, so a global name as a non-computed property (`globalThis.globalThis`, `.global`, `.module`) is refused by the global-name selector, and a computed literal key naming a global is refused by a new selector; (d) `ObjectPattern > Property` whose key (identifier, string, computed literal) is `constructor`, `construct`, or a loader name, and any computed non-literal key in a destructuring pattern; (e) `VariableDeclarator` and `AssignmentExpression` whose value is a string (or a template without expressions) equal to a loader name, `constructor`, or `construct`. `src/server/errors.ts:408` stays clean (control probe added). ### SEC-084 A specifier that starts with ./.. or is absolute passes the normal-form check
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN (re-checked 2026-10-23 against 84b93f6: the eight registered spellings are rejected, but the same climb is still written past the check with percent-encoded dot segments, and other specifier forms pass; registered as SEC-086. Closes with SEC-086)
Owner: devops (`eslint.config.mjs`, `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs`)
Location: eslint.config.mjs `NOT_NORMAL`; the SEC-083 pattern and the sibling db pattern anchor on `../` and `./db`
Description: found in the third SEC-081 re-check (commit 23720f2). `import "./../../../scripts/x"` from `src/app/x/` and `import "./../db"` from a new `src/server/` subfolder resolve like the `../` forms but are not matched by the SEC-083 or sibling patterns; a Windows absolute path to `scripts/` is not refused either. Defence-in-depth guard; contributor-only. Low.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec081-recheck-3.test.ts --project security`, describe "SEC-084" (6 cases, formerly `it.fails`).
Fix (devops, session 106): `NOT_NORMAL` also matches `^\./\.\.`, a drive letter (`^[A-Za-z]:`), a leading `/`, and a `file:` URL, in static, dynamic, and `require` forms. Eight probes (the six plus a POSIX absolute path and a `file:` URL) are in `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs`; the real-tree case of the test file checks that no specifier under `src` has these shapes.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-23, commit 84b93f6): `eslint.config.mjs:27-28` read; the six `sec081-recheck-3.test.ts` cases pass as plain `it` (old config 0 of 6 rejected, HEAD 6 of 6, part of the 19 in SEC-081's fourth re-check); variants `FILE:/C:/...` (upper case, one slash) and the drive-relative `C:scripts/...` are rejected too (`NOT_NORMAL` is matched case-insensitively). Still passing: `./%2e%2e/%2e%2e/%2e%2e/scripts/bootstrap-admin`, which Node's ESM resolver decodes to the same climb (checked: `fileURLToPath(new URL(spec, base))` gives `scripts\bootstrap-admin.ts`, which exists). That and the other forms are SEC-086; SEC-084 stays OPEN until SEC-086 is closed.


### SEC-082 The e2e scanner reads a development DATABASE_URL written as a reference as empty
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN
Owner: qa (`e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts`)
Location: e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts:84-99 (only the driver names are taken from pass 2); e2e/support/env-file-scan.ts:149 (`collectDevelopmentUrls` drops an empty value)
Description: found in the SEC-080 re-check; it is the `DATABASE_URL` part of SEC-080's description, which the fix in c2bf1f9 does not cover. `DATABASE_URL` is not a driver name, so `scanEnvText` takes it from pass 1, parsed with an empty environment. `DATABASE_URL=${DEV_URL}` in `.env.local`, with `DEV_URL` set in the environment, is read by `next dev` as the URL, by the scanner as empty, and `collectDevelopmentUrls` drops it. The check that refuses a test URL on the development server (CR-156) then does not see that development URL. The e2e server itself is not affected: `webServer.env` sets `DATABASE_URL` and it wins over every file. The run needs a developer who keeps the development URL only as a reference in an env file and a `DATABASE_URL_TEST` on the same server; the outcome would be e2e data written next to development data on the same server, not a remote attack. Low.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec080-recheck.test.ts`. "the server reads the reference as the URL" passes; the `it.fails` case "the scanner reads it as the server does, so the comparison sees it", changed to `it`, fails with `expected [] to deeply equal [ 'the .env.local file' ]`.
Remediation: take `DATABASE_URL` from a pass with the real environment minus `DATABASE_URL` and the driver names (generalise pass 2 to the set of names the checks read), and keep pass 1's value as well if the two differ, so both are compared. When fixed, flip the `it.fails` to `it`.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-083 Application code may import scripts, tests, and the seed, which may import the db layer
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN (re-checked 2026-10-23 against 84b93f6: the direct forms are rejected, but `src` can still leave for the repository root or a new top-level folder, whose file may import `scripts/` or `tests/`; registered as SEC-087. The percent-encoded `%73cripts` form is SEC-086. Closes with SEC-086 and SEC-087)
Owner: orchestrator (`eslint.config.mjs`); devops (`scripts/check-import-guards.mjs`, probe list)
Location: eslint.config.mjs:37-44 (`DB_LAYER_IMPORTERS` lets `scripts/**`, `tests/**`, `prisma/seed.ts` import `src/server/db`), :195-216 (no pattern refuses an import of those folders, or of `e2e/**`, from `src`)
Description: found in the second SEC-081 re-check (2026-10-22, commit 0a7ea5e). Rule 7 allows `scripts/**`, `tests/**`, and `prisma/seed.ts` to import `src/server/db`, and nothing stops a page, route handler, or component from importing one of them. No trick and no comment is needed: `import { x } from "../../../scripts/bootstrap-admin"` in `src/app/x/probe.ts` lints clean, in static and dynamic form, and `scripts/bootstrap-admin.ts:19` imports `db` from `../src/server/db` (and calls `main()` at load, `:54`, so importing it would also run the bootstrap). The same holds for `tests/**` (any helper that holds the test database client), `prisma/seed.ts`, and `e2e/**` (not an allowed importer, but not refused to application code). A page that does this gets the database client by a transitive import, which is what rule 7 is meant to stop; unlike the SEC-081 spellings, this one is an easy accident (for example reusing a helper from `scripts/`). Attacker is a contributor; code review is the remaining control. The real tree has no such import: the only imports from `src` into those folders are `src/server/db/*` reaching `prisma/generated/**` (checked on every run by the real-tree case of `tests/security/sec081-recheck-2.test.ts`). Low: defence-in-depth guard; nothing reachable from a request.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec081-recheck-2.test.ts --project security`, describe "group 1": 5 `it.fails` (page imports `scripts/bootstrap-admin`, component imports `tests/helpers/db`, page imports `prisma/seed`, page imports `e2e/support/db`, page dynamic import of `scripts/bootstrap-admin`), each asserting an ESLint error; all 5 are expected fail today.
Remediation: in the hardened guard for `src` outside `src/server/db/**`, add a `no-restricted-imports` pattern (and the same to the dynamic-import and `require` selectors) that refuses any specifier resolving into `scripts/`, `tests/`, `e2e/`, or `prisma/` other than `prisma/generated/` from the db layer, for example `^(\.\./)+(scripts|tests|e2e|prisma)(/|$)` for files under `src` (relative depth only reaches the root from `src`, and `@/` cannot reach outside `src`). Add the 5 spellings to `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs` as rejected, keep the db layer's `prisma/generated` imports as a clean control, and flip the 5 `it.fails` to `it`. The module-graph check proposed in SEC-081 would also cover this.
Re-check evidence: none yet.
Fix (devops, session 105): `eslint.config.mjs` `outsideSrcPattern` (`^(\.\./)+(scripts|tests|e2e|prisma)(/|$)`) is part of the hardened guard for `src` outside `src/server/db/**`, in static, dynamic, and `require` forms. Vitest-run test files under `src` are not covered by it: no application code may import them (`testImportPattern`). The 5 spellings plus a `require` form are in `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs` as rejected; the db layer reaching `prisma/generated` is a clean control; the 5 `it.fails` in `tests/security/sec081-recheck-2.test.ts` are plain `it`. Residual: the `@/` alias cannot leave `src`, so relative depth is the only route and it is covered; an import through a path alias added later to tsconfig would need the same pattern. Owner accepted the SEC-081 name-based residual in D-045.
Re-check evidence (security, 2026-10-23, HEAD 84b93f6, fix in 23720f2 and 84b93f6): `outsideSrcPattern` (`eslint.config.mjs:170-173`) read. The 5 spellings of `tests/security/sec081-recheck-2.test.ts` group 1 pass as plain `it`; upper-case `../../../SCRIPTS/...` and `@/../scripts/...` are rejected; the `./..` forms are rejected since 84b93f6 (SEC-084). Not closed: the claim "relative depth is the only route and it is covered" holds only for the four listed folder names. `import { x } from "../../../shared/leak"` and `"../../../leak"` from `src/app/x/probe.ts` lint clean, and a file `shared/leak.ts` with `export * from "../tests/helpers/reset"` (which imports `db` from `@/server/db`, `tests/helpers/reset.ts:17`) or a root `leak.ts` with `export * from "./scripts/bootstrap-admin"` lints clean too, so the page reaches the db layer in two hops (SEC-087). `../../../%73cripts/bootstrap-admin` also passes (SEC-086). In the real tree the only root file reachable this way that imports further is `playwright.config.ts` (to `e2e/support`, then `tests/helpers/test-database-url.ts`, which imports nothing of the db layer); the real-tree case of `tests/security/sec081-recheck-4.test.ts` checks that no relative specifier under `src` leaves `src` except the db layer reaching `prisma/generated`.

### SEC-085 A single literal name wrapped in a TypeScript expression or bound as a default value passes the loader selectors
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN
Owner: orchestrator or devops (`eslint.config.mjs`); devops (`scripts/check-import-guards.mjs`, probe list)
Location: eslint.config.mjs:143, :147-155 (each selector reads `.value` of the key or initialiser node itself: `MemberExpression[computed=true][property.value=...]`, `VariableDeclarator[init.value=...]`, `AssignmentExpression[right.value=...]`; the template form is checked for `VariableDeclarator` only)
Description: found in the fourth SEC-081 re-check (commit 84b93f6). A TypeScript wrapper puts the literal one level down (`TSAsExpression`, `TSSatisfiesExpression`, `TSTypeAssertion`, `TSNonNullExpression`), and a default value is an `AssignmentPattern`, which none of the selectors names. Fifteen spellings pass in `src/app/x/probe.ts`: as a computed key, `(() => {})["constructor" as string]`, `[("constructor" as const)]`, `[<string>"constructor"]`, `["constructor"!]`, `["constructor" satisfies string]`, and `(globalThis as any)["eval" as string]` (the residual names the globalThis cast with a key held in a variable, not a literal); as a variable, `const k = "constructor" as const`, `satisfies string`, `<const>"constructor"`, `"constructor"!`; as an assignment, `k = "constructor" as const` and ``k = `constructor` ``; as a default, `function f(k = "constructor")`, `const { k = "constructor" } = ...`, `const [k = "constructor"] = ...`; each then `(() => {})[k]`. Each gives the `Function` constructor (or `eval`), which loads any module by code. None of them builds a name from pieces: it is the single literal that remediation (e) of the third re-check and the computed-literal selector of the second refuse, so it is not inside the written residual. Attacker is a contributor; code review is the remaining control. The real tree has no such shape: outside `src/server/db/` and Vitest tests, a string literal naming `constructor` or a loader appears only in `src/server/errors.ts` (a call argument) and `src/server/auth/library-error.ts` (`Reflect.get`), checked by the real-tree case of the test file. Low: defence-in-depth guard; nothing reachable from a request.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec081-recheck-4.test.ts --project security`, describe "SEC-085": 15 `it.fails`, each asserting an ESLint error; all 15 expected fail today.
Remediation: match through the wrappers, for example with `:matches(Literal, TSAsExpression, TSSatisfiesExpression, TSTypeAssertion, TSNonNullExpression)` and the descendant form `MemberExpression[computed=true] > .property :matches(Literal, TemplateLiteral)[value=/^(names)$/]` (or `:has(...)`), and the same for `VariableDeclarator > .init`, `AssignmentExpression > .right`, and `AssignmentPattern > .right`; check the expression-free template on `AssignmentExpression` and `AssignmentPattern` as well as `VariableDeclarator`. A simpler, wider rule: refuse any string literal or expression-free template equal to a loader name, `constructor`, or `construct` anywhere in hardened files except as a call argument (the one shape the real tree uses). Add the 15 spellings to `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs` as rejected and flip the `it.fails`.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-086 Percent-encoded segments, an NTFS stream suffix, and data: URLs pass the normal-form check
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN
Owner: devops (`eslint.config.mjs` `NOT_NORMAL`, `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs`)
Location: eslint.config.mjs:27-28 (`NOT_NORMAL` has no `%`, no `:` after the first segment, and no URL scheme other than `file:`)
Description: found in the fourth SEC-081 re-check (commit 84b93f6). Eleven specifier forms lint clean: (1) percent-encoding, which Node's ESM resolver decodes (checked with `fileURLToPath(new URL(spec, base))`: the targets exist): `./%2e%2e/%2e%2e/%2e%2e/scripts/bootstrap-admin` (static, and dynamic in a `.js` file), `../../server/%64b`, `../../../%73cripts/bootstrap-admin`, and `../%64b` from a new `src/server/` subfolder; (2) the NTFS directory stream `::$INDEX_ALLOCATION`, which Windows treats as the directory itself and which TypeScript's resolver follows on this machine (checked with `ts.resolveModuleName`: `@/server/db::$INDEX_ALLOCATION` resolves to `src/server/db::$INDEX_ALLOCATION/index.ts`, and `fs.statSync` finds it): `@/server/db::$INDEX_ALLOCATION`, `require("../../server/db::$INDEX_ALLOCATION")`, `../../../scripts::$INDEX_ALLOCATION/bootstrap-admin`, and `../db::$INDEX_ALLOCATION` from a `src/server/` subfolder; (3) `data:` URLs, whose content the linter never reads: `await import("data:text/javascript;base64,...")` and `export * from "data:text/javascript,..."` in a `.js` file (Node imports a `data:` module and it may import `node:module`; checked: the probe's module yields `createRequire` as a function). A `.ts` file with a percent-encoded or `data:` specifier fails `tsc` (module not found) unless an ambient `declare module` or `@ts-expect-error` is added; a `.js` file is not type-checked (`checkJs` is off). The stream suffix works on Windows only; on Linux the path does not exist and the build fails. Whether Turbopack resolves any of these in a Next.js bundle was not tested; a lint pass counts as the finding, as for SEC-081 and SEC-084. Attacker is a contributor. Low.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec081-recheck-4.test.ts --project security`, describe "SEC-086": 11 `it.fails`, all expected fail today.
Remediation: add to `NOT_NORMAL` a `%` anywhere, a `:` anywhere after the start (keep `^node:` for built-ins, which `no-restricted-imports` and `moduleLoaderPattern` already handle), and any URL scheme at the start (`^[a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*:` other than `node:`, so `data:`, `http:`, `https:`, `blob:` are refused as `file:` is). The real tree has no such specifier under `src` (real-tree case of the test file). Add the 11 forms to `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs` and flip the `it.fails`.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-087 Code under src can leave src for the repository root or a new top-level folder, which may import scripts and tests
Severity: Low
Status: OPEN
Owner: orchestrator (`eslint.config.mjs`); devops (`scripts/check-import-guards.mjs`)
Location: eslint.config.mjs:170-173 (`outsideSrcPattern` lists only `scripts`, `tests`, `e2e`, `prisma`); :220-232 (files outside `src` and outside the allow-list are refused `src/server/db` but not `scripts/` or `tests/`)
Description: found in the fourth SEC-081 re-check (commit 84b93f6). SEC-083 stops a page importing `scripts/`, `tests/`, `e2e/`, or `prisma/` directly. It does not stop a page importing any other file outside `src`, and nothing stops such a file importing `scripts/` or `tests/`, which may import the db layer. `shared/leak.ts` with `export * from "../tests/helpers/reset"` (which imports `db`) and `src/app/x/probe.ts` with `import { x } from "../../../shared/leak"` both lint clean; so do a root `leak.ts` with `export * from "./scripts/bootstrap-admin"` and `import { x } from "../../../leak"`. The page then holds the database client in two hops. This is not "a re-export through an allowed folder" (D-045): the hop is in a folder that is not on the allow-list. In the real tree the root `playwright.config.ts` imports `e2e/support`, which imports `tests/helpers/test-database-url.ts` (no db import), so the same route exists today one helper away from the db layer. Attacker is a contributor. Low.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec081-recheck-4.test.ts --project security`, describe "SEC-087": 2 `it.fails`, each asserting that at least one of the two hops is an ESLint error; both expected fail today.
Remediation: refuse in hardened `src` files any relative specifier that resolves outside `src` (the db layer reaching `prisma/generated` excepted). A text pattern cannot know the depth of the importing file; `import/no-restricted-paths` from `eslint-plugin-import` (installed with `eslint-config-next`) resolves the path, for example a zone with `target: "./src"`, `from: "./"`, `except: ["./src", "./node_modules"]`, and a separate zone allowing `./src/server/db` to reach `./prisma/generated`. Alternatively, or as well, refuse imports of `scripts/` and `tests/` from every file that is not itself in `scripts/`, `tests/`, `e2e/`, or `prisma/seed.ts`. Add both hops of each spelling to `scripts/check-import-guards.mjs` and flip the `it.fails`. The module-graph check proposed under SEC-081 would also cover this.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-088 A deactivated admin's request that passed the guard before the deactivation still completes its write
Severity: Medium
Status: OPEN (needs an owner answer on D-024 semantics, TEST_STRATEGY open item T12)
Owner: backend-api (`src/modules/admin/repository.ts`); orchestrator for the decision
Location: src/server/auth/session.ts:64-76 (`loadActor` reads `isActive` once, when the request starts); src/modules/admin/service.ts:79-82 (`adminId` checks only the role of that value); src/modules/admin/repository.ts:320-369 (`deactivateUser`), :376-398, :546-579, :753-806, :846-881, :900-949, :958-995, :1166-1221, :1253-1303 (no write transaction checks that the acting admin is still active)
Description: found in the T-050 review (HEAD f57d929). The actor is a value read once per request. Every admin write then runs in its own transaction with `adminId` from that value and never reads the acting admin's row again. A request of admin B that passed `requireActor` before admin A's deactivation of B commits (for example one waiting on the admin row locks that A's deactivation holds) still completes after it: B, already deactivated and with every session deleted, deactivates admin C, cancels a seller order, changes the commission or shipping fee, suspends a store, or takes a product down, and the audit row names B as actor. With two admins the "last admin" lock check stops the worst case (two admins deactivating each other: exactly one wins, checked 5 rounds by the itest); with three or more admins, a rogue admin who is being removed can still remove one more admin, or make one more money or moderation change, in that window. Every such action is audited, and the window is the time between the guard and the transaction (milliseconds, longer when queued on a lock). D-024 does not say whether such a request is honoured (TEST_STRATEGY T12). Medium: hard to exploit, real integrity effect, fully audited.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.integration.config.mts tests/security/t050-admin.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-088": 2 `it.fails`. Each takes the actor `loadActor` returns for admin B while B is active, deactivates B through `POST /api/v1/admin/users/{id}/deactivate` as admin A (200, B's sessions gone), then calls `deactivateUser(staleB, C)` and `updateSettings(staleB, { commissionRateBps: 9900 })`. Flipped to `it` on 2026-10-23 (then restored) both failed with `AssertionError: expected 'done' to be an instance of AppError`: C was deactivated and the commission changed.
Remediation: inside every admin write transaction, before the change, lock and read the acting admin's row (`SELECT "isActive", "role" FROM "User" WHERE "id" = ${adminId} FOR SHARE`) and refuse with 401 UNAUTHENTICATED (or 403) unless it is still an active `ADMIN`. In `deactivateUser` for an admin target, the existing `FOR UPDATE` over every active admin can serve: refuse when `adminId` is not among the locked rows. One helper (`assertActingAdmin(tx, adminId)`) called first in each of the nine write transactions keeps it in one place. The sellers module's `approveApplicationRow` and `rejectApplicationRow` need the same check. Flip the two `it.fails`. If the owner decides that in-flight requests are honoured, record that in D-024 and mark this ACCEPTED.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-089 The installed Next.js 16.4.0 has two published Critical remote code execution advisories and one High denial-of-service advisory
Severity: High
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-26, commit f9d916f, tree efbdb9f; see [reviews/SEC-089-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-089-recheck.md))
Owner: orchestrator or devops (`package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`)
Location: package.json:28 (`"next": "16.4.0"`)
Description: found by `pnpm audit --prod` during the T-050 review (2026-10-23). Advisories against `next >=16.0.0 <=16.4.0`, patched in 16.4.1: GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw (critical, "Unauthenticated Remote Code Execution in Image Optimization API when SVG files are used"), GHSA-673q-mjm6-r5xr (critical, "Unauthenticated Remote Code Execution in `use cache` functions returning typed arrays"), GHSA-p3m5-59mp-jv5r (high, "Denial of Service in App Router using Server Actions"). Advisory texts were not fetched (no third-party access from this review); only the titles and version ranges from the audit output are known. What was checked locally: `src` has no `"use cache"` and no `"use server"`; both `next/image` uses pass `unoptimized`; `public/` holds no file; uploads refuse SVG (T-041); `next.config.ts` sets no `images` option, so the optimisation endpoint keeps its defaults and is still served. So the two RCE preconditions named in the titles were not found in this code, but that is not proof the endpoints are unreachable (`cacheComponents: true` is on, and the framework's own code paths were not read), and the DoS title names a feature every App Router server has. High rather than Critical because no reachable path was shown; it must not be lowered further without reading the advisories.
Reproduction: `pnpm audit --prod` (pnpm 12.11.2): "3 vulnerabilities found, Severity: 1 high | 2 critical"; `pnpm audit --prod --json` lists the three ids above.
Remediation: upgrade `next` (and `eslint-config-next` to the same version) to 16.4.1 or later, `pnpm install`, record the version in DECISIONS.md, run `pnpm verify` and the e2e suite. Until then, do not deploy. Read the three advisories when upgrading and note any configuration they require.
Re-check evidence: none yet. Fix evidence (2026-10-26, orchestrator): `next` and `eslint-config-next` 16.4.3 (latest), `zod` ^4.6.7 (a new High advisory GHSA-mrhx-c7xc-m3w5, zod <4.6.6, appeared in the same audit); `pnpm audit --prod` -> "No known vulnerabilities found"; `pnpm verify` -> 207 files, 5688 passed, 32 expected fail, build compiled. Advisory texts still not read; e2e not run. Security re-check (2026-10-26, tree efbdb9f): `pnpm audit --prod` (pnpm 12.11.2) -> "No known vulnerabilities found", exit 0; `pnpm audit --json` lists no advisory in `next`, `zod`, or `eslint-config-next` (8 development-only advisories in the Prisma CLI and `jsdom`, see SEC-010); installed `node_modules/next/package.json` 16.4.3, `eslint-config-next` 16.4.3, `zod` 4.6.7, `pnpm why zod --prod` "Found 1 version of zod". `git show --stat f9d916f`: 5 files, no `src` change; the `pnpm-lock.yaml` diff changes only `next`, `@next/env`, `@next/eslint-plugin-next`, the eight `@next/swc-*` binaries, `eslint-config-next`, `zod`, and peer-resolution keys embedding those versions (`better-auth`, `@better-auth/*`, `better-call`, `zod-validation-error` keep their versions); no package added or removed, no override changed. Configuration against the advisory titles, repeated: no `"use cache"` and no `"use server"` in `src`; `next.config.ts` sets no `images` option; both `next/image` uses pass `unoptimized`; no `public/` directory. Limits: the three advisory texts were not read (no web-fetch tool in this session), so any configuration they require is unknown and the fix rests on the version range (16.4.3 > 16.4.1); e2e, `pnpm build`, and `pnpm verify` were not run by security (the orchestrator runs e2e on the same `.next`).

### SEC-090 The admin product review page cannot show the pictures it asks the admin to approve
Severity: High
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-23, commit 027edfa, rule BR-41 and REQ-A-05 acceptance (a) to (g); see [reviews/SEC-090-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-090-recheck.md))
Owner: backend-api (`src/modules/uploads/service.ts` `mayServe`, API_CONTRACTS 5.8) with product-requirements for the rule; frontend (`src/components/admin/product-section.tsx`, `products-table.tsx`) for what the page says while pictures cannot be shown
Location: src/modules/uploads/service.ts:167-170 (`mayServe`: a non-public file only to `role === "SELLER"` of the owning store); src/components/admin/product-section.tsx:160-172 (renders `ProductImage` with `image.url` for every picture, line 168); src/components/admin/products-table.tsx:45-50 (the queue's first picture)
Description: found in the T-051 review (HEAD ec243f5). BR-17 makes approval mandatory and BR-18 and BR-19 send a product back to `PENDING` when a picture is added, removed, or replaced, so pictures are moderated content. The T-051 review page lists the product's pictures as `<img src="/api/files/{key}">`, but since SEC-068 item 1 a picture of a product that is not visible is served only to a `SELLER` session of the owning store; an admin session gets the unknown-key 404. The admin therefore sees an empty, broken picture box (`alt=""`, no text says the pictures could not be loaded) and decides on the title and description alone; on approval the pictures become public at once. What an attacker with an approved seller account gains: any JPEG, PNG, or WebP picture (prohibited goods, a bank-transfer QR code, contact details, offensive content) published on the marketplace under an admin approval that never saw it, either on a new product or by replacing a picture of an approved product with unchanged text. No other tenant's data is exposed and every approval is audited, so not Critical; High because the moderation control of BR-17 does not work for pictures and any approved seller can exploit it without timing or special access.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run --config vitest.integration.config.mts tests/security/t051-admin-ui.itest.ts`, describe "SEC-090": the documented case shows that `getProduct` (admin) returns `images[0].url = /api/files/{key}` for a queued product, the owner reads it (200), the admin gets 404; the `it.fails` asserts the admin's session reads every picture the review page shows. Flipped to `it` in a temporary copy (deleted) on 2026-10-23 it failed with `AssertionError: expected 404 to be 200`.
Remediation: decide the rule, then build it: serve a non-public product picture also to an active `ADMIN` session (at least for products whose `moderationStatus` is `PENDING`, and arguably for every product an admin can open, since takedown review needs them too), keeping `no-store`, `nosniff`, and the sandbox CSP and keeping the 404 for every other caller; update API_CONTRACTS 5.8 "Who may read" and the SEC-068 re-check, whose `expectOwnerOnly` lists the admin among the refused callers and will need the admin moved out of that list for the pictures concerned (the security agent re-checks that change). Until then the review page must not offer approve on a product with pictures it cannot show, or must say plainly that the pictures could not be loaded. Flip the `it.fails`.
Re-check evidence (2026-10-23, tree 263d89b, fix 027edfa and docs 10c0cd9): read `mayServe` and `openFile` (`src/modules/uploads/service.ts`), `fileLookupStatement` (`src/modules/uploads/repository.ts`), the route, `getActor`/`loadActor`, and `resolveOwnUploads`. `productPicture` is `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "ProductImage" pim WHERE pim."uploadId" = f."id")`, evaluated per request with no condition on the product, so it follows BR-41 (1) and becomes false the moment the row goes (seller replace, row delete, product delete by cascade). `mayServe` answers a `SELLER` by store only, an `ADMIN` by `isProductPicture` only (an admin carrying a store id gains nothing), every other role false; the actor is loaded from the user row on each request, so a deactivated admin (null actor) and an admin demoted to `CUSTOMER` with a live session row both get the 404. The expired-orphan check runs before `mayServe`, and a ProductImage row makes an upload not an orphan, so the two cannot disagree. Headers are unchanged (`no-store`, `nosniff`, sandbox CSP). The fix's own tests were read: unit cases in `service.test.ts`, REQ-A-05 (a) to (g) in `tests/integration/uploads/endpoints.test.ts` describe "BR-41", the changed `sec068-recheck.itest.ts` (see SEC-068), and `t051-admin-ui.itest.ts`, whose `it.fails` became `it` and whose first case, which documented the defect, now asserts admin 200 instead of 404. That edit was made by backend-api in a security-owned file; it is accepted because the case had documented the defective state and the new assertion is the BR-41 behaviour, with the no-session 404 kept; security renamed the case and the describe and updated the file header. New `tests/security/sec090-recheck.itest.ts` (10 cases, written from BR-41, not from the author's cases): demoted admin, signed-out admin, forged admin cookie; ProductImage row deleted, product deleted; an upload that is both a suspended store's logo and a product picture (documented: admin 200 by BR-41 (1), 404 again once detached); admin 200 identical to the owner's 200 in status, every header but `X-Request-Id`, and bytes; admin refusal of an unattached upload identical to the unknown-key 404 in status, every header, and body; seven variants of a readable key (upper case, `.jpg` suffix, trailing space, `./` prefix, `%2e`, one character short, one long) identical 404; three admin reads add no audit row and change no session row. Commands: `pnpm test:integration tests/security/sec090-recheck.itest.ts tests/security/sec068-recheck.itest.ts tests/security/t051-admin-ui.itest.ts tests/integration/uploads/endpoints.test.ts` -> 4 files, 87 passed, 1 expected fail (SEC-071); `pnpm test:integration tests/security/t041-uploads.itest.ts tests/security/sec065-recheck.itest.ts tests/security/t042-inventory.itest.ts tests/security/sec090-recheck.itest.ts tests/security/sec068-recheck.itest.ts tests/security/t051-admin-ui.itest.ts` -> 6 files, 71 passed, 3 expected fail (SEC-064, SEC-066, SEC-071); `pnpm exec vitest run src/modules/uploads/service.test.ts tests/security/t041-uploads.test.ts` -> 64 passed. Negative control in a scratch copy (deleted): with `findUploadByKey` mocked to report every upload as a product picture, 4 of the 10 new cases fail (both detach cases, the logo case, the unattached refusal). Limits: in-process route calls, not `next start`; no browser check of the review page's `<img>`; a broken picture (missing bytes) still shows an empty box with no message on the review page (frontend, not a security control).

### SEC-091 The global CSP from next.config.ts replaces the sandbox policy of uploaded files
Severity: Medium
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-26)
Owner: orchestrator (`next.config.ts`, lent to devops for T-072); security re-checks
Location: next.config.ts:52-63 (`headers()`, one rule `source: "/:path*"` with the site CSP); src/app/api/files/[key]/route.ts:38 (`"Content-Security-Policy": "default-src 'none'; sandbox"`); node_modules/next/dist/server/send-response.js (a route handler's header is appended only when the outgoing response does not already carry it)
Description: found in the T-072 review. Next.js 16.4.3 sets the `next.config.ts` headers on the response before the route handler runs and then copies the handler's headers only if the same name is not already present. Since T-072, every response carries the site CSP (`default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; ...`), so the file route's `default-src 'none'; sandbox` is dropped and a stored upload opened as a document runs under the site policy, with inline script allowed and no sandbox. The upload controls of TM-17 still hold (magic bytes, image types only, fixed `Content-Type`, `nosniff`), so no script runs from an upload today; what is lost is the second layer that T-041 relied on if any of those ever fails (a polyglot, a type mapping change, a browser sniffing bug). Same-origin uploads are the classic place for that layer. Medium: a defence-in-depth regression with no working exploit shown.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/t072-headers-review.test.ts`, describe "SEC-091": the `it.fails` computes the config headers for `/api/files/{key}` with Next.js's own path matcher and asserts the CSP is the sandbox policy (or absent, leaving the route's). Flipped in a temporary copy (deleted, 2026-10-26) it fails with `expected 'default-src \'self\'; script-src \'se…' to match /default-src 'none'/`. Behaviour of the framework shown with a two-file probe app on this repository's Next.js 16.4.3 (`next build --webpack`, `next start`): config CSP `GLOBAL-CONFIG-POLICY`, route CSP `default-src 'none'; sandbox`; `curl -D -` of the route showed only `Content-Security-Policy: GLOBAL-CONFIG-POLICY` (details in [reviews/T-072-security.md](reviews/T-072-security.md)).
Remediation: add a second, later rule in `headers()` for `source: "/api/files/:key"` that sets `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox` (the last matching rule wins for one key), or exclude `/api/files/` from the site CSP rule and leave the header to the route. Keep `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` on both. Flip the `it.fails`, and check once under `next start` that a real file response carries the sandbox policy.
Re-check evidence (2026-10-26, fix 01c6bf2, [reviews/SEC-091-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-091-recheck.md)): `next.config.ts:61-66` adds a second rule after `/:path*`: `source: "/api/files/:path*"`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`. I read the precedence in Next.js 16.4.3. The headers documentation says "the last header key will override the first". `load-custom-routes.js` keeps the order of the rules. `resolve-routes.js` merges them with `resHeaders[key] = value` in rule order, with no case folding, and both rules use the same spelling. `send-response.js` then drops the route's own header of the same name. The flipped case in `t072-headers-review.test.ts` gets the same answer, but it uses a simplified model: `getPathMatch` without the router's options, no `loadCustomRoutes`, key case folded, and an `if` that would also pass if no config CSP were sent. New `tests/security/sec091-recheck.test.ts` (6 cases) uses the framework's `loadCustomRoutes` and `buildCustomRoute("header")` with the `resolve-routes` merge. It shows the following. File key paths and case variants get exactly the sandbox policy, plus `nosniff` and `X-Frame-Options: DENY`. `/api/files` also gets the sandbox; no route exists there. Other paths keep the site CSP. The sandbox rule comes after the site rule, and with the two rules swapped the site CSP wins. The route still sets the same value. Negative control: run against `01c6bf2~1:next.config.ts` in temporary copies (deleted), 4 of the 6 cases failed (`expected 'default-src \'self\'; script-src \'se…' to be 'default-src \'none\'; sandbox'`). Probe app (scratchpad, this repository's Next.js 16.4.3, `next dev --webpack -p 3318`, the same two rules, the route returning `ROUTE-POLICY`): `curl -D -` of `/api/files/<key>` returned the sandbox CSP. So did the variants with a query string, `%2E`, `%61`, `%2F`, a backslash, and upper case. `/api/%66iles/...` got the site policy, but it is a 404 that does not reach the route. Trailing-slash and double-slash forms are 308 redirects. With the two rules swapped, the probe returned the site policy. Commands: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/security/sec091-recheck.test.ts tests/security/t072-headers-review.test.ts tests/security/headers-t072.test.ts` gave 3 files, 19 passed. ESLint, Prettier, and `tsc` reported nothing for the new file. Not verified: the project under `next build` + `next start` (I did not run `pnpm build`, as instructed, and did not inspect the production `routes-manifest.json`); a real stored upload through the project's server; a proxy or CDN in front of the container; a browser. Production uses the same `resolve-routes.js` merge, but the original finding was reproduced under `next start` and this fix was not.

### SEC-092 Review text is published at once and nothing can hide or remove it
Severity: Medium
Status: OPEN
Owner: owner and product-requirements (whether reviews are moderated and how); backend-api (an admin hide or delete with an audit row); frontend (admin page)
Location: src/modules/reviews/service.ts:40-55 (`createReview` inserts and the review is public at once); src/modules/catalog (public list shows every review of a visible product); no `review.update` or `review.delete` anywhere in `src` (`git grep -nE "review\.(update|delete|deleteMany|updateMany)" -- src` is empty); REQUIREMENTS REQ-C-14 has no moderation item, REQ-A-* have none for reviews
Description: found in the T-035 review. Products, pictures, and stores pass admin moderation (BR-17 to BR-19, BR-41), but a review comment of up to 2000 characters goes on the product page with no check and no way back: no admin action, no seller reply, no report button, no edit or delete by the author. A buyer of a delivered item can post scam contact lines ("pay by transfer to ..., LINE id ...", phone numbers, URLs as text), abuse, or personal data about others on any product it bought. With the mock provider and SEC-072, delivery to a second account of one's own costs nothing, so a seller can also target its own listings or a competitor's product it bought. What an attacker gains: a permanent, trusted-looking place for fraud lines and defamation on the marketplace; the only removal today is a database operator or taking the whole product down. No data of another user is exposed and the text is escaped, so not High.
Reproduction: as any customer with a `DELIVERED` order item: `POST /api/v1/orders/{id}/items/{itemId}/review` with `{"rating":1,"comment":"Pay by transfer to 000-0-00000-0 for a discount"}` answers 201, and `GET /api/v1/products/{slug}/reviews` lists the text at once. The author's integration case "returns markup-like text exactly as stored, in the answer and in the public list" (`tests/integration/reviews/endpoints.test.ts`) shows the same path. No endpoint exists to undo it.
Remediation: owner to choose: (1) an admin action that hides a review (a `hiddenAt` or status column, excluded from the public list and the rating summary, with an audit row); (2) optionally a report button for customers and sellers; (3) optionally hold reviews with a comment until approved, or until the buyer account is older than N days (also limits SEC-072). (1) is the minimum before launch.
Re-check evidence: none yet.

### SEC-093 The second production refusal of SIGN_UP_RATE_LIMIT reads the runtime NODE_ENV, not the build-time one
Severity: Low
Status: FIXED (re-checked 2026-10-27, session 119; re-checked again independently 2026-10-27, session 120, [reviews/SEC-093-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-093-recheck.md); fix 039613d: `getAuthRuntime` no longer passes `env.nodeEnv`, so `signUpRateLimiter` reads its own default `process.env.NODE_ENV`, inlined as "production" in a build; the `it.fails` in `tests/security/s116-sign-up-limit.test.ts` is now a passing test)
Owner: auth (`src/server/auth/routes.ts`, `getAuthRuntime`); devops (author of 949285f) may take it; owner decides the wider start-up check in the remediation
Location: src/server/auth/routes.ts:94 (`signUpRateLimiter(env.signUpRateLimit, env.nodeEnv)`); src/server/rate-limit.ts:564-571 (`nodeEnv` parameter, default `process.env.NODE_ENV`); src/server/env.ts:576-583 (production refusal keyed on the runtime `NODE_ENV`)
Description: found in the session 116 review of 949285f. The commit message and DEPLOYMENT.md say `signUpRateLimiter` "refuses a non-default limit in production a second time". Both refusals read the same value: `parseEnv(process.env)` reads `source["NODE_ENV"]` at run time, and `getAuthRuntime` passes that `env.nodeEnv` to `signUpRateLimiter`, so the function's own default (`process.env.NODE_ENV`, which Next.js inlines as `"production"` in a `next build` output) is never used. A production build started with a runtime `NODE_ENV` of `test` or `development` (a `docker run -e NODE_ENV=test`, a platform default, a copied `.env`) plus `SIGN_UP_RATE_LIMIT=1000` therefore accepts the override at both layers: one address can create 1000 accounts an hour instead of 5. Compare `getPaymentProvider` (SEC-056), which keeps the inlined default and is refused in that same build. Low: it needs an operator to set a wrong `NODE_ENV` and the variable, and that wrong `NODE_ENV` already drops the other production checks of env.ts (SEC-009, SEC-044, `Secure` cookie), a residual listed below; the second layer simply does not add the independence it claims.
Reproduction: `pnpm exec vitest run --project security tests/security/s116-sign-up-limit.test.ts`, describe "SEC-093": the `it.fails` stubs `process.env.NODE_ENV=production` (what the inlined default sees in a build) while `getEnv` returns `nodeEnv: "test", signUpRateLimit: 1000`, and expects `getAuthRuntime()` to throw; it does not. The control case shows `signUpRule.limit` is 1000. Build evidence that the default is inlined and `parseEnv` is not: `.next/server/chunks/src_modules_1hc4la3idtpbg._.js` (build of 2026-10-26) holds `function(t="production"){if("production"===t)throw new i;...}` for `getPaymentProvider`, and another chunk holds `function(A){let e=[],t=d(A,"NODE_ENV",e),...` for `parseEnv`. That build predates 949285f; the project was not rebuilt (shared `.next`, e2e may run in parallel).
Remediation: in `getAuthRuntime`, refuse when either value says production, for example `signUpRateLimiter(env.signUpRateLimit, process.env.NODE_ENV === "production" ? "production" : env.nodeEnv)`, or drop the second argument so the inlined default applies. Then flip the `it.fails`. Wider, for the owner: refuse to start (in `loadEnvAtStartup`) when the build-time `process.env.NODE_ENV` is `production` and the runtime `NODE_ENV` is not; that closes the whole class (env.ts production checks, `Secure` cookie) instead of one variable.
Re-check evidence (2026-10-27, session 119, tree 690d812; also closes CR-S116-14, see [reviews/S116-security.md](reviews/S116-security.md#cr-s116-14-and-sec-093-re-check-session-119-2026-10-27)): (1) `$env:NEXT_DIST_DIR = ".next-sec119"; pnpm exec next build` -> exit 0; the compiled chunk holds `function(e,i="production"){if(e===o.SIGN_UP.limit)return{limiter:f,rule:o.SIGN_UP};if("production"===i)throw TypeError("The sign-up rate limit cannot be changed when NODE_ENV=produ...` and `getAuthRuntime` compiles to `t=(0,p.A)(e.signUpRateLimit)` (one argument), so a production build refuses the override whatever the runtime `NODE_ENV` is (the dir was deleted afterwards). (2) `pnpm exec vitest run --project security tests/security/cr-s116-14-sign-up-limit-node-env.test.ts tests/security/s116-sign-up-limit.test.ts` -> 2 files, 31 passed: runtime `NODE_ENV` `test` and `development` with the build-time value `production` both throw; static cases pin the one-argument call, the default parameter, Next.js `define-env.js:76` (inlined from the `dev` flag), no `allowDevelopmentBuild` in `next.config.ts`, and the Dockerfile's `NODE_ENV=production` with `next start`. The throw happens inside the handler's `try`, so the auth routes answer 500 (fail closed); `next start` itself was not run. The wider start-up check (build-time vs runtime `NODE_ENV`) is still the owner's choice and stays in Residual risks.
Re-check evidence (2026-10-27, session 120, independent, tree 8cafc0a; [reviews/SEC-093-recheck.md](reviews/SEC-093-recheck.md)): (1) `$env:NEXT_DIST_DIR = ".next-sec120"; pnpm exec next build` -> exit 0; two server chunks hold `function(e,i="production"){if(e===o.SIGN_UP.limit)return{limiter:f,rule:o.SIGN_UP};if("production"===i)throw TypeError("The sign-up rate limit cannot be changed when NODE_ENV=production.")` and `getAuthRuntime` compiles to `let e=(0,d.y)(),t=(0,p.A)(e.signUpRateLimit);` (one argument); the dir was deleted and the build's rewrite of `tsconfig.json` restored. (2) New `tests/security/s120-sec-093-recheck.test.ts`: through the real handlers, a build-time `production` with runtime `NODE_ENV` `test` or `development` and limit 1000 makes sign-up answer 500 with no `Set-Cookie`, and sign-in, sign-out, and session answer 500 too (fail closed); the default limit 5 in the same build gets the shared limiter; statically, `signUpRateLimiter` has exactly one call site in `src` (`signUpRateLimiter(env.signUpRateLimit)`), `getEnv` parses `process.env` only, the sign-up rule comes only from the runtime, the four auth routes pass `getAuthRuntime` itself, and `signUpBodySchema` is strict. No request header, query, or body reaches the limit. `pnpm exec vitest run --project security tests/security/s120-sec-093-recheck.test.ts tests/security/cr-s116-14-sign-up-limit-node-env.test.ts tests/security/s116-sign-up-limit.test.ts` -> 3 files, 43 passed; `pnpm exec vitest run tests/unit/e2e/server-environment.test.ts` -> 6 passed (override only in `webServer.env`, with `NODE_ENV: "development"`). Not run: `next start` against the build, and a mutation run of the new file against the pre-fix `routes.ts` (src is not the reviewer's to edit).
